M_{H}
-equivariant map
S_{-j}\subset C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2n-1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
described above by sending the lowest weight vector
v^{\operatorname{can}}
to the function

(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\mapsto a_{n+1}^{j_{\tau_{0}}}

and we denote the resulting embedding by
\Phi\colon S_{-j}\to C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2n-1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. In particular, if
f\in S_{-j}
is an algebraic function and
\Phi(f)\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2n-1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes its image under this embedding, then we have

(m^{-1}\cdot\Phi(f))((0,\dots,0,1,0,\dots,0))=f(m),\quad\quad m\in M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

where
1
is in the
n
-th place (i.e.
a_{n+1}=1
).

We make the following definition:

Definition 2.2.9
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. We let
\delta_{\kappa,j}\in V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2n-1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the image of
v_{\kappa}^{[j]}
under the map

1\times\Phi\colon V_{\kappa}\otimes S_{-j}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2n-1},\mathbb{Q}_{p}).

Note that
\delta_{\kappa,j}
is an eigenvector for the diagonal action of
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with eigencharacter
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}
.

We now discuss the
p
-adic analogue of these polynomial functions. Recall that
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
denotes the depth
p^{\beta}
upper-triangular Iwahori subgroup in
M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Definition 2.2.10
.

We let
U_{G,\beta}=p^{-\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus n-1}\subset\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1}
, and we let

U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus n-1}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\oplus(p\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus n-1}\subset U_{G,\beta}.

Note that both
U_{G,\beta}
and
U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}
are stable under the conjugation action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
.

We have the following important lemma, which allows us to
p
-adically interpolate the polynomial functions above.

Lemma 2.2.11
.

If we view
\delta_{\kappa,j}
as a polynomial function on
M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1}
satisfying the transformation property
\delta_{\kappa,j}(mb,a)=(w_{M_{G}}^{\operatorname{max}}\kappa)(b^{-1})\delta_{\kappa,j}(m,a)
for all
b\in B_{M_{G}}
, then

[(u^{-1},u^{-1})\cdot\delta_{\kappa,j}](M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\times U_{G,\beta}^{\circ})\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}.

Proof.

Let
b=(a_{2},\dots,a_{n},a_{n+1},a_{n+2},\dots,a_{2n})\in U^{\circ}_{G,\beta}
(so
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
a_{n+1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and
a_{n+2},\dots,a_{2n}\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
). Consider the element
z\in\overline{Q}_{M_{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is given by

1\times 1\times\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{n+1}&\\
c&1\end{array}\right)

where
\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{n+1}&\\
c&1\end{array}\right)
is the
n\times n
-matrix block matrix with
(1\times 1)
upper left block and
(n-1\times n-1)
-lower block, with
c=(a_{n}+a_{n+2},a_{n-1}+a_{n+3},\dots,a_{2}+a_{2n})^{t}
.

By Convention
2.2.8
and the definition of
x_{\kappa}^{[j]}
, we see that for all
i\in M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})

\displaystyle[(u^{-1},u^{-1})\cdot\delta_{\kappa,j}](i,b)

\displaystyle=(1\times\Phi)(x_{\kappa}^{[j]})(i,(a_{2},\dots,a_{n+1},a_{n}+a_{n+2},\dots,a_{2}+a_{2n}))

\displaystyle=(1\times\Phi)(x_{\kappa}^{[j]})(i,(0,\dots,0,a_{n+1},a_{n}+a_{n+2},\dots,a_{2}+a_{2n}))

\displaystyle=(1\times z^{-1})\cdot(1\times\Phi)(x_{\kappa}^{[j]})(i,(0,\dots,0,1,0,\dots,0))

\displaystyle=x_{\kappa}^{[j]}(i,z).

This is an element of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
by Corollary
2.2.5
.
∎

We make the following definition:

Definition 2.2.12
.

For
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
, let
\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\in V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the restriction of
(u^{-1},u^{-1})\cdot\delta_{\kappa,j}
to
M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times U_{G,\beta}
.

Let
s\geq 1
be an integer and
(R,R^{+})
a complete Tate affinoid algebra over
(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. For any
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
, we let
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\colon M_{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
denote the corresponding
s
-analytic character defined by the same formula as in (
2.2.3
). Let
C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},R)
denote the
R
-algebra of
s
-analytic functions
U_{G,\beta}\to R
, which comes equipped with an action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
via the same formula as in (
2.2.6
). We can
p
-adically interpolate the vectors in Definition
2.2.12
by following the same strategy in
[
Gra21
, Appendix A]
. More precisely, let
V^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa}
denote the
s
-analytic induction

V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}=\left\{f\colon M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}:\begin{array}[]{c}f\text{ is }s\text{-analytic }\\
f(-\cdot b)=(w_{M_{G}}^{\operatorname{max}}\kappa)(b^{-1})f(-)\text{ for all }b\in B_{M_{G}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\end{array}\right\}

which carries an action of
m\in M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
via the formula
m\cdot f(-)=f(m^{-1}\cdot-)
.

Definition 2.2.13
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
and write

\displaystyle(\kappa,j)=a_{0}\circ(\mu_{0},0)+a_{w}\circ(\mu_{w},0)

\displaystyle+a_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\circ(\mu_{n+1,\tau_{0}},0)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\tau\in\Psi}a_{i,\tau}\circ(\mu_{i,\tau},0)+\sum_{\tau\in\Psi}b_{\tau}\circ(\mu_{n,\tau},1_{\tau})

for unique
s
-analytic characters
a_{0},a_{w},a_{i,\tau},b_{\tau}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to(R^{+})^{\times}
(see Remark
2.1.6
). Then we define
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\times U_{G,\beta}\to R
as the extension-by-zero of the function supported on
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\times U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}
given by

(\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{0},0,\beta})^{a_{0}}\cdot(\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{w},0,\beta})^{a_{w}}\cdot(\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n+1,\tau_{0}},0,\beta})^{a_{n+1,\tau_{0}}}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1,\dots,n\\
\tau\in\Psi\end{subarray}}(\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{i,\tau},0,\beta})^{a_{i,\tau}}\cdot\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}(\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau},1_{\tau},\beta})^{b_{\tau}}

which is well-defined by Lemma
2.2.11
. This function defines an element
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\in V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\hat{\otimes}C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},R)
, and note that
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\in V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
whenever
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
.

These vectors satisfy the following properties:

Proposition 2.2.14
.

We have the following:

(1)

The element
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
is an eigenvector for the action of
u^{-1}M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})u\subset M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
with eigencharacter
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}
.

(2)

The construction of
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
is compatible with changing the Tate affinoid algebra
(R,R^{+})
and the radius
s
of analyticity (in the obvious way).

(3)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite order character, for some finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
\beta\geq 1
be any integer such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. Let
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi}
denote the weighted indicator function given by

1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi}(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{\tau_{0}}(a_{n+1})&\text{ if }(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\in U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\end{array}\right.\quad\in C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},L)

with
s\geq 1
sufficiently large. Then
\delta_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}=1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi}\cdot\delta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\dagger}
.

(4)

Let
\beta^{\prime}\geq\beta
. Then the vector
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta^{\prime}}
is equal to the image of
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
under the map induced from restricting a function on
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\times U_{G,\beta}
to
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta^{\prime}})\times U_{G,\beta}
and then extending by zero to
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta^{\prime}})\times U_{G,\beta^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Properties (1), (2), and (4) are clear from the construction. For part (3), the claim is clear when
\chi_{\tau_{0}}
is trivial, so without loss of generality we may assume that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial for
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
. It is enough to show that
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{0,\chi,\beta}(i,-)\in C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
is equal to
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi}
for any
i\in\overline{N}^{1}_{M_{G},\beta}
. Clearly
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{0,\chi,\beta}(i,-)
is supported on
U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}
. Let
\underline{a}=(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\in U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}
. Then, by construction, we have

\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{0,\chi,\beta}(i,\underline{a})=\chi_{\tau_{0}}\left(\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}},1_{\tau_{0}},\beta}(i,\underline{a})\cdot\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}},0,\beta}(i,\underline{a})^{-1}\right)

where we view
\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}},0,\beta}
and
\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}},1_{\tau_{0}},\beta}
as functions on
M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times U_{G,\beta}
. Let
z(\underline{a})\in\overline{Q}_{M_{H}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the element which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is equal to the block matrix

1\times 1\times\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a_{n+1}&\\
c&1\end{array}\right)

with
c=(a_{n}+a_{n+2},a_{n-1}+a_{n+3},\dots,a_{2}+a_{2n})^{t}
. From the proof of Lemma
2.2.11
and Corollary
2.2.5
, we see that
\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}},0,\beta}(i,\underline{a})=x_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}}}^{[0]}(i,z(\underline{a}))\in 1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and

\delta^{\dagger}_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}},1_{\tau_{0}},\beta}(i,\underline{a})=x_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}}}^{[1_{\tau_{0}}]}(i,z(\underline{a}))\quad\in\quad a_{n+1}+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}

where the containment uses the transformation properties of the vector
x_{\mu_{n,\tau_{0}}}^{[1_{\tau_{0}}]}
under right-translation by
Q_{M_{H}}
. The result follows.
∎

Remark 2.2.15
.

If
j
is the trivial character on the
\tau_{0}
-component and
j^{\prime}\colon\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to(R^{+})^{\times}
is the character away from
\tau_{0}
, then
\delta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}=x_{\kappa}^{[j^{\prime}]}\otimes 1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}
where
x_{\kappa}^{[j^{\prime}]}\in V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}
is the vector constructed in
[
Gra21
, Theorem A.5.10]
(in the notation of
loc.cit.
).

2.3.
The main construction

We now describe the key construction that will be used in the definition of the “evaluation maps” (which, in turn, will give rise to the
p
-adic
L
-functions). This abstract construction will be applied in §
5.3.2
, §
7.4.2
and can be skipped on first reading. As in the previous subsection, we fix an integer
\beta\geq 1
which we will often omit from the notation. We also allow ourselves to work over a fixed finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

2.3.1.
Nearly holomorphic forms

Suppose that we have a topological
L
-module
\mathcal{N}_{G}
which comes equipped with a
L
-algebra action

(2.3.1)

C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}_{G}.

We assume that
\mathcal{N}_{G}
has a left action of
M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and the action map (
2.3.1
) is equivariant for the diagonal action of
M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Suppose that we have a topological
L
-module
\mathcal{N}_{H}
with a left action of
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and a continuous
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant map
p\colon\mathcal{N}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}_{H}
. These spaces mimic the properties of (the sheaves of) nearly holomorphic modular forms appearing in §
5.3.1
.

We consider the following construction.

Definition 2.3.2
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Set
\mathcal{N}_{G,\kappa^{*}}=\left(\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\right)^{M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
and
\mathcal{N}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}=\left(\mathcal{N}_{H}\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\right)^{M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}
. Then we define a
L
-linear map

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathcal{N}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}

induced from passing to
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-invariants of the map

(2.3.3)

\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathcal{N}_{H}

which is the composition of the following
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant maps:

•

The morphism
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}
induced from sending the first factor to
\delta_{\kappa,j}
.

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}
induced from the action map in (
2.3.1
).

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathcal{N}_{G}
induced from the natural map
V_{\kappa}\otimes V^{*}_{\kappa}\to L
.

•

The morphism
p\colon\mathcal{N}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}_{H}
.

2.3.2.
Nearly overconvergent forms of classical weight

We now suppose that we have an ind-system
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
of topological
L
-modules which are equipped with a left action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. We suppose that we have a
L
-algebra action

(2.3.4)

C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},L)\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}

equivariant for the diagonal action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
(i.e. (
2.3.4
) is a morphism in the ind-category of topological
L
-modules equipped with a continuous action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
). We also suppose that we have an ind-system
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
of topological
L
-modules which are equipped with a left action of
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, and a continuous
L
-linear morphism
p^{\dagger}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
which is equivariant for the action of
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, where
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
acts on
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
through the inclusion
u^{-1}M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})u\subset M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. These spaces mimic (the ind-sheaves of) nearly overconvergent forms that appear in §
6.4
. We will always view
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
as acting on
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
or any representation of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
through the inclusion
u^{-1}M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})u\subset M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
.

We consider the following construction:

Definition 2.3.5
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Set
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}=\left(\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\right)^{M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}=\left(\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\right)^{M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})}
. Then we define a
L
-linear map

\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}

induced by taking
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-invariants of the map

\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}

which is the composition of the following
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant maps:

•

The morphism
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},L)\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}
induced from sending the first factor to
\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
(this is indeed
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant by our conventions above and Proposition
2.2.14
).

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},L)\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}
induced from the action map in (
2.3.4
).

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
induced from the natural map
V_{\kappa}\otimes V^{*}_{\kappa}\to L
.

•

The morphism
p^{\dagger}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
.

The operators
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
and
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\dagger}
will be compatible in the following way. Suppose that we have a commutative diagram of continuous
L
-linear maps

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{N}_{H}^{\dagger}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p\circ u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\dagger}}

where the vertical maps are equivariant for the actions of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
and
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
respectively. In particular, the maps
\iota_{\bullet}
induce morphisms on isotypic pieces for the actions of these groups. We also assume that
\iota_{G}
intertwines the actions (
2.3.1
) and (
2.3.4
) via the natural restriction map
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)\to C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
.

Proposition 2.3.6
.

We have the following relation
\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\circ\iota_{G}=\iota_{H}\circ\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
.

Proof.

To prove this, we will show that we can reinterpret
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
using the map
p\circ u
instead of
p
. More precisely, by replacing the first bullet point in Definition
2.3.2
with the map induced from
(u^{-1},u^{-1})\cdot\delta_{\kappa,j}
and replacing the last bullet point in Definition
2.3.2
with
p\circ u
, we obtain an induced map

(2.3.7)

\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathcal{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathcal{N}_{H}

and one can verify that (
2.3.7
)
(a\otimes u^{-1}b\otimes u^{-1}c)=
(
2.3.3
)
(a\otimes b\otimes c)
. Thus
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
is induced from passing to
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-invariants of the map (
2.3.7
).
The proposition follows, because
\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
is just the restriction of
(u^{-1},u^{-1})\cdot\delta_{\kappa,j}
to
U_{G,\beta}
(and the map
\iota_{G}
is equivariant for the actions (
2.3.1
) and (
2.3.4
)).
∎

2.3.3.
Nearly overconvergent forms of
p
-adic weight

Continuing with the same notation and conventions as above, we now suppose that
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
is an ind-system of Fréchet spaces over
L
. We also suppose that (
2.3.4
) extends to a continuous
L
-algebra action

(2.3.8)

C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},L)\;\hat{\otimes}\;\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}

which is equivariant for the diagonal action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, for some integer
s\geq 1
(recall our conventions on functors between ind-categories in §
1.5
). For an
s
-analytic character
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to R^{\times}
, let
D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}
denote the continuous
R
-dual of

V^{\circ,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{f\colon M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\to R:\begin{array}[]{c}f\text{ is }(s+\varepsilon)\text{-analytic for all }\varepsilon>0\\
f(-\cdot b)=(w_{M_{G}}^{\operatorname{max}}\kappa)(b^{-1})f(-)\text{ for all }b\in B_{M_{G}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\end{array}\right\}.

We consider the following construction:

Definition 2.3.9
.

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a complete Tate affinoid algebra over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
, and let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
. Set
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}=\left(\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}\right)^{M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}=\left(\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}\hat{\otimes}\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\right)^{M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})}
. Then we define an
R
-linear map

\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}

induced by taking
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-invariants of the map

\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\;\hat{\otimes}\;\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\;\hat{\otimes}\;D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}

which is the composition of the following
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant maps:

•

The morphism
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}\to V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\hat{\otimes}C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},R)\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}
induced from sending the first factor to
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
.

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\hat{\otimes}C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},R)\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}\to V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}
induced from the action map in (
2.3.4
).

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
induced from the natural map
V_{\kappa}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\hat{\otimes}D^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{*}}\to R
.

•

The morphism
p^{\dagger}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
.

We will also need a version for locally algebraic characters.

Definition 2.3.10
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite-order character such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. Then we define an
L
-linear map

\vartheta_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}^{\dagger,\circ}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}=\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j+\chi]}}

by taking the
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-invariants of the map
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j+\chi],-1}\;\hat{\otimes}\;\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\;\hat{\otimes}\;V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
defined in exactly the same way as in Definition
2.3.9
(note that
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}\in V_{\kappa}\hat{\otimes}C^{s\operatorname{-an}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
by Proposition
2.2.14
(3)). If
\chi
is the trivial character, we will denote this morphism by
\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
.

As in the previous section, there exist relations between
\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
,
\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}
, and
\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
, and also compatibility as the Tate affinoid algebra
(R,R^{+})
varies.

Proposition 2.3.11
.

We have the following:

(1)

Let
(R,R^{+})\to(R^{\prime},(R^{\prime})^{+})
be a morphism of complete Tate affinoid algebras over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
, and
s^{\prime}\geq s\geq 1
integers. Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
and let
(\kappa^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathcal{X}_{R^{\prime},s^{\prime}}
denote its image under the natural map
\mathcal{X}_{R,s}\to\mathcal{X}_{R^{\prime},s^{\prime}}
. Then we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,s^{\prime}\operatorname{-an}}_{G,(\kappa^{\prime})^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,s^{\prime}\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{[j^{\prime}]}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,s^{\prime}\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{\prime},j^{\prime},\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,s^{\prime}\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}

where the unlabelled arrows are the natural ones.

(2)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite-order character such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. Let
s\geq 1
be a sufficiently large integer such that
\chi
is
s
-analytic. Then

\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}=\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}

via the natural maps
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}=\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
. If
\chi
is the trivial character, then

\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\beta}=\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\circ 1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}

where
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}\colon\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
denotes the action of the indicator function of
U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}
(which preserves the weight).

Proof.

The first part just follows from the fact that the vectors
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
are compatible with changing the Tate affinoid algebra and the radius of analyticity (see Proposition
2.2.14
). For the second part, this follows from the fact that
\delta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}=1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi}\cdot\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
.
∎

Remark 2.3.12
.

One should view the action of
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}
as a kind of “
p
-depletion” which is necessary for
p
-adically interpolating the maps
\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
. This will be made more precise in §
8.5
.

3.
Continuous operators on Banach spaces

In this section we establish the abstract results on locally analytic actions needed to
p
-adically interpolate the Gauss–Manin connection (see §
7.1
–
7.3
). This section is essentially an adaptation of
[
GPR23
, §4]
to the setting of this article.

3.1.
Function spaces

Let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, equipped with the
p
-adic norm
|\cdot|
such that
|p|=p^{-1}
. We let
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{L})
(resp.
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
) denote the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-algebra (resp.
L
-algebra) of continuous functions
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}
(resp.
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to L
). For an integer
k\geq 0
, consider the continuous function

\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{x}{k}=\frac{x(x-1)\cdots(x-k+1)}{k!}\in C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathcal{O}_{L}).

Then it is well-known that the collection
\{\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{x}{k}\}_{k\geq 0}
forms an orthonormal basis for
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
(see
[
Col10
, Corollaire I.2.4]
for example).

For an integer
h\geq 0
, let
C^{h\operatorname{-an}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\subset C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
denote the subspace of functions which are analytic on discs of radius
p^{-h}
, and let
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)=\varinjlim_{h\geq 0}C^{h\operatorname{-an}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
denote the space of locally analytic functions equipped with the direct limit topology. In terms of the orthonormal basis above,
C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
is the subspace of functions

(3.1.1)

f(x)=\sum_{k\geq 0}a_{k}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{x}{k}\quad\in\quad C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)

such that
p^{k\varepsilon}|a_{k}|\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
for some
\varepsilon>0
.

Definition 3.1.2
.

For
\varepsilon>0
, let
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\subset C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
denote the subspace of functions as in (
3.1.1
) satisfying the condition that
p^{k\varepsilon}|a_{k}|\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
. This is a
L
-Banach algebra with norm given by

|\!|f|\!|=\operatorname{sup}\{p^{k\varepsilon}|a_{k}|:k\geq 0\}.

We introduce the following terminology:

Definition 3.1.3
.

Let
W
be a topological
L
-vector space and
T\in\operatorname{End}_{L}(W)
a continuous operator. We say that
T
extends to a continuous (resp. locally analytic, resp.
\varepsilon
-analytic) action if there exists a continuous
L
-bilinear map

C\times W\to W,\quad C=C_{\operatorname{cont}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\text{ (resp. }C=C^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\text{, resp. }C=C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\text{ )}

such that

•

the induced map
C\to\operatorname{End}_{L}(W)
is a morphism of
L
-algebras

•

the structural map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow L
acts as
T
.

Here
C\times W
is equipped with the product topology.

3.2.
A pertubation lemma

We will need the following lemma which describes the convergence properties of two operators which are congruent modulo a power of
p
.

Lemma 3.2.1

(c.f.
[
GPR23
, Proposition 4.2.4]
)
.

Let
V
be an
L
-Banach space and
T_{1},T_{2}\in\operatorname{End}_{L}(V)
two continuous operators. Suppose that
T_{1}
extends to a locally analytic action and
|\!|T_{2}|\!|\leq 1
(i.e.
T_{2}
preserves the unit ball in
V
). Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists an integer
n(\varepsilon)\geq 1
(depending on
T_{1}
only) such that for all
n\geq n(\varepsilon)
, the operator
T_{1}+p^{n}T_{2}
extends to a
\varepsilon
-analytic action.

Proof.

Let
n\geq 0
and set
T=T_{1}+p^{n}T_{2}
. Let
f_{k}(X)
denote the polynomial

f_{k}(X)=\frac{X(X-1)\ldots(X-k+1)}{k!}

with the convention that
f_{0}=1
. Let
\Lambda
denote the set of all ordered tuples
I=(-1=t_{0}\leq t_{1}<t_{2}<\ldots<t_{2r-1}\leq t_{2r}=k-1)
with
r\geq 1
. For such an
I\in\Lambda
and any
1\leq i\leq r
, let
k_{i}=t_{2i-1}-t_{2i-2}
and
\ell_{i}=t_{2i}-t_{2i-1}
, and set

z_{I}=f_{k_{1}}(T_{1}-(t_{0}+1))\cdot T_{2}^{\ell_{1}}\cdot f_{k_{2}}(T_{1}-(t_{2}+1))\cdot T_{2}^{\ell_{2}}\cdot\ldots\cdot f_{k_{r}}(T_{1}-(t_{2r}+1))\cdot T_{2}^{\ell_{r}}.

Then we can write

(3.2.2)

f_{k}(T)=f_{k}(T_{1})+\sum_{I\in\Lambda,b\neq 0}\frac{p^{nb}}{b!}\binom{k}{a}^{-1}\binom{a}{k_{1},\ldots,k_{r}}^{-1}z_{I},

where we have denoted
a=\sum_{i=1}^{r}k_{i}
and
b=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\ell_{i}
so that
a+b=k
.

We now give a bound for the operators
z_{I}
. Note that for any integer
t\in\mathbb{Z}
,
f_{k}(X-t)
is also a polynomial in
X
, and the Mahler expansion of
f_{k}(X-t)
is of the form

f_{k}(X-t)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}a_{i}f_{i}(X)

for
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
because
f_{k}(x-t)\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for any
x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
(and the coefficients are just computed
using the discrete difference operator). Let
\varepsilon>0
and choose a constant
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that

p^{-k\varepsilon/2}|\!|f_{k}(T_{1})|\!|\leq C

for all
k\geq 0
(which exists because
T_{1}
extends to a locally analytic action). Then, for any
t\in\mathbb{Z}
and
k\geq 0
, we also have

p^{-k\varepsilon/2}|\!|f_{k}(T_{1}-t)|\!|\leq C.

Hence, by the fact that
|\!|T_{2}|\!|\leq 1
, we deduce

(3.2.3)

p^{-a\varepsilon/2}|\!|z_{I}|\!|\leq C^{r}.

Using that for any multinomial coefficient we have
v_{p}(\binom{n}{a_{1},\ldots,a_{s}}^{-1})\geq-\log_{p}(n)
, we find from (
3.2.2
) and (
3.2.3
) that

(3.2.4)

p^{-k\varepsilon}|\!|f_{k}(T)|\!|\leq\mathrm{max}(C,p^{-k\varepsilon}p^{-nb+b/(p-1)}p^{2\log_{p}(k)}p^{(k-b)\varepsilon/2}C^{r}).

Let
n(\varepsilon)\geq 1
be such that
p^{-n(\varepsilon)+1/(p-1)}\leq C^{-1}
. Since
b\geq r-1
we deduce that, for any
n\geq n(\varepsilon)
,
p^{-nb+b/(p-1)}C^{r}\leq C
and the right hand side of (
3.2.4
) is bounded above by

p^{-k\varepsilon/2+2\log_{p}(k)}C.

This latter quantity is uniformly bounded in
k
because
\varepsilon>0
, which concludes the proof.
∎

3.3.
Nilpotent operators

Let
S^{+}
be an admissible
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-algebra, and set
S=S^{+}[1/p]
. Let
|\!|\cdot|\!|
denote the Banach norm on
S
such that
S^{+}
is the unit ball. Consider the following two-variable Tate algebra
V^{+}=S^{+}\langle X,Y\rangle
and set
V=V^{+}[1/p]
. Equip
V
with the Banach norm such that
V^{+}
is the unit ball, i.e. equip
V
with the Banach norm

|\!|f|\!|=\operatorname{sup}\{|\!|s_{a,b}|\!|:a,b\geq 0\}

where
f(X,Y)=\sum_{a,b\geq 0}s_{a,b}X^{a}Y^{b}
.

Suppose that we have a
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear derivation
D\colon S^{+}\to S^{+}
. Fix an element
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
and consider the
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear derivation
T_{D}=T_{D,\lambda}\colon V^{+}\to V^{+}
uniquely determined by the following properties:

•

T_{D}(s)=D(s)
for any
s\in S^{+}

•

T_{D}(X)=\lambda Y

•

T_{D}(Y)=0
.

Proposition 3.3.1
.

Suppose that
D
extends to a locally analytic action on
S
. Then
T_{D}
extends to a locally analytic action on
V
.

Proof.

We first introduce some polynomial functions that will be useful in the proof of this proposition. For any integers
k\geq 1
and
0\leq r\leq k
, let
\Sigma_{k,r}
denote the set of subsets of
\{0,\dots,k-1\}
of size
r
. For any non-empty
I\in\Sigma_{k,r}
, let
k_{1},\dots,k_{\ell}
be the lengths of the largest blocks of consecutive integers in
I
, so that
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}k_{i}=r
. In other words,
I
can be written as

I=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\ell}\{i_{j},i_{j}+1,\dots,i_{j}+k_{j}-1\}=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\ell}I_{j}

with no adjacent intervals (i.e.
i_{j}>i_{j-1}+k_{j-1}
for all
2\leq j\leq\ell
). For any
I\in\Sigma_{k,r}
, consider the following polynomial function
f_{I}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}
given by the formula

f_{I}(x)=\prod_{1\leq j\leq\ell}\frac{1}{k_{j}!}\prod_{i\in I_{j}}(x-i),\quad\quad x\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}

with the convention that
f_{\varnothing}=1
. We also set
f_{k}=f_{\{0,\dots,k-1\}}
. Then, by a simple induction argument on
k
(see
[
GPR23
, Lemma 4.3.2]
), we have the following formula:

(3.3.2)

f_{k}(T_{D})(sX^{a}Y^{b})=\sum_{r=0}^{\operatorname{min}(k,a)}\sum_{I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k-r}{k_1, \dots, k_{\ell}}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k}{r}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{a}{r}f_{I}(D)(s)\lambda^{r}X^{a-r}Y^{b+r}

for
s\in S
and
a,b\geq 0
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. To show that
T_{D}
extends to a locally analytic action, it suffices to show that there exists a constant
C_{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that for all
k\geq 0
,
s\in S
and
a,b\geq 0
, we have

p^{-k\varepsilon}|\!|f_{k}(T_{D})(sX^{a}Y^{b})|\!|\leq C_{\varepsilon}|\!|sX^{a}Y^{b}|\!|=C_{\varepsilon}|\!|s|\!|.

Indeed, this implies that the operator norm satisfies
|\!|f_{k}(T_{D})|\!|\leq p^{k\varepsilon}C_{\varepsilon}
, and hence any expression
\sum_{k\geq 0}a_{k}f_{k}(T_{D})
, with
p^{k\varepsilon}|a_{k}|\to 0
as
k\to+\infty
, converges (under the operator norm) to a well-defined operator on
V
.

By the formula in (
3.3.2
), it suffices to show that for each
0\leq r\leq\operatorname{min}(k,a)
and
I\in\Sigma_{k,k-r}
, we have

p^{-k\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k-r}{k_1, \dots, k_{\ell}}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k}{r}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{a}{r}f_{I}(D)(s)\lambda^{r}\right|\!\right|\leq C_{\varepsilon}|\!|s|\!|.

Since
D
extends to a locally analytic action on
S
and
f_{I}(-)
is valued in
\mathcal{O}_{L}
, there exists a constant
C_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}
such that
p^{-k\varepsilon/2}|\!|f_{I}(D)(s)|\!|\leq C_{\varepsilon/2}^{\prime}|\!|s|\!|
for all
s\in S
and
k\geq 0
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle p^{-k\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k-r}{k_1, \dots, k_{\ell}}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k}{r}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{a}{r}f_{I}(D)(s)\lambda^{r}\right|\!\right|

\displaystyle\leq p^{-k\varepsilon/2}\left|\!\left|\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k-r}{k_1, \dots, k_{\ell}}^{-1}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{k}{r}^{-1}\right|\!\right|p^{-k\varepsilon/2}|\!|f_{I}(D)(s)|\!|

\displaystyle\leq p^{-k\varepsilon/2+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k-r)+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)}C^{\prime}_{\varepsilon/2}|\!|s|\!|.

Since
\varepsilon>0
, we have
-k\varepsilon/2+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k-r)+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)\to-\infty
as
k\to+\infty
, hence we can find a constant
C_{\varepsilon}
, independent of
k,s,a,b
, such that

p^{-k\varepsilon/2+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k-r)+\operatorname{log}_{p}(k)}C^{\prime}_{\varepsilon/2}\leq C_{\varepsilon}

as required.
∎

3.4.
Overconvergence

To conclude this section, we discuss a result which will allow us to extend
\varepsilon
-actions to “overconvergent neighbourhoods”. Suppose that we have a sequence of
L
-Banach spaces

V_{0}\to V_{1}\to\cdots\to V_{\infty}

and denote the Banach norm on
V_{r}
by
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{r}
. By abuse of notation, if
v\in V_{r}
, then for any
r\leq s\leq\infty
we write
|\!|v|\!|_{s}
for the Banach norm of the image of
v
under the map
V_{r}\to V_{s}
. Finally, suppose that we have continuous operators
T=T_{r}\colon V_{r}\to V_{r}
which are all compatible with each other under the maps above. We assume that
|\!|v|\!|_{s}\leq|\!|v|\!|_{r}
for all
v\in V_{r}
and
r\leq s\leq\infty
.

Proposition 3.4.1

(c.f.
[
GPR23
, Proposition 4.4.1]
)
.

Assume that the following property holds: for any
0<\delta<1
and
r\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
s=s(\delta)\geq r
such that, for all
c\in\mathbb{Q}
,
h\in\mathbb{N}
and
v\in V_{r}
we have

(3.4.2)

|\!|v|\!|_{r}\leq p^{c}\text{ and }|\!|v|\!|_{\infty}\leq p^{c-h}\implies|\!|v|\!|_{s}\leq p^{c-\delta h}.

Assume that, for some
\varepsilon>0
, the operator
T
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
V_{\infty}
. Then, for any
\gamma>\varepsilon
, there exists
s\in\mathbb{N}
(depending only on
\varepsilon
,
\gamma
and the operator norm
|\!|T|\!|_{r}
) such that, for any
v\in V_{r}
,

p^{-k\gamma}|\!|f_{k}(\nabla)(v)|\!|_{s}\to 0\text{ as }k\to+\infty.

Proof.

Let
v\in V_{r}
be such that
|\!|v|\!|_{r}\leq 1
(and hence
|\!|v|\!|_{\infty}\leq 1
). Let
C_{r}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq\varepsilon}
such that
|\!|T|\!|_{r}\leq p^{C_{r}}
. Then we deduce that

|\!|k!f_{k}(T)(v)|\!|_{r}\leq p^{kC_{r}}.

On the other hand, since
T
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
V_{\infty}
, there exists an integer
C_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

|\!|k!f_{k}(T)(v)|\!|_{\infty}\leq p^{-v_{p}(k!)+C_{\varepsilon}+k\varepsilon}=p^{kC_{r}-(kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!))}

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
k
be any sufficiently large integer such that
kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!)>0
, which is always possible as
C_{r}\geq\varepsilon
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
h_{k}=kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!)
is always a positive integer (by increasing
C_{r}
such that
C_{r}-\varepsilon
is an integer).

Applying (
3.4.2
) to the element
k!f_{k}(T)(v)
with
h=h_{k}
, we deduce that, for any
\delta<1
, there exists
s\in\mathbb{N}
such that

|\!|k!f_{k}(T)(v)|\!|_{s}\leq p^{kC_{r}-\delta(kC_{r}-C_{\varepsilon}-k\varepsilon+v_{p}(k!))}.

Now let
\gamma>\varepsilon
. We obtain

(3.4.3)

\displaystyle p^{-k\gamma}|\!|f_{k}(T)(v)|\!|_{s}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{kC_{r}-\delta kC_{r}+\delta C_{\varepsilon}+k\delta\varepsilon-\delta v_{p}(k!)-k\gamma+v_{p}(k!)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{-k\left((\delta-1)C_{r}+(\gamma-\delta\varepsilon)+\frac{(\delta-1)v_{p}(k!)}{k}\right)+\delta C_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{-k\left((\delta-1)C_{r}+(\gamma-\delta\varepsilon)+(\delta-1)\right)+\delta C_{\varepsilon}}

where the last inequality follows from
v_{p}(k!)\leq k
. Since
C_{r}+1-\gamma<C_{r}+1-\varepsilon
, we can choose
\delta<1
(which will depend on
\varepsilon
,
\gamma
and
C_{r}
) such that
\left((\delta-1)C_{r}+(\gamma-\delta\epsilon)+(\delta-1)\right)>0
, which implies that (
3.4.3
) goes to
0
as
k\to+\infty
. This finishes the proof.
∎

4.
Cohomology and correspondences

In §
8
, we will describe the action of certain Hecke correspondences on the cohomology of unitary Shimura varieties with partial compact support. In particular, we will summarise the main results from higher Coleman theory
[
BP21
]
that we will need for the construction of the
p
-adic
L
-function. Unfortunately, the support conditions we need to consider for these Hecke correspondence are slightly different from those in
op.cit.
, and it will therefore be useful to have a slight generalisation of this theory.

Consider the following correspondence of smooth adic spaces (over a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are finite flat. For any subset
\mathcal{Y}\subset\mathcal{X}
, we set
T(\mathcal{Y})=p_{2}p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{Y})
and
T^{t}(\mathcal{Y})=p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Y})
. By our assumptions,
T
and
T^{t}
take (quasi-compact) open subsets to (quasi-compact) open subsets, and closed subsets to closed subsets. We now introduce a version of support conditions suitable for our purposes.

Definition 4.0.1
.

A system of support conditions for the above correspondence is a collection of open subsets
\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and closed subsets
\{\mathcal{Z}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
of
\mathcal{X}
such that:

(1)

\{\mathcal{U}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\mathcal{Z}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
are nested (i.e.
\mathcal{U}_{k+1}\subset\mathcal{U}_{k}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{m+1}\subset\mathcal{Z}_{m}
for all
k,m\in\mathbb{N}
).

(2)

For any
k,m\in\mathbb{N}
, both
\mathcal{U}_{k}
and the complement of
\mathcal{Z}_{m}
are finite unions of quasi-Stein open subspaces.

(3)

For any integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist integers
k^{\prime},m\in\mathbb{N}
such that

T(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}\subset\mathcal{U}_{k}.

(4)

For any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
T^{t}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})\subset\mathcal{Z}_{m}
.

For any locally projective Banach sheaf
\mathscr{F}
on
\mathcal{U}_{1}
, we set

(4.0.2)

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{k}\varprojlim_{m}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k},\mathscr{F})

where the direct limit is with respect to the natural restriction maps and the (derived) inverse limit is with respect to the natural corestriction maps.

Suppose that we have a system of support conditions and a locally projective Banach sheaf
\mathscr{F}
as in Definition
4.0.1
. Fix integers
a,b\in\mathbb{N}
such that
T(\mathcal{U}_{a})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{b}\subset\mathcal{U}_{1}
and suppose that we have a continuous morphism
\phi\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{F}
defined on a open neighbourhood
p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{a})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{b})\subset\mathcal{V}\subset p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{a})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{1})
. Then, for any integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
and integers
k^{\prime}\geq a
,
m\geq b
such that
T(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}\subset\mathcal{U}_{k}
, we obtain an operator
T
defined by the following composition:

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k},\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{2}^{*}}R\Gamma_{p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})}(p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k}),p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}R\Gamma_{p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})}(p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap\mathcal{V},p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi}R\Gamma_{p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})}(p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap\mathcal{V},p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma_{p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})}(p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}}),p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}}\cap T^{t}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}},\mathscr{F})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{cores}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}},\mathscr{F})

where for the restriction map “
\operatorname{res}
” we have used the fact
p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})=p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k^{\prime}})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}_{k})\cap\mathcal{V}\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})
(by properties (1) and (3) in Definition
4.0.1
), the equality follows from excision, the trace map
\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}
in the one constructed in
[
BP21
, Lemma 2.1.2]
, and the corestriction “
\operatorname{cores}
” follows from Definition
4.0.1
(4). By a lengthy verification, one can show that these operators are compatible as one changes
(k,k^{\prime},m,a,b)
with respect to the various restriction and corestriction maps in (
4.0.2
), hence we obtain an operator

T\colon R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})\to R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})

independent of choices (including the choice of
\mathcal{V}
).

We now compare two choices of support conditions.

Definition 4.0.3
.

We say that two systems of support conditions
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime})
are intertwined if:

•

For any pair of integers
k_{1},m_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist a pair of integers
k_{2},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{m_{2}}\subset\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m_{1}}.

•

For any pair of integers
k_{1},m_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist a pair of integers
k_{2},m_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m_{2}}\subset\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{m_{1}}.

In particular, we have
\bigcap_{k,m}\left(\mathcal{U}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}\right)=\bigcap_{k,m}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{m}\right)
.

Lemma 4.0.4
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime})
are two systems of support conditions which are intertwined, and suppose that
\mathscr{F}
is a locally projective Banach sheaf on
\mathcal{U}_{1}\cup\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\prime}
. Then there is a natural quasi-isomorphism

(4.0.5)

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F}).

functorial in
\mathscr{F}
. If
\mathcal{V}
and
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
are open neighbourhoods as above (for the systems
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime})
respectively) and
\phi\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{F}\to p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{F}
is continuous morphism defined on
\mathcal{V}\cup\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, then (
4.0.5
) is
T
-equivariant.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k}=\mathcal{U}_{k}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{k}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\prime\prime}_{m}=\mathcal{Z}_{m}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}^{\prime}
, then the two systems
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime})
are intertwined and we are reduced to proving the statement for these two systems (with the morphism
\phi
restricted to
\mathcal{V}
). Furthermore, we can reduce this two further simpler cases:

(1)

The statement for the systems
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})

(2)

The statement for the systems
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime})
.

Suppose we are in case (1). Then one can show that the restriction maps
R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}\left(\mathcal{U}_{k},\mathscr{F}\right)\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}\left(\mathcal{U}_{k}^{\prime\prime},\mathscr{F}\right)
are compatible as one varies
k,m
and induce a
T
-equivariant map

(4.0.6)

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F}).

We claim that this is a quasi-isomorphism. Let
k_{1},m\in\mathbb{N}
be integers, and
k_{2}\geq k_{1}
such that

\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}\subset\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}\subset\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}.

Then we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}},\mathscr{F})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}},\mathscr{F})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}},\mathscr{F})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}},\mathscr{F})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}},\mathscr{F})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where all the maps are induced from restriction. The right-hand vertical arrow is an isomorphism by excision. This implies that we have factorisations

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k_{1}},\mathscr{F})\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{1}},\mathscr{F})\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}_{k_{2}},\mathscr{F})\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{m}}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime}_{k_{2}},\mathscr{F})\to\cdots

functorial in
m
, which implies that the map (
4.0.6
) is a quasi-isomorphism.

For case (2), the proof of the statement is very similar to (1), but now the quasi-isomorphism

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime};\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{\operatorname{cores}}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})

is built up from corestriction maps. We leave the details to the reader. Finally, we now see that the map (
4.0.5
) is the following composition

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{F})\xleftarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime};\mathscr{F})\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime};\mathscr{F})\xleftarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}_{\bullet}^{\prime},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet}^{\prime};\mathscr{F})

which is
T
-equivariant.
∎

Example 4.0.7
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{Z})
is an open/closed support condition for the correspondence
\mathcal{C}
as in
[
BP
, Definition 6.1.3]
. Then the collection
\mathcal{U}_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T^{k-1}(\mathcal{U})
,
\mathcal{Z}_{m}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(T^{t})^{m-1}(\mathcal{Z})
is a system of support conditions as in Definition
4.0.1
.

5.
Nearly holomorphic automorphic forms

In this section we introduce the unitary Shimura varieties that will be used throughout this article. We will also describe the space of nearly holomorphic automorphic forms equipped with an action of differential operators, and construct classical “evaluation maps” which are closely related to unitary Friedberg–Jacquet periods.

5.1.
PEL data and torsors

In this section we describe the abelian varieties with extra structure that will feature in our moduli problems. Suppose
E=\mathbb{Q}(\sqrt{-d})
for a fixed choice of square-root
\sqrt{-d}
.

5.1.1.
The PEL data

Let
n\geq 2
be an integer and, as in
[
Gra21
, §2]
, we fix a
2n
-dimensional Hermitian space over
F
with signature given by the generalised CM-type of rank
2n

\boldsymbol{\Psi}=\tau_{0}+(2n-1)\tau_{0}^{c}+\sum_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}2n\tau^{c}.

Let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{W}
denote the Hermitian pairing on
W
, and let
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon W\times W\to\mathbb{Q}
denote the induced alternating bilinear pairing given by
\operatorname{tr}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\circ\operatorname{Im}_{\sqrt{-d}}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{W}
, where
\operatorname{Im}_{\sqrt{-d}}\colon F\to F^{+}
is the map given by
x\mapsto(2\sqrt{-d})^{-1}(x+\bar{x})
, and
\operatorname{tr}_{F^{+}}\colon F^{+}\to\mathbb{Q}
is the trace map. Recall we have a decomposition
W\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R}=W^{+}\oplus W^{-}
into maximal subspaces where the Hermitian pairing is
\pm
-definite (we may assume they are orthogonal to each other). We let

h_{\mathbf{G}}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{R}}(W\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})

be the map sending
h_{\mathbf{G}}(z)
to the element which acts as scalar multiplication by
z
(resp.
\bar{z}
) on
W^{+}
(resp.
W^{-}
). Then (with the convention that the Hermitian form is antilinear in the first-variable) the conditions in
[
Lan13
, Definition 1.2.1.2]
are satisfied.

Let
\mathbf{G}
be the unitary similitude group associated with
W
as in
[
Gra21
, Definition 2.0.1]
(note that we can replace
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{W}
with
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
in the definition). We suppose that we have a decomposition
W=W_{1}\oplus W_{2}
into the direct sum of
n
-dimensional Hermitian spaces, with respective generalised CM-types

\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}=\tau_{0}+(n-1)\tau_{0}^{c}+\sum_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}n\tau^{c},\quad\quad\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}=\sum_{\tau\in\Psi}n\tau^{c}.

We let
\mathbf{H}\subset\mathbf{G}
denote the subgroup preserving this decomposition and, similar to above, we obtain a morphism

h_{\mathbf{H}}\colon\mathbb{C}\to\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{R}}(W_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})\oplus\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{R}}(W_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})

satisfying the conditions in
[
Lan13
, Definition 1.2.1.2]
. Without loss of generality, we may assume that
h_{\mathbf{G}}
is the composition of
h_{\mathbf{H}}
with the natural inclusion
\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{R}}(W_{1}\otimes\mathbb{R})\oplus\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{R}}(W_{2}\otimes\mathbb{R})\subset\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb{R}}(W\otimes\mathbb{R})
.

Finally, we fix
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-lattices
L_{i}\subset W_{i}
(
i=1,2
) and set
L=L_{1}\oplus L_{2}\subset W
. We let
L^{\#}
denote the dual lattice under the pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
above.

Assumption 5.1.1
.

Fix an odd prime
p
not dividing
\operatorname{Disc}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}}[L^{\#}:L]
that splits completely in
F/\mathbb{Q}
.

Remark 5.1.2
.

The pairs
(\mathbf{G},h_{\mathbf{G}})
and
(\mathbf{H},h_{\mathbf{H}})
are precisely the PEL Shimura–Deligne data considered in
[
Gra21
]
. In
op.cit.
we worked with the Shimura–Deligne varieties associated with these data, however in this article it seems more natural to work with the associated PEL moduli problems. For
\mathbf{G}
these two spaces are the same, but they can be different for
\mathbf{H}
due to the potential failure of the Hasse principle (see Lemma
5.1.8
below).

We introduce some useful notation:

Notation 5.1.3
.

Let
G
denote the reductive group over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
of symplectic
\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-equivariant similitudes of
L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and
H\subset G
the reductive subgroup preserving the decomposition

L\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}=(L_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\oplus(L_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

We can and do identify

H=\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}(\operatorname{GL}_{n}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n})\subset\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\operatorname{GL}_{2n}=G

with the embedding being the block diagonal one described in
[
Gra21
, Remark 2.0.2]
.

5.1.2.
Unitary abelian varieties

We now introduce unitary abelian varieties. Recall that
F^{\operatorname{cl}}
denotes the Galois closure of
F
.

Definition 5.1.4
.

Let
S
be a locally noetherian scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}},(p)}
. We say that a tuple
(A,\lambda,i)
is a unitary abelian scheme of signature
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
(resp.
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}
, resp.
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}
) if:

(1)

A
is an abelian scheme over
S

(2)

\lambda\colon A\to A^{\vee}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-polarisation

(3)

i\colon\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\to\operatorname{End}_{S}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})_{S}
is a ring homomorphism such that the restriction of the Rosati involution to
\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
coincides with complex conjugation

(4)

The characteristic polynomial of
i(a)
for
a\in\mathcal{O}_{F}
on
\operatorname{Lie}_{A/S}
is given by

\displaystyle(T-\tau_{0}(a))(T-\tau_{0}^{c}(a))^{2n-1}\prod_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}(T-\tau^{c}(a))^{2n}

\displaystyle(\text{ resp. }

\displaystyle(T-\tau_{0}(a))(T-\tau_{0}^{c}(a))^{n-1}\prod_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}(T-\tau^{c}(a))^{n},\text{ resp. }\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}(T-\tau^{c}(a))^{n}).

Note that condition (4) implies that
A/S
has relative dimension
2n[F^{+}\colon\mathbb{Q}]
(resp.
n[F^{+}:\mathbb{Q}]
). For brevity, we will often simply say
A
is a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary (resp.
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}
-unitary, resp.
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}
-unitary) abelian scheme (over
S
).

We also introduce prime-to-
p
level structures.

Definition 5.1.5
.

Let
K^{p}\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be a neat compact open subgroup and
(A,\lambda,i)
a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme over
S
. Then a
K^{p}
-level structure
\eta^{p}
for
A
is the data of a
\pi_{1}(S,s)
-invariant
K^{p}
-orbit of
F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-equivariant symplectic isomorphisms

\eta^{p}_{s}\colon W\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{p}A_{s}

for each geometric point
s\in S
, which are compatible in changing
s
(see
[
Lan13
, Definition 1.3.8.7]
for more details).

Similarly, let
U^{p}\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
be a neat compact open subgroup and let
(A_{1},\lambda_{1},i_{1})
and
(A_{2},\lambda_{2},i_{2})
be
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}
-unitary and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}
-unitary abelian schemes over
S
respectively. Set

(A=A_{1}\oplus A_{2},\lambda=\lambda_{1}\oplus\lambda_{2},i=i_{1}+i_{2})

which is a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme over
S
. Then a
U^{p}
-level structure
\eta^{p}
is the data of a
\pi_{1}(S,s)
-invariant
U^{p}
-orbit of
F\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
-equivariant symplectic isomorphisms

\eta^{p}_{s}\colon(W_{1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\oplus(W_{2}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\xrightarrow{\sim}V^{p}(A_{1})_{s}\oplus V^{p}(A_{2})_{s}

for each geometric point
s\in S
, which are compatible in changing
s
and respect the decompositions on both sides.

We now introduce the moduli of these unitary abelian schemes.

Definition 5.1.6
.

Let
K^{p}\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
U^{p}\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
) be a neat compact open subgroup. We let
X_{\mathbf{G}}
(resp.
X_{\mathbf{H}}
) denote the functor taking a locally noetherian scheme
S
over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}},(p)}
to the set of
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian schemes (resp. pairs of
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{1}
-unitary and
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{2}
-unitary abelian schemes) over
S
equipped with a
K^{p}
(resp
U^{p}
) level structure, up to equivalence (as in
[
Lan13
, §1.4.2]
). This is representable by a smooth projective scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}},(p)}
of dimension
2n-1
(resp.
n-1
).

If
U^{p}\subset K^{p}
, we let
\iota\colon X_{\mathbf{H}}\to X_{\mathbf{G}}
denote the natural finite unramified morphism sending
(A_{1},\lambda_{1},i_{1},A_{2},\lambda_{2},i_{2},\eta^{p})
to
(A_{1}\oplus A_{2},\lambda_{1}\oplus\lambda_{2},i_{1}+i_{2},\eta^{p}K^{p})
.

5.1.3.
Deeper level at
p

We now introduce some additional level structure at the prime
p
. Let
S
be a locally noetherian scheme over
\operatorname{Spec}F^{\operatorname{cl}}
. Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer. Since
p\nmid[L^{\#}:L]
, we have an induced symplectic pairing on
L/p^{\beta}L
which we will continue to denote by
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
. Since
p
splits completely in
F/\mathbb{Q}
, we have a decomposition

L/p^{\beta}L=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Psi}\left(L/\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}L\oplus L/\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}}^{\beta}L\right)

and similarly for
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
. Each factor is a symplectic space with pairing induced from
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
, and both
L/\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}L
and
L/\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}}^{\beta}L
are free of rank
2n
over
\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}
. The decomposition
L/\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}L\oplus L/\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}}^{\beta}L
is a decomposition into maximal isotropic subspaces for the symplectic pairing, in particular
L/\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}}^{\beta}L
is in perfect duality with
L/\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}L
via
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
.

Let
A/S
be a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme. Then the polarisation and endomorphism structure (and the fact that
p
is invertible in
F^{\operatorname{cl}}
) imply that

A[p^{\beta}]=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Psi}\left(A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}]\oplus A[\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}}^{\beta}]\right)

is an etale rank
2n[F^{+}:\mathbb{Q}]
symplectic
\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{F}
group scheme over
S
. Hence it must be etale locally isomorphic to
\left(L/p^{\beta}L\right)_{S}
(since
p
splits completely in
F/\mathbb{Q}
). Similar isomorphisms exist for
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{i}
-unitary schemes (
i=1,2
).

Definition 5.1.7
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer and let
\hat{\gamma}=\gamma w_{n}\in G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the element introduced in Definition
2.1.1
.

(1)

Let
X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})\to X_{\mathbf{G},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
denote the finite etale cover which, for a given point
(A,\lambda,i,\eta^{p})\in X_{\mathbf{G},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(S)
, parameterises flags

0=C_{0,\tau}\subset C_{1,\tau}\subset\cdots\subset C_{2n,\tau}=A[\mathfrak{p}^{\beta}_{\tau}],\quad\quad\tau\in\Psi

where
C_{i,\tau}
is a finite flat (etale) subgroup scheme of rank
p^{i\beta}
, and
C_{i,\tau}/C_{i-1,\tau}
is etale locally isomorphic to
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z})_{S}
(for all
i=1,\dots,2n
). Equivalently, it parameterises
B_{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z})
-orbits of symplectic
\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{F}
-equivariant isomorphisms
\left(L/p^{\beta}L\right)_{S}\cong A[p^{\beta}]
. Here
B_{G}\subset\mathbf{G}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
denotes the upper-triangular Borel subgroup.

(2)

Let
X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})\to X_{\mathbf{H},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
denote the finite etale cover which, for a given point
(A_{1},A_{2},\eta^{p})\in X_{\mathbf{H},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(S)
, parameterises
\hat{\gamma}B_{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z})\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z})
-orbits of symplectic
\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{F}
-equivariant isomorphisms

\left(L_{1}/p^{\beta}L_{1}\right)_{S}\oplus\left(L_{2}/p^{\beta}L_{2}\right)_{S}\cong A_{1}[p^{\beta}]\oplus A_{2}[p^{\beta}]

respecting the decomposition on both sides.

We have a natural finite unramified map
\hat{\iota}\colon X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
given by sending a
\hat{\gamma}B_{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z})\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z})
-orbit of symplectic
\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\beta}\mathcal{O}_{F}
-equivariant isomorphisms

\alpha\colon\left(L_{1}/p^{\beta}L_{1}\right)_{S}\oplus\left(L_{2}/p^{\beta}L_{2}\right)_{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}A_{1}[p^{\beta}]\oplus A_{2}[p^{\beta}]

to the orbit of isomorphisms
[\alpha\circ\hat{\gamma}]
(for the unitary abelian scheme
A=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}
). In particular, we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta+1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta+1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

for any
\beta\geq 1
. The top square is Cartesian (see
[
Gra21
, Lemma 2.5.3]
).

5.1.4.
Relation to Shimura–Deligne varieties

The moduli spaces considered above are closely related to the Shimura–Deligne varieties associated with
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{H}
. More precisely, let
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(resp.
H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
) denote the subgroup of similitudes which preserve the lattice
L=L_{1}\oplus L_{2}
. These are hyperspecial subgroups. We let
S_{\mathbf{G}}
(resp.
S_{\mathbf{H}}
) denote the Shimura–Deligne variety over
F^{\operatorname{cl}}
associated with the datum
(\mathbf{G},h_{\mathbf{G}})
(resp.
(\mathbf{H},h_{\mathbf{H}})
) of level
K^{p}G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
U^{p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
) for some neat compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
(resp.
U^{p}\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
).

For an integer
\beta\geq 1
, let
K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\subset H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the compact open subgroups defined in
[
Gra21
, Definition 2.5.2]
. We let
S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
) denote the Shimura–Deligne variety of level
K^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
U^{p}K_{\diamondsuit}^{H}(p^{\beta})
).

Lemma 5.1.8
.

There exist Cartesian diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathbf{H}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{\mathbf{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the horizontal arrows are open and closed embeddings. Furthermore, the morphisms

\hat{\iota}\colon X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta}),\quad\quad\hat{\iota}\colon S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\to S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})

(the latter given by right-translation by
\hat{\gamma}
) are compatible under these embeddings.

Proof.

Since
p
splits completely in
F/\mathbb{Q}
, there exists a unique (up to isomorphism)
\mathcal{O}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-Hermitian space of a given dimension. In particular, this implies that (over any locally Noetherian scheme
S\to\operatorname{Spec}F^{\operatorname{cl}}
) a prime-to-
p
level structure can be lifted to an orbit of trivialisations of the Tate module at all finite places. Hence all the moduli problems in this lemma can be replaced with the versions which include level stucture at
p
.

The commutative diagrams then follow from
[
Lan12
, §2.5]
– they are Cartesian because the vertical maps are finite etale covers and have the same degree. Finally, we note that the horizontal arrows in the diagram for
\mathbf{G}
are isomorphisms. Indeed, the moduli spaces for
\mathbf{G}
are a disjoint union of Shimura varieties for unitary similitude groups associated with Hermitian spaces which are locally isomorphic (up to similitude) to
W
at all places. By the Hasse principle for
even dimensional
unitary groups (see
[
Kot92
, §7]
), there is only one such Hermitian space (up to similitude), namely
W
.
∎

5.1.5.
Torsors

We now describe the de Rham torsor that will play an important role in the discussion of nearly holomorphic/overconvergent automorphic forms. Let
P_{\mathbf{G}}\subset\mathbf{G}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
denote the parabolic subgroup associated with the Shimura–Deligne datum for
\mathbf{G}
, as defined in
[
Gra21
, §2]
. Let
M_{\mathbf{G}}
denote its Levi subgroup. We let
P_{\mathbf{H}}=P_{\mathbf{G}}\cap\mathbf{H}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
and
M_{\mathbf{H}}
its Levi subgroup. Recall that we have identifications

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{G}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times(\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2n-1})\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}\operatorname{GL}_{2n}

\displaystyle M_{\mathbf{H}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times(\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n-1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n})\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}(\operatorname{GL}_{n}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}).

We let
P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
and
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
denote the opposite parabolic subgroups with respect to the standard upper triangular Borel subgroups of
\mathbf{G}
and
\mathbf{H}
respectively.

Notation 5.1.9
.

For a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme
A
over a locally noetherian scheme
S\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}},(p)}
, let
\mathcal{H}_{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\operatorname{dR}}(A/S)
denote the first relative de Rham homology of
A/S
. This is a vector bundle of rank
4n[F^{+}:\mathbb{Q}]
and comes equipped with the Hodge filtration

(5.1.10)

0\to\omega_{A^{D}}\to\mathcal{H}_{A}\to\operatorname{Lie}(A/S)\to 0

where
\omega_{A^{D}}=\pi_{*}\Omega^{1}_{A^{D}/S}
denotes the Hodge bundle (with
\pi\colon A^{D}\to S
) and
\operatorname{Lie}(A/S)
denotes the Lie algebra of
A/S
.

The
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-endomorphism action induces a decomposition

\mathcal{H}_{A}=\bigoplus_{\tau\in\Psi}(\mathcal{H}_{A,\tau}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{A,\bar{\tau}})

into isotypic pieces (where on each piece,
\mathcal{O}_{F}
acts through scalar multiplication via the corresponding embedding). One has a similar exact sequence to (
5.1.10
) on isotypic pieces. The same discussion applies to
\boldsymbol{\Psi}_{i}
-unitary abelian schemes (
i=1,2
) and we will use similar notation for the de Rham homology and Hodge bundles.

We now introduce the de Rham torsors.

Definition 5.1.11
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer.

(1)

Let
G_{\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denote the right
\mathbf{G}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
-torsor parameterising isomorphisms

\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{H}_{A},\quad\quad A\in X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})(S)

which respect the symplectic and endomorphism structures on both sides. Similarly, let
H_{\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denote the right
\mathbf{H}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
-torsor parameterising isomorphisms

(\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{1})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}W_{2})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}},\quad\quad(A_{1},A_{2})\in X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})(S)

respecting the symplectic, endomorphism structures and the decompositions on both sides.

(2)

Let
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
and
P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denote the
P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
and
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
reductions of
G_{\operatorname{dR}}
and
H_{\operatorname{dR}}
respectively, given by trivialisations which respect the Hodge filtration.

(3)

Let
M_{G,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
and
M_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denote the pushouts of
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}
and
P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}
along the projection maps
P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}\to M_{\mathbf{G}}
and
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}\to M_{\mathbf{H}}
respectively.

Note that the pullback of any of these torsors along the open and closed embedding of the corresponding Shimura–Deligne variety (see Lemma
5.1.8
) coincides with the torsors considered in
[
Gra21
]
.

5.2.
D-modules on flag varieties

In this section, we describe the construction of
\mathcal{D}
-modules on flag varieties from
(\mathfrak{g},P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}})
or
(\mathfrak{h},P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}})
representations. We will then explain how to transport these modules to
\mathcal{D}
-modules on Shimura varieties, and prove a key result (Corollary
5.2.17
) which will be used throughout the rest of this section. To ease notation, we will only describe the construction for
\mathbf{G}
– the construction for
\mathbf{H}
(or indeed any well-behaved Shimura variety) follows exactly the same arguments.

5.2.1.
Running notation

In this subsection, we fix some notation that will be used in the rest of this section. Since we will only consider the setting for
\mathbf{G}
, we will drop the group from the notation for almost all objects in this section.

Throughout, we will let
k=F^{\operatorname{cl}}
. We let
\operatorname{FL}=\operatorname{FL}_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}=\mathbf{G}_{k}/P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
denote the partial flag variety over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
. Also, to simplify notation, we will write
G=\mathbf{G}_{k}
,
P=P_{\mathbf{G}}
,
\overline{P}=P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
, and
M=M_{\mathbf{G}}
. We will denote the Lie algebra of
\mathbf{G}
(resp.
P
, resp.
\overline{P}
) by
\mathfrak{g}
(resp.
\mathfrak{p}
, resp.
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
), and let
\mathfrak{u}
denote the (upper-triangular) nilpotent part of
\mathfrak{p}
. The adjoint action of a group on its Lie algebra will be denoted by
\operatorname{Ad}
.

Definition 5.2.1
.

Let
\operatorname{Rep}_{k}(\mathfrak{g},\overline{P})
denote the category of
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{P})
-representations, i.e., algebraic representations
V
of
\overline{P}
which come equipped with an action of
\mathfrak{g}
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

For any
p\in\overline{P}
,
X\in\mathfrak{g}
and
v\in V
, one has

p\cdot(X\cdot v)=(\operatorname{Ad}(p)X)\cdot v.

(2)

For any
X\in\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
and
v\in V
, one has

X\cdot v=\left.\left(\frac{d}{dt}\operatorname{exp}(tX)\cdot v\right)\right|_{t=0}.

For any scheme
Y
over
\operatorname{Spec}(k)
, we let
\mathcal{T}_{Y}
denote its tangent bundle and
\mathcal{D}_{Y}
the sheaf of differential operators. If
\mathcal{A}
is a sheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-algebras, then we let
\operatorname{Der}_{k}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
denote the
k
-module of
k
-linear derivations
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}
. Finally, we fix an integer
\beta\geq 1
and set
X=X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
.

5.2.2.
Actions on the flag variety

We will consider the following sheaves of Lie algebras on the flag variety
\operatorname{FL}
.

Definition 5.2.2
.

Set
\mathfrak{g}^{0}=\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}
. We denote by
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
the vector bundle on
\operatorname{FL}
which on any open
U\subset\operatorname{FL}
is given by

\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}(U)=\{f\colon U\to\mathfrak{g}:f(x)\in\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{x}\text{ for all }x\in U\}

where
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{x}
denotes the Lie algebra of the parabolic corresponding to the point
x\in U\subset\operatorname{FL}
. The tangent bundle of
\operatorname{FL}
then coincides with
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}=\mathfrak{g}^{0}/\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0}
.

Let
\pi\colon G\to\operatorname{FL}
be the natural right
\overline{P}
-torsor. Then, as explained in
[
GPR23
, Appendix A]
, to any
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{P})
-representation
V
one has a natural action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
on
\mathcal{V}=\left(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{k}V\right)^{\overline{P}}
(denoted
\star_{\mathcal{D}}
) factoring through
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. Let us recall how this action is constructed.

Definition 5.2.3
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be as above. Then we define an action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
on
\mathcal{V}
by the following formula: for
\lambda\in\mathfrak{g}^{0}(U)
viewed as a function
U\to\mathfrak{g}
, and
F\in\mathcal{V}(U)
viewed as a function
F\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to V
, we set

(\lambda\star_{\mathcal{D}}F)(g)=(\lambda(g)\star_{1}F)(g)-(\operatorname{Ad}(g^{-1})\lambda(g)\star_{2}F)(g)

where
\star_{1}
denotes the action of
\mathfrak{g}
induced from differentiating the
G
-equivariant structure
(h\cdot F)(-)=F(h^{-1}\cdot-)
(
h\in G
), and
\star_{2}
denotes the
\mathfrak{g}
-action on
V
.

We explain how to construct a “horizontal action” out of this for a specific choice of
V
.

Definition 5.2.4
.

Let
k[\overline{P}]
denote the
k
-algebra of algebraic functions on
\overline{P}
. This comes equipped with two actions of
\overline{P}
, namely for
p\in\overline{P}
we set

(p\star_{l}f)(-)=f(p^{-1}\cdot-),\quad(p\star_{r}f)(-)=f(-\cdot p),\quad f\in k[\overline{P}].

The
\star_{l}
-action can be enhanced to a
(\mathfrak{g},\overline{P})
-action, by considering the open immersion
\overline{P}\hookrightarrow G/U
, where
U
is the unipotent radical of
P
, and differentiating the
G
-action on
G/U
. This representation comes with some additional structure, namely the action of
\mathfrak{g}
(resp.
\overline{P}
) is through derivations (resp. algebra automorphisms).

Remark 5.2.5
.

Let
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
and let
W_{\kappa}
denote the algebraic representation of
M
of highest weight
\kappa
. Then the (relative) Verma module of weight
\kappa
is given by
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes_{\mathcal{U}(\overline{\mathfrak{p}})}W_{\kappa}
and the dual of this representation in the Bernstein–Gelfand–Gelfand category
\mathcal{O}
coincides with the representation
(k[\overline{P}]\otimes_{k}W_{\kappa})^{(M,\star_{r})}
(invariants of the action of
M
under
\star_{r}\otimes\cdot
).

We now construct the desired action.

Lemma 5.2.6
.

Let
V=k[\overline{P}]
. Then the action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
on
\mathcal{V}
can be extended to an action of
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathfrak{g}^{0}
factoring through the quotient
