(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathfrak{g}^{0})/(\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}^{0})=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. In particular, we obtain an induced Lie algebra homomorphism

(5.2.7)

\mathfrak{u}\to\operatorname{Der}_{k}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{V})

where
\mathfrak{u}
denotes the Lie algebra of the unipotent radical of
P
.

Proof.

Let
\star_{\mathcal{D}}
denote the action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
on
\mathcal{V}
. Note that we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}(U)

\displaystyle=\{f\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to k[\overline{P}]:f(-\cdot p)=p^{-1}\star_{l}f(-)\text{ for all }p\in\overline{P}\}

\displaystyle=\{F\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\times\overline{P}\to k:F(-\cdot p,-)=F(-,p\cdot-)\text{ for all }p\in\overline{P}\}

\displaystyle=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}(U)

so we get an action of
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathfrak{g}^{0}
by using the multiplication structure on
\mathcal{V}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}
(i.e.
x\otimes y
acts as
x\cdot(y\star_{\mathcal{D}}-)
). Clearly this action factors through
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
.

Alternatively, one can view an element
\lambda\in\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathfrak{g}^{0}
as a function
\lambda\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to\mathfrak{g}
and define the action as

(\lambda\;\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\;F)(g,p)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\lambda(gp)\star_{1}F)(g,p)-(\operatorname{Ad}(g^{-1})\lambda(gp)\star_{2}F)(g,p)

where
\star_{1}
is the
\mathfrak{g}
-action induced from left-translation on the first variable, and
\star_{2}
arises from the
\mathfrak{g}
-action on
k[\overline{P}]
. Now the action in (
5.2.7
) is given as follows. For
X\in\mathfrak{u}
, we consider the function
\lambda_{X}\colon\pi^{-1}(U)\to\mathfrak{g}
given by
\lambda_{X}(g)=-\operatorname{Ad}(g)X
. The action of
X
is then given by the action of
\lambda_{X}
. Explicitly, we have

(5.2.8)

(\lambda_{X}\;\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\;F)(g,p)=(-\operatorname{Ad}(gp)X\star_{1}F)(g,p)+(\operatorname{Ad}(p)X\star_{2}F)(g,p).

Note that the second term in (
5.2.8
) vanishes because
X\in\mathfrak{u}
, and we can rewrite the action in (
5.2.8
) as

\lambda_{X}\;\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}\;F=X\star_{r}F,\quad\quad\quad F\in\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}(U)=\mathcal{O}_{G}(\pi^{-1}U),

where
\star_{r}
denotes the action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
\mathcal{O}_{G}
given by differentiating the action of
G
given by right-translation of the argument. This automatically implies that
X\mapsto\lambda_{X}\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}-
is a Lie algebra homomorphism (i.e., the actions of
\lambda_{X}
and
\lambda_{Y}
commute for
X,Y\in\mathfrak{u}
).
∎

Note that
\mathcal{V}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}
comes with an additional action of
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
given by differentiating
\star_{r}
on
k[\overline{P}]
(which coincides with the action of
\star_{r}
on
\mathcal{O}_{G}
above). We will denote this action by
\star_{\overline{P}}
. We have the following key relation:

Proposition 5.2.9
.

Let
\mathcal{V}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}
. Let
X\in\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
,
Y\in\mathfrak{u}
, and let
[X,Y]\in\mathfrak{g}
denote the Lie bracket as elements of
\mathfrak{g}
. Suppose that
[X,Y]\in\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then for any
F\in\mathcal{V}
, we have the relation

X\star_{\overline{P}}(Y\star_{\mathfrak{u}}F)=Y\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(X\star_{\overline{P}}F)+[X,Y]\star_{\overline{P}}F

where
\star_{\mathfrak{u}}
denotes the action of
\mathfrak{u}
constructed in Lemma
5.2.6
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the fact that
\star_{\mathfrak{u}}
and
\star_{\overline{P}}
are both induced from the action of
\mathfrak{g}
on
\mathcal{O}_{G}
via
\star_{r}
.
∎

5.2.3.
Passage to Shimura varieties

In this section we describe the relation between the tangent bundles of Shimura varieties and flag varieties. Recall that
X=X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denotes the Shimura–Deligne variety associated with
\mathbf{G}
of level
K^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Consider the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{\operatorname{dR}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{FL}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

where
G_{\operatorname{dR}}
denotes the right
G
-torsor (the standard principal bundle) parameterising frames of the first de Rham homology respecting PE-structures (see Definition
5.1.11
). Here
p
denotes the natural map, and
q
denotes the map measuring the position of the Hodge filtration with respect to such a frame. The map
q
is
G
-equivariant, i.e.,
q(xg)=g^{-1}q(x)
.

Since
G_{\operatorname{dR}}\to X
is a principal
G
-torsor, we have a
G
-equivariant short exact sequence

(5.2.10)

0\to\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{p\text{-vert}}\to\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\xrightarrow{dp}p^{*}\mathcal{T}_{X}\to 0

where
\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{p\text{-vert}}=\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes_{k}\mathfrak{g}
with the
G
-equivariant structure given by the diagonal action (i.e., the
G
-equivariant structure on
\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}
and the adjoint action on
\mathfrak{g}
). Since the relative de Rham homology comes equipped with an integrable connection (the Gauss–Manin connection), we obtain a
G
-equivariant splitting
s_{X}\colon p^{*}\mathcal{T}_{X}\to\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}
of (
5.2.10
) whose image we denote by
\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{\operatorname{horz}}
. Since the connection is integrable, the subbundle
\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{\operatorname{horz}}
is closed under the
G
-equivariant Lie bracket on
\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}
. In particular, the section
s_{X}
induces a
G
-equivariant Lie bracket structure
[\cdot,\cdot]_{X}
on
p^{*}\mathcal{T}_{X}
– concretely this is described on pure tensors as

[f\otimes v,f^{\prime}\otimes v^{\prime}]_{X}=s_{X}(f\otimes v)f^{\prime}\otimes v^{\prime}-s_{X}(f^{\prime}\otimes v^{\prime})f\otimes v+ff^{\prime}\otimes[v,v^{\prime}]_{X}

for
f\otimes v
and
f^{\prime}\otimes v^{\prime}
elements of
\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{X}}p^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{X}
(and we also use the notation
[\cdot,\cdot]_{X}
to denote the Lie bracket on
\mathcal{T}_{X}
). As seen from this description, this Lie bracket extends the one on
\mathcal{T}_{X}
, so the notation is justified.

On the other hand, let
\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{q\text{-vert}}
denote the kernel of the
G
-equivariant map
dq\colon\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\to q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. The morphism
dq
is surjective and we have a G-equivariant short exact sequence

(5.2.11)

0\to\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{q\text{-vert}}\to\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\xrightarrow{dq}q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}\to 0

We have the following result:

Proposition 5.2.12
.

One has an isomorphism
dq\colon\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{\operatorname{horz}}\xrightarrow{\sim}q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. In particular, we obtain a
G
-equivariant splitting
s_{\operatorname{FL}}\colon q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}\to\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}
of (
5.2.11
).

Proof.

This is proved in
[
Har85
]
. The result is first established over
\mathbb{C}
(as complex manifolds) and then is shown to be algebraic and descends to
F^{\operatorname{cl}}
.
∎

As a consequence of the splitting
s_{\operatorname{FL}}
, we obtain a
G
-equivariant Lie bracket on
q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
which extends the bracket on
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. We have an induced
G
-equivariant Lie algebra isomorphism
p^{*}\mathcal{T}_{X}\xrightarrow{s_{X}}\mathcal{T}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}^{\operatorname{horz}}\xrightarrow{dq}q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
, which encodes the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism.

We now recall how one passes from
\mathcal{D}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-modules on
\operatorname{FL}
to
\mathcal{D}_{X}
-modules on
X
.

Definition 5.2.13
.

Let
\mathcal{V}
be a quasi-coherent
G
-equivariant sheaf on
\operatorname{FL}
. We set
[\mathcal{V}]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(p_{*}q^{*}\mathcal{V})^{G}
, which defines a quasi-coherent sheaf on
X
.

Example 5.2.14
.

As seen from Proposition
5.2.12
, one has
\mathcal{T}_{X}\cong[\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}]
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{V}
is a quasi-coherent
G
-equivariant sheaf on
\operatorname{FL}
equipped with a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra action of
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
, i.e., a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra morphism

\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}\to\underline{\operatorname{End}}_{k}(\mathcal{V})

where the latter is equipped with the commutator bracket. Then we obtain a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra action of
q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
on
q^{*}\mathcal{V}
. Explicitly, given
f\otimes v\in\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}q^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
and
\lambda\otimes\gamma\in\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}q^{-1}\mathcal{V}
, the action is given by

(f\otimes v)\cdot(\lambda\otimes\gamma)=s_{\operatorname{FL}}(f\otimes v)(\lambda)\otimes\gamma+f\lambda\otimes v\cdot\gamma.

This induces a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra action of
p^{*}\mathcal{T}_{X}\cong q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
on
q^{*}\mathcal{V}
, and by passing to
G
-invariants, we obtain a Lie algebra action of
\mathcal{T}_{X}
on
[\mathcal{V}]
.

Example 5.2.15
.

Let
\pi\colon G\to\operatorname{FL}
be the natural (right)
\overline{P}
-torsor, and let
\mathcal{V}
denote the
G
-equivariant sheaf on
\operatorname{FL}
given by the bundle associated with the standard representation of
\overline{P}
(via this torsor). This carries an action of
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
(see §
5.2.2
) and the induced action of
\mathcal{T}_{X}
on
[\mathcal{V}]
simply corresponds to the Gauss–Manin connection on the first de Rham homology.

We will analyse this construction further for a specific choice of
\mathcal{V}
. For the rest of this subsection let
\mathcal{V}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}
, where
\pi\colon G\to\operatorname{FL}
is the natural
\overline{P}
-torsor. This comes equipped with a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra action of
\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
(as explained in §
5.2.2
). We consider the following
G
-equivariant sheaves of Lie algebras:

•

\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
with
G
-equivariant Lie bracket induced by the Leibniz rule, i.e., for
f\otimes v
and
f^{\prime}\otimes v^{\prime}
elements of
\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
the bracket is given

[f\otimes v,f^{\prime}\otimes v^{\prime}]_{\mathcal{D}}=f(v\cdot f^{\prime})\otimes v^{\prime}-(v^{\prime}\cdot f)f^{\prime}\otimes v+ff^{\prime}\otimes[v,v^{\prime}]_{\operatorname{FL}}.

As indicated by the notation, this bracket is induced from a similar bracket
[\cdot,\cdot]_{\mathcal{D}}
on
\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}\otimes\mathfrak{g}^{0}
via the constructions in §
5.2.2
.

•

q^{*}(\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}})=q^{*}\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}}q^{*}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}\cong q^{*}\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{G_{\operatorname{dR}}}}p^{*}\mathcal{T}_{X}
with
G
-equivariant Lie bracket defined similarly.

•

[\mathcal{V}]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{T}_{X}
with Lie bracket induced from taking
G
-invariants of the Lie bracket in the above bullet point.

We have a natural action of
\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
on
\mathcal{V}
given by
(f\otimes v)\cdot x=f(v\cdot x)
for
f\otimes v\in\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
, which is a
G
-equivariant Lie algebra action. By pulling back along
q
and taking
G
-invariants, we obtain a Lie algebra action of
[\mathcal{V}]\otimes\mathcal{T}_{X}
on
[\mathcal{V}]
extending the action of
\mathcal{T}_{X}
.

We recall that we are in the following situation. Let
\mathfrak{u}
denote the Lie algebra of the unipotent radical of
P_{\mathbf{G}}
, which comes equipped with the trivial Lie bracket and the trivial action of
G
. Then, as explained in the proof of Lemma
5.2.6
, we have a
G
-equivariant map of Lie algebras:

\mathfrak{u}\to\left(\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}\right)(\operatorname{FL})

i.e., commuting
G
-invariant global sections of
\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}}\mathcal{T}_{\operatorname{FL}}
. Then, by pulling back under
q
and taking
G
-invariants, we obtain a Lie algebra morphism
\mathfrak{u}\to\left([\mathcal{V}]\otimes\mathcal{T}_{X}\right)
, and hence a Lie algebra action

\mathfrak{u}\to\operatorname{End}_{k}([\mathcal{V}]).

In fact this action map factors through
\operatorname{Der}_{k}([\mathcal{V}])
(the space of derivations
[\mathcal{V}]\to[\mathcal{V}]
), which makes sense because
[\mathcal{V}]
carries an algebra structure. One can easily show that
[\mathcal{V}]\cong\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{\operatorname{dR}}}
, where
\pi\colon P_{\operatorname{dR}}\to X
denotes the
\overline{P}
-reduction of
G_{\operatorname{dR}}
(frames of the first relative de Rham homology preserving the Hodge filtration – see Definition
5.1.11
). We denote this action by
\star_{\mathfrak{u}}
.

Recall
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
denotes the Lie algebra of
\overline{P}
. Then
[\mathcal{V}]
has a
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-linear action of
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
given by differentiating the torsor structure, which we denote by
\star_{\overline{P}}
. We have the following analogue of Proposition
5.2.9
.

Proposition 5.2.16
.

Let
\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
,
\delta\in\mathfrak{u}
and let
[\gamma,\delta]\in\mathfrak{g}
denote the Lie bracket of
\gamma
and
\delta
viewed as elements of
\mathfrak{g}
. Suppose
[\gamma,\delta]\in\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
. Then for any
F\in[\mathcal{V}]
, we have the relation

\gamma\star_{\overline{P}}(\delta\star_{\mathfrak{u}}F)=\delta\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(\gamma\star_{\overline{P}}F)+[\gamma,\delta]\star_{\overline{P}}F.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.2.9
, the analogous relation holds for
\mathcal{V}=\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{G}
. Since the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}
on
\mathcal{V}
is
G
-equivariant and
\mathcal{O}_{\operatorname{FL}}
-linear, the result therefore follows by pulling back under
q
and passing to
G
-invariants.
∎

It will be useful to iterate this relation.

Corollary 5.2.17
.

For
i,j\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
, let
E_{ij}\in\mathfrak{g}
denote the elementary matrix with
1
in the
(i,j)
-th place in the
\tau_{0}
-component, and
0
outside the
\tau_{0}
-component. For
j\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
, let
x_{j}\colon[\mathcal{V}]\to[\mathcal{V}]
denote the endomorphism given by
E_{1,j}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}-
. Then for any polynomial
p\in k[x_{n+1},\dots,x_{2n}]
and any
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
, one has

E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}(p\cdot F)=p\cdot(E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}F)+\sum_{j=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial p}{\partial x_{j}}\cdot(E_{i,j}\star_{\overline{P}}F)

for any
F\in[\mathcal{V}]
.

Proof.

By linearity, it is enough to prove this when
p
is a monomial. Note that
[E_{i,1},E_{1,j}]=E_{i,j}
and
[E_{i,j},E_{1,j^{\prime}}]=0
for any
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
,
j,j^{\prime}\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
. The result then follows from Proposition
5.2.16
and a simple induction argument on the degree of
p
, using the Leibniz rule.
∎

5.3.
Classical nearly holomorphic forms

In this section we introduce the sheaves of nearly holomorphic forms for
G
and
H
, and describe a classical “evaluation map” on the cohomology of these sheaves which encodes the twisted unitary Friedberg–Jacquet periods.

5.3.1.
Nearly holomorphic forms

Recall from Definition
5.1.11
that we have torsors

\pi_{G}\colon P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})\quad\text{ and }\quad\pi_{H}\colon P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta}).

We introduce the following notation:

Notation 5.3.1
.

Let
\mathscr{N}_{G}=\mathscr{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\pi_{G})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}
and
\mathscr{N}_{H}=\mathscr{O}_{P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\pi_{H})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}}
, which we refer to as the sheaves of nearly holomorphic forms for
G
and
H
respectively. Given an algebraic representation
V
of
M_{\mathbf{G}}
of highest weight
\kappa
, we let
\mathscr{N}_{G,\kappa}=(\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V)^{M_{\mathbf{G}}}
where the invariants are with respect to the natural diagonal action of
M_{\mathbf{G}}\subset P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
. We use similar notation for
\mathscr{N}_{H}
with respect to algebraic representations of
M_{\mathbf{H}}
.

Remark 5.3.2
.

The sheaf
\mathscr{N}_{G,\kappa}
is the quasi-coherent sheaf on
X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
associated with the dual Verma module of weight
\kappa
, via the construction in §
5.2.3
. A similar assertion is true for
H
.

The sheaf
\mathscr{N}_{G}
(resp.
\mathscr{N}_{H}
) comes equipped with an action of
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G}=\operatorname{Lie}P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
(resp.
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}=\operatorname{Lie}P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
) given by differentiating the torsor structure. We let
\mathscr{M}_{G}\subset\mathscr{N}_{G}
(resp.
\mathscr{M}_{H}\subset\mathscr{N}_{H}
) denote the subsheaf of elements killed by the action of the nilpotent subalgebra
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G}
(resp.
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}
) – these are the sheaves of holomorphic forms for
G
and
H
respectively. We have identifications
\mathscr{M}_{G}=(\pi^{\prime}_{G})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{M_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}
and
\mathscr{M}_{H}=(\pi^{\prime}_{H})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{M_{H,\operatorname{dR}}}
where
\pi^{\prime}_{G}\colon M_{G,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
and
\pi^{\prime}_{H}\colon M_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\to X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denote the torsors in Definition
5.1.11
. We will also use the notation
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa}
if we wish to specify the weight, and similarly for
H
.

As explained in §
5.2
, the sheaf
\mathscr{N}_{G}
is in fact a
\mathcal{D}_{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}
-module on
X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
and we have an action of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}
(the Lie algebra of the unipotent of
P_{\mathbf{G}}
) on
\mathscr{N}_{G}
through derivations. Recall that
\mathfrak{u}_{G}\cong\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\oplus 2n-1}
corresponding to the Lie algebra of the unipotent of the standard
(1,2n-1)
-parabolic in
\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
(using the identification of
\mathbf{G}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}}
as a product of general linear groups). Here is a more concrete description of this action:

Lemma 5.3.3
.

For
i=1,\dots,2n-1
, let

\nabla_{i}\colon\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}

denote the derivation given by the action of the element
(0,\dots,0,1,0,\dots,0)
of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}=\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\oplus 2n-1}
with
1
in the
i
-th place. Then
\nabla_{i}
can be described as the composition of:

•

The
\mathcal{D}
-module structure
\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}

•

The inverse of the Kodaira–Spencer map
\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}}\otimes\omega_{A,\tau_{0}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Omega^{1}_{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})}
, where
A\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
denotes the universal
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme, and
A^{D}
its dual

•

The adjoint map
\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}}\otimes\omega_{A,\tau_{0}}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}
to the morphism

\pi_{G}^{*}\left(\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}}\otimes\omega_{A,\tau_{0}}\right)\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}

which is obtained as follows. Over
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}
, we have universal trivialisations
\psi_{1}\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{G}^{*}(\omega_{A,\tau_{0}}^{\vee})
and
\psi_{2}\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}^{\oplus 2n-1}\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{G}^{*}(\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}})
. Let
\pi_{G}^{*}(\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}})\otimes\pi_{G}^{*}(\omega_{A,\tau_{0}})\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}
denote the map
\operatorname{pr}_{i}\circ\;\psi_{2}^{-1}\otimes\psi_{1}^{\vee}
, where
\operatorname{pr}_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}^{\oplus 2n-1}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}}
denotes projection to the
i
-th component.

•

The multiplication map
\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}
.

We have a similar description for the
\mathfrak{u}_{H}
action on
\mathscr{N}_{H}
; in particular we will use the notation
\nabla_{i}\colon\mathscr{N}_{H}\to\mathscr{N}_{H}
(
i=1,\dots,n-1
) for the derivations coming from the standard basis vectors of
\mathfrak{u}_{H}=\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\oplus n-1}\subset\mathfrak{u}_{G}
.

Let
k=F^{\operatorname{cl}}
and let
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
denote the space of polynomial functions
k^{\oplus 2n-1}\to k
, which we equip with an action of
M_{\mathbf{G}}
via the formula:

(5.3.4)

(m\cdot\phi)(a)=\phi(m_{\tau_{0}}^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
a^{t}&1\end{array}\right)m_{\tau_{0}}),\quad\phi\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k),m\in M_{\mathbf{G}}.

Here we view the row vector
a\in k^{\oplus 2n-1}
as a lower triangular matrix
\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
a^{t}&1\end{array}\right)
in the lower triangular unipotent of the
(1,2n-1)
-parabolic of
\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
, and
m_{\tau_{0}}
is the projection of
m
to the
\tau_{0}
-component of
M_{\mathbf{G}}
. Note that
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
is naturally isomorphic to the universal enveloping algebra of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}
equipped with the adjoint action of
M_{\mathbf{G}}
. We therefore obtain an action map

(5.3.5)

C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}

which one can verify is
M_{\mathbf{G}}
-equivariant by the explicit description of the action of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}
in §
5.2
.

5.3.2.
Classical evaluations maps

Let
k=F^{\operatorname{cl}}
for ease of notation. We now describe the construction of a linear functional on the cohomology of
X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},k}(p^{\beta})
which will be shown to recover unitary Friedberg–Jacquet periods in the following subsection.

The first step is to apply the general branching law construction in §
2.3
to construct a map of sheaves from nearly holomorphic forms for
\mathbf{G}
to nearly holomorphic forms for
\mathbf{H}
incorporating the action of certain differential operators. Recall from §
5.1.3
that we have a finite unramified morphism:

\hat{\iota}\colon X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,k}(p^{\beta})\to X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},k}(p^{\beta}).

Lemma 5.3.6
.

The natural
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
-equivariant map
P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\to P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}
given by sending a trivialisation of
\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{A_{2}}
to the induced trivialisation of
\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}\oplus A_{2}}
gives a reduction of structure

\hat{\iota}^{*}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}=P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}\times^{P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}}P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}.

Hence we obtain a natural
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
-equivariant map of sheaves
\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H}
.

Proof.

Immediate from the definitions.
∎

We now construct the aforementioned map of sheaves. Recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}
from §
2.2.1
, i.e., pairs
(\kappa,j)
where
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
is a
M_{\mathbf{G}}
-dominant weight that is “pure of non-positive weight” and
j=(j_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Psi}
is a tuple of integers, such that
\kappa
and
j
satisfy a certain intertwining property. For
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
, let
V_{\kappa}
denote the algebraic representation of
M_{\mathbf{G}}
of highest weight
\kappa
, and recall that we have an eigenvector
\delta_{\kappa,j}\in V_{\kappa}\otimes_{k}C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
for the diagonal action of
M_{\mathbf{H}}
with eigencharacter
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}
(see Definition
2.2.9
).

Definition 5.3.7
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and
\beta\geq 1
. Then we define a
k
-linear map

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}

of abelian sheaves over
X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},k}(p^{\beta})
as the
M_{\mathbf{H}}
-invariants of the map

\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H}

arising as the composition of the following
M_{\mathbf{H}}
-equivariant maps:

•

The morphism
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}
induced from sending the first factor to
\delta_{\kappa,j}
.

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to V_{\kappa}\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}
induced from the action map in (
5.3.5
).

•

The morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}
induced from the natural map
V_{\kappa}\otimes V^{*}_{\kappa}\to k
.

•

The morphism
\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H}
.

We have the following result which crucially uses the property in Corollary
5.2.17
.

Proposition 5.3.8
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and
\beta\geq 1
. Then the morphism
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
induces a morphism

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}},

i.e., the morphism takes holomorphic forms for
\mathbf{G}
to holomorphic forms for
\mathbf{H}
.

Proof.

We can (and do) assume that
j_{\tau_{0}}>0
, otherwise there is nothing to prove. We first note the following property. Let
E_{i,k}\in\mathfrak{gl}_{2n}
denote the elementary matrix with
1
in the
(i,k)
-th position and
0
elsewhere. We can view
E_{i,k}\in\mathfrak{g}
as elements only concentrated in the
\tau_{0}
-component.

If
x_{i}\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
is the polynomial function sending
(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\mapsto a_{i}
, then the action of
x_{i}
on
\mathscr{N}_{G}
corresponds to the action of
E_{1,i}\in\mathfrak{u}_{G}
under
\star_{\mathfrak{u}}
. Note that we have
E_{i,1}\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
for
i=2,\dots,n
. We have the commutator relations

[E_{i,1},E_{1,k}]=E_{i,k}\in\operatorname{Lie}M_{\mathbf{G}}\quad\quad\text{ for }i\in\{2,\dots,n\}\text{ and }k\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}

and

[E_{i,k},E_{1,k^{\prime}}]=0\quad\quad\text{ for }i\in\{2,\dots,n\}\text{ and }k,k^{\prime}\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}.

Therefore, by Corollary
5.2.17
, we find that for any polynomial
p\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
in the coordinates
x_{n+1},\dots,x_{2n}
, any
i=2,\dots,n
and any
F\in\mathscr{N}_{G}
, we have

(5.3.9)

E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}(p\star_{\mathfrak{u}}F)=p\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}F)+\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial p}{\partial x_{k}}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(E_{i,k}\star_{\overline{P}}F)

where we have also used the notation
\star_{\mathfrak{u}}
to denote the action map in (
5.3.5
).

Now suppose that we have an element
F\in(\mathscr{M}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*})^{M_{\mathbf{G}}}\subset(\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*})^{M_{\mathbf{G}}}
. We can also view this as a
M_{\mathbf{G}}
-equivariant map
\widetilde{F}\colon V_{\kappa}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}
. For any finite multiset
T\subset\{2,\dots,2n\}
, we let
x^{T}=\prod_{i\in T}x_{i}\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
. These form a
k
-basis of
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
. Let
\{v_{l}\}
denote a fixed basis for
V_{\kappa}
and
\{v_{l}^{*}\}\subset V_{\kappa}^{*}
the dual basis. We note that
\delta_{\kappa,j}
can be expressed as a linear combination

\delta_{\kappa,j}=\sum_{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}\\
\#T=j_{\tau_{0}}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{l,T}(v_{l}\otimes x^{T})

for some
\lambda_{l,T}\in k
.

Let
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\prime}\colon\mathscr{N}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}
denote the composition of the maps in Definition
5.3.7
, except for the final bullet point (we omit the notation for the character
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}
). Then we have
h\cdot\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\prime}(F)=\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}(h)\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\prime}(F)
for all
h\in M_{\mathbf{H}}
. Since the map
\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H}
is
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
-equivariant, it is enough to show that
E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\prime}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F)=0
for all
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
.

Now if we write
F=\sum_{l}F_{l}\otimes v_{l}^{*}
for some (unique)
F_{l}\in\mathscr{M}_{G}\subset\mathscr{N}_{G}
(i.e.,
F_{l}=\widetilde{F}(v_{l})
), then we have the formulae:

\displaystyle\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\prime}(F)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}\\
\#T=j_{\tau_{0}}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{l,T}(x^{T}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}F_{l})

\displaystyle E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\prime}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}\\
\#T=j_{\tau_{0}}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{l,T}\left(\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial x^{T}}{\partial x_{k}}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(E_{i,k}\star_{\overline{P}}F_{l})\right)

using the fact that
E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}F_{l}=0
because
F_{l}\in\mathscr{M}_{G}
.

With notation as in Theorem
2.2.4
and Convention
2.2.8
, we have a
M_{\mathbf{H}}
-equivariant map

g\colon S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}\to\mathscr{N}_{G}

given by
g(y\otimes z)=\Phi(y)\star_{\mathfrak{u}}\widetilde{F}(z)
on pure tensors. Since
\frac{\partial x^{T}}{\partial x_{k}}
is either zero or a monomial of degree
j_{\tau_{0}}-1
, there exist unique elements
w_{k,T}\in S_{-(j-1)}
such that
\Phi(w_{k,T})=\frac{\partial x^{T}}{\partial x_{k}}
. We consider the elements
A_{i}\in S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}
given by

A_{i}=\sum_{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}\\
\#T=j_{\tau_{0}}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{l,T}\left(\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}w_{k,T}\otimes(E_{i,k}\star_{\mathfrak{m}_{G}}v_{l})\right)

and we see that
g(A_{i})=E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\prime}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F)
(because
E_{i,k}\in\mathfrak{m}_{G}=\operatorname{Lie}M_{\mathbf{G}}
and
\widetilde{F}
is
M_{\mathbf{G}}
-equivariant).

On the other hand, let
W
denote the algebraic representation of
M_{\mathbf{H}}
with trivial highest weight outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and weight
(-1,1,0,\dots,0)
in the
\tau_{0}
-component. If
\{e_{1},\dots,e_{2n}\}
denotes the standard basis of the standard representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
(seen as the
\tau_{0}
-component of
\mathbf{G}
), we have a
M_{\mathbf{H}}
-equivariant map

\displaystyle h\colon W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}_{G}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{*}e_{i}

\displaystyle\mapsto E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\prime}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F).

We define a
k
-linear map
q\colon W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\to S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}
sending
e_{1}^{*}e_{i}
to
A_{i}
. Then clearly we have
g\circ q=h
and the induced map

\bar{q}\colon W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\to(S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa})/\operatorname{ker}(g)

is
M_{\mathbf{H}}
-equivariant. Since the category of algebraic representations of
M_{\mathbf{H}}
is semisimple (as the group is reductive), this gives rise to a
M_{\mathbf{H}}
-equivariant morphism
W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\to S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}
– if we show that any such morphism has to be zero, this will imply that
E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\prime}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F)=0
, as required.

Restricting to the
\tau_{0}
-component and considering the subgroup
1\times 1\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}\subset(M_{\mathbf{H}})_{\tau_{0}}
, it is enough to show that

\operatorname{Hom}_{1\times 1\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}}(\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1},S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa})=0

since
W
is the trivial representation when restricted to this group. But we have already shown this in the proof of Theorem
2.2.4
.
∎

By passing to cohomology and restricting along the open and closed embeddings in Lemma
5.1.8
, we obtain a
k
-linear map

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right).

The following proposition describes the compatibility of these maps as one varies
\beta
. For any
\beta\geq 1
, let
p_{G,\beta}\colon S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta+1})\to S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
and
p_{H,\beta}\colon S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta+1})\to S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
denote the natural finite étale maps.

Proposition 5.3.10
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Then we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta+1}),\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta+1}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{G,\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{H,\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}}

where
\operatorname{Tr}_{\bullet}
denotes the trace maps associated with the corresponding finite étale morphisms.

Proof.

In this proof only, we will add the subscript
\beta
or
\beta+1
to the objects to indicate which level we are working at. Note that
p_{G,\beta}^{*}\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta}=\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta}
and similarly for
\mathbf{H}
, so the trace maps in the statement of the proposition do indeed exist. Furthermore, it is enough to prove the statements over the varieties
X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
and
X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,F^{\operatorname{cl}}}(p^{\beta})
(we will continue to use the notation
p_{G,\beta}
and
p_{H,\beta}
for the natural maps. Recall that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,k}(p^{\beta+1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},k}(p^{\beta+1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit,k}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},k}(p^{\beta})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{H,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}_{\beta+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{G,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}_{\beta}}

is Cartesian.

The claim in the proposition is a local one. Let
U\subset X_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw},k}(p^{\beta})
be an open, and let
V=p_{H,\beta}^{-1}\hat{\iota}_{\beta}^{-1}U=\hat{\iota}_{\beta+1}^{-1}p_{G,\beta}^{-1}U
. We first note that the following diagram is commutative because the above square is Cartesian:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta+1}(p_{G,\beta}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{H,\beta+1}(V)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{H,\beta}(\hat{\iota}_{\beta}^{-1}U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}_{\beta+1}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{G,\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{H,\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}_{\beta}^{*}}

Furthermore, we claim that for any
\phi\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(k^{\oplus 2n-1},k)
, the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta+1}(p_{G,\beta}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta+1}(p_{G,\beta}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\beta}(U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\star_{\mathfrak{u}}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{G,\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{G,\beta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi\star_{\mathfrak{u}}-}

Indeed, it is enough to check this statement after pulling back to
G_{\operatorname{dR}}
, and then the claim follows from the description of
\nabla_{i}
in Lemma
5.3.3
, the fact that the morphisms
p_{G,\beta}
and
p_{H,\beta}
are finite etale, and the fact that the sheaves
\mathscr{N}_{G,\bullet}
arise from the same
G
-equivariant sheaf over the flag variety. The remainder of the proposition now follows from unwinding the definition of
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
in Definition
5.3.7
.
∎

We are now in a position to define the classical evaluation maps. We first recall, from
[
Gra21
, §7]
, how to associate cohomology classes to anticyclotomic algebraic Hecke characters. Let
\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1)
denote the unitary group associated with the one dimensional Hermitian space over
F
. Then we have a morphism

\displaystyle\nu\colon\mathbf{H}

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1)

\displaystyle(h_{1},h_{2})

\displaystyle\mapsto\operatorname{det}h_{2}/\operatorname{det}h_{1}

which is open and surjective on
\mathbb{A}_{f}
-points. We also have a natural map
\mathcal{N}\colon\operatorname{Res}_{F/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1)
given by
\mathcal{N}(z)=\bar{z}/z
.

Definition 5.3.11
.

Let
K_{\beta}\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
denote the level of the Shimura–Deligne variety
S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. We let
\mathfrak{N}_{\beta}
denote the smallest ideal of
\mathcal{O}_{F}
such that
\nu(K_{\beta})\subset\mathcal{N}((\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}+\mathfrak{N}_{\beta}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F})^{\times})
. Note that
\mathfrak{N}_{\beta}=\mathfrak{N}p^{\beta}
for some ideal
\mathfrak{N}\subset\mathcal{O}_{F^{+}}
prime to
p
.

We now introduce the space of anticyclotomic characters that we are interested in. For this, we need to make an additional assumption on the pair of weights
(\kappa,j)
.

Assumption 5.3.12
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. We assume that
\kappa_{0}=0
,
\kappa_{1,\tau_{0}}+\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}=n-1
, and
w\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\kappa_{2,\tau_{0}}+\kappa_{2n,\tau_{0}}=-1
.

For any
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
, let
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}=-w_{M_{H}}^{\operatorname{max}}\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}}
denote the Serre dual of
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}
. By Assumption
5.3.12
,
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}
extends to a character of
\mathbf{H}
.

Definition 5.3.13
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
. We let
\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
denote the set of algebraic Hecke characters
\chi\colon\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}/F^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
satisfying:

(1)

\chi
is anticyclotomic, i.e.,
\chi
is trivial on
\mathbb{A}^{\times}_{F^{+}}
.

(2)

The infinity type of
\chi
is
(j_{\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}},-(j_{\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}))
in the
\tau_{0}
-component and
(j_{\tau},-j_{\tau})
for
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
, i.e.,

\chi(z)=z_{\tau_{0}}^{\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}}}\bar{z}_{\tau_{0}}^{j_{\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}}\cdot\prod_{\tau\in\Psi-\{\tau_{0}\}}z_{\tau}^{-j_{\tau}}\bar{z}_{\tau}^{j_{\tau}}

for all
z=(z_{\tau})\in(\mathbb{R}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}F)^{\times}=\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.

(3)

The conductor of
\chi
divides
\mathfrak{N}_{\beta}
.

We let
\chi^{\prime}\colon(\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1))(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1))(\mathbb{A})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denote unique character such that
\chi=\chi^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{N}
.

For any
\chi\in\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
let
F^{\operatorname{cl}}(\chi)
denote the smallest number field containing
F^{\operatorname{cl}}
and over which the finite part of
\chi
is defined. Then, by the discussion in
[
Gra21
, §7]
, one can associate a cohomology class

[\chi]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}(\chi)},\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)

which, after base-changing to
\mathbb{C}
, coincides with the function

\displaystyle H_{\operatorname{dR}}(\mathbb{C})=\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash X\times\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{C})\times\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{\beta}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{C}

\displaystyle[x,h,h^{\prime}]

\displaystyle\mapsto\xi^{[j]}_{\kappa}(h)\chi^{\prime}(\nu(h^{\prime}))

where
X
denotes the Hermitian space associated with the Shimura–Deligne datum
(\mathbf{H},h_{\mathbf{H}})
, and
\xi_{\kappa}^{[j]}\colon\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{C})=\mathbb{C}^{\times}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\left(\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\mathbb{C})\right)\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denotes the character

(h_{0};h_{1,\tau},h_{2,\tau})\mapsto(\operatorname{det}h_{2,\tau_{0}}/\operatorname{det}h_{1,\tau_{0}})^{j_{\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}}\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}(\operatorname{det}h_{2,\tau}/\operatorname{det}h_{1,\tau})^{j_{\tau}}.

Definition 5.3.14
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
, and suppose that
\chi\in\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
. For any field extension
L/F^{\operatorname{cl}}
contained in
\mathbb{C}
(resp.
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
), let

\operatorname{Ev}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})_{L},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to L(\chi)

denote the
L
-linear morphism given by
\operatorname{Ev}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\eta)=\langle\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}(\eta),[\chi]\rangle
, where
L(\chi)
denotes the compositum of
L
and
F^{\operatorname{cl}}(\chi)
in
\mathbb{C}
(resp. in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
) and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the pairing given by Serre duality.

We have the following compatibility with varying
\beta
.

Proposition 5.3.15
.

With notation as in Definition
5.3.14
(and
\chi\in\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
), we have
\operatorname{Ev}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta+1}=\operatorname{Ev}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{G,\beta}}
.

Proof.

Since
\chi
has conductor dividing
\mathfrak{N}_{\beta}
, we see that
[\chi]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta+1})_{F^{\operatorname{cl}}(\chi)},\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)
is the image of the class at level
\beta
under
p_{H,\beta}^{*}
. The claim now follows from Proposition
5.3.10
.
∎

5.3.3.
Relation with unitary Friedberg–Jacquet periods

In this section we work over
L=\mathbb{C}
, but will often omit this from the notation. In particular, gothic letters will denote the complexification of the Lie algebra of the corresponding group, unless specified otherwise.

Let
K_{\infty}\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{R})
denote the maximal compact-mod-centre subgroup whose complexification is equal to
M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{C})
. Let
K_{\circ}\subset K_{\infty}
denote the maximal compact subgroup, and let
A_{\mathbf{G}}
denote the maximal
\mathbb{Q}
-split torus in the centre of
\mathbf{G}
. Then one can verify that
K_{\infty}=K_{\circ}A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
, where
A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}\subset A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the connected component of the identity. Let
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G}
(resp.
\mathfrak{a}_{G}
) denote the Lie algebra of
P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}
(resp.
A_{\mathbf{G}}
), and set
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\circ}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G}\cap\operatorname{Lie}(\mathbf{G}_{0})
. Then one has
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G}=\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\circ}\oplus\mathfrak{a}_{G}
.

Let
[\mathbf{G}]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A})/A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
, and let
K=K^{p}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
denote the level subgroup for
S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Following
[
Su19
]
, consider the following (right)
K_{\circ}
-torsor

\pi_{\circ}\colon[\mathbf{G}]/K\to S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})(\mathbb{C}).

Let
V
be an algebraic representation of
P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}(\mathbb{C})
on which
A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{C})
acts trivially. Then the automorphic sheaf
\mathcal{V}
on
S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})(\mathbb{C})
associated with
V
satisfies

\mathcal{V}(U)\cong\left(C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes V\right)^{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\circ},K_{\circ})}

where the isomorphism also respects equivariant structures. Here
C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(-)
denotes the space of complex-valued smooth functions on a real-analytic manifold, and
U\subset S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})(\mathbb{C})
is an open subspace. We have a similar description for automorphic vector bundles on
S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})(\mathbb{C})
. In particular, if
W
is an algebraic representation of
P_{\mathbf{H}}^{\operatorname{std}}
on which
A_{\mathbf{H}}(\mathbb{C})=A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{C})
acts trivially, and
\mathcal{W}
denotes the associated sheaf on
S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, then

\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathcal{W}(U)\cong\left(C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes W\right)^{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{h},K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R}))}

where
\hat{\iota}\colon[H]/(\hat{\gamma}K\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\to[G]/K
is the natural map induced from right-translation by
\hat{\gamma}
.

We equip the sheaf
U\mapsto C^{\infty}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)
with an action of
\mathfrak{g}
in the following way. For any
X\in\operatorname{Lie}\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{R})
and
f\in C^{\infty}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)
, we define

(X\star f)(-)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}f(-\cdot\operatorname{exp}(tX))

where
\operatorname{exp}(tX)\in\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{R})
denotes the exponential of
tX
. We then extend this linearly to an action of
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{C})
. This induces an action of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{u}_{G})\cong C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{C}^{\oplus 2n-1},\mathbb{C})
such that the action map is equivariant for
K_{\circ}
(via the action in (
5.3.4
)).

Lemma 5.3.16
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Consider the following
\mathbb{C}
-linear morphism

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\to C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}

defined on pure tensors as:

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}(f\otimes\lambda)=\left.\left[\lambda(\delta_{\kappa,j})\star f\right]\right|_{\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U}

where
f\in C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)
,
\lambda\in V_{\kappa}^{*}
, and
\delta_{\kappa,j}
is defined in Definition
2.2.9
. Then:

(1)

The morphism
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
is
K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant.

(2)

For any
m\geq 0
, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left(C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}^{*}\right)^{K_{\circ}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}^{*}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}^{*}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}^{*}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\otimes\operatorname{pr}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\otimes\operatorname{pr}\otimes\operatorname{id}}

where
\alpha_{i}
satisfies

\langle\alpha_{i}(f\otimes\lambda\otimes\gamma),X\rangle=(X\star f)\otimes\lambda\otimes\gamma\text{ for }\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f\in C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U),\lambda\in V_{\kappa}^{*},\gamma\in\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}^{*},X\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}&\text{ if }i=1\\
f\in C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U),\lambda\in\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]},\gamma\in\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}^{*},X\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}&\text{ if }i=2\end{array}\right.

and
\operatorname{pr}\colon\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}^{*}\to\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}^{*}
is the map induced from the dual of the inclusion
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
.

(3)

The morphism
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
induces a morphism

\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)\cong\left(C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\right)^{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\circ},K_{\circ})}\to\left(C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\right)^{(\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{h},K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R}))}\cong\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U)

which coincides with the morphism in Proposition
5.3.8
.

Proof.

The
K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariance is clear. We claim that part (2) follows from the same argument as in the proof of Proposition
5.3.8
. Indeed, let
F\in{\left(C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}^{*}\right)^{K_{\circ}}}
and consider the element:

F^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left[\alpha_{2}\circ(\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\otimes\operatorname{pr})\right](F)-\left[(\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\otimes\operatorname{pr}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\alpha_{1}\right](F)

which is fixed by the action of
K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})
. This is therefore equivalent to a
K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant morphism:

F^{\prime}\colon\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\to C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)

which we wish to show is zero. Consider the elementary matrices
E_{i,1}\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
with
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
, which form a basis of this Lie algebra. For any such elementary matrix
E_{i,1}
(with
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
) and any polynomial
Q\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{C}^{\oplus 2n-1},\mathbb{C})
in the last
n
coordinates
X_{n+1},\dots,X_{2n}
of
\mathbb{C}^{\oplus 2n-1}
, we have

E_{i,1}\star(Q\star f)=Q\star(E_{i,1}\star f)+\sum_{j=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial Q}{\partial X_{j}}\star(E_{i,j}\star f)

for any
f\in C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)
. Fix a basis
\{v_{l}\}
of
V_{\kappa}
and let
\{v_{l}^{*}\}
denote the dual basis. By viewing
F
as a
K_{\circ}
-equivariant morphism
V_{\kappa}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\to C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)
, we see that the morphism
F^{\prime}
satisfies

\displaystyle F^{\prime}(1\otimes\gamma\otimes E_{i,1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\left.\left[E_{i,1}\star v_{l}^{*}(\delta_{\kappa,j})\star F(v_{l}\otimes\gamma)-v_{l}^{*}(\delta_{\kappa,j})\star E_{i,1}\star F(v_{l}\otimes\gamma)\right]\right|_{\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l}\sum_{j=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial v_{l}^{*}(\delta_{\kappa,j})}{\partial X_{j}}\star(E_{i,j}\star F(v_{l}\otimes\gamma))|_{\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U}

where
\gamma\in\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
(note that
\gamma
is killed by
E_{i,j}
for
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
and
j\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
). We can define a
K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})
-equivariant map

F^{\prime\prime}\colon V_{\kappa}\otimes\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\otimes S_{-(j-1)}\to C^{\operatorname{\infty}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}U)

such that the image of
F^{\prime}
is contained in the image of
F^{\prime\prime}
. Then, by the same argument as in Proposition
5.3.8
, one can then show that the obstruction to inducing a morphism
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
is given by an element in

\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}\otimes\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H},V_{\kappa}\otimes S_{-(j-1)}\right).

But this hom-space is trivial for the same reasons as in Proposition
5.3.8
. This also implies that the morphism
F^{\prime}
is zero, and hence completes the proof of part (2).

It remains to show that the induced morphism in part (3) coincides with the one in Proposition
5.3.8
. But by the calculations in §
5.2
, the action of
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{C}^{\oplus 2n-1},\mathbb{C})
is induced from the action of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}
in Lemma
5.2.6
(denoted
\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
). Indeed, let
\pi_{\mathbb{C}}\colon\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{C})/P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}(\mathbb{C})
denote the natural
P_{\mathbf{G}}^{\operatorname{std}}(\mathbb{C})
-torsor. Then, from Lemma
5.2.6
, the action of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}
on
\pi_{\mathbb{C},*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{C})}
satisfies
X\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}f=X\star_{r}f
(with
X\in\mathfrak{u}_{G}
and
f\in\pi_{\mathbb{C},*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{C})}
), where
\star_{r}
denotes the action induced from right-translation of the argument. One then sees that the action of
\mathfrak{u}_{G}
on
\pi_{\circ,*}C^{\infty}(-)
(defined just before this lemma) extends
\widetilde{\star}_{\mathcal{D}}
. The compatibility between the two morphisms now follows.
∎

For any
m\geq 0
, let
\iota\colon\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\hookrightarrow\bigwedge^{m}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
denote the natural map induced from
\mathbf{H}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{G}
.

Lemma 5.3.17
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Then
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
induces a morphism of Chevalley-Eilenberg complexes:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{\circ}}\left(\wedge^{\bullet}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G},C^{\infty}(\pi_{\circ}^{-1}-)\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\right)

\displaystyle\to\operatorname{Hom}_{K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})}\left(\wedge^{\bullet}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H},C^{\infty}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}\pi_{\circ}^{-1}-)\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\right)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\circ f\circ\iota

and, after passing to cohomology, coincides with the map

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{H},\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right)

from §
5.3.2
via the identifications in
[
Su19
, §1.1]
.

Proof.

Noting that
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
are abelian, this follows from Lemma
5.3.16
.
∎

Finally, we can relate the evaluation maps above to unitary Friedberg–Jacquet periods. Set
[\mathbf{H}]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A})/A_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{R})^{\circ}
(note that
A_{\mathbf{G}}\subset\mathbf{H}
). Let
dh
denote the Tamagawa measure on
[\mathbf{H}]
. Fix bases
\alpha_{+}
and
\alpha_{-}
of
\bigwedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{u}_{H}
and
\bigwedge^{n-1}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
respectively, and recall the definition of
\Delta_{\kappa}^{[j]}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
from Definition
A.1.5
.

Proposition 5.3.18
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and
\chi\in\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
. Let
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{n-1}\left(S_{\mathbf{G},\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)
, which we view as a
K_{\circ}
-equivariant morphism
F\colon\bigwedge^{n-1}\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}\to C^{\infty}([\mathbf{G}]/K)
. Set
\phi=F(\iota(\alpha_{-})\otimes v_{\kappa}^{[0]})
. Then

\operatorname{Ev}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\eta)=(2\pi i)^{-(n-1)}\operatorname{Vol}(K_{H,\beta};dh)\int_{[\mathbf{H}]}\left(\Delta_{\kappa}^{[j]}\cdot\phi\right)(h\hat{\gamma})\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\operatorname{det}h_{2}}{\operatorname{det}h_{1}}\right)dh

where we write
h=(h_{1},h_{2})
for the components preserving the corresponding factor in the decomposition
W=W_{1}\oplus W_{2}
, we set
K_{H,\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(K_{\circ}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{R})\right)\cdot\left(K^{p}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\right)\cdot K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, and we view
\hat{\gamma}\in\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.3.16
, Lemma
5.3.17
, and the definition of
\Delta_{\kappa}^{[j]}
, we have

\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F)(\alpha_{-})=\left.\left[\Delta^{[j]}_{\kappa}\cdot F(\iota(\alpha_{-})\otimes v_{\kappa}^{[0]})\right]\right|_{[\mathbf{H}]/(\hat{\gamma}K\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))}

where the restriction is via the map
\hat{\iota}
. Here we have used the fact that
\iota(\alpha_{-})
is killed by any
E_{1,k,\tau_{0}}
for
k\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
. Similarly, we can view
[\chi]
as a homomorphism

[\chi]\colon\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\otimes\bigwedge^{n-1}\mathfrak{u}_{H}\to C^{\infty}([\mathbf{H}]/(\hat{\gamma}K\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})))

which satisfies
[\chi](\alpha_{+})(h)=\chi^{\prime}(\operatorname{det}h_{2}/\operatorname{det}h_{1})
. The cup product
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F)\smile[\chi]
then corresponds to the
K_{H,\beta}
-invariant volume form

h\mapsto\left(\Delta_{\kappa}^{[j]}\cdot\phi\right)(h\hat{\gamma})\chi^{\prime}\left(\frac{\operatorname{det}h_{2}}{\operatorname{det}h_{1}}\right)\alpha_{+}^{*}\wedge\alpha_{-}^{*}.

The result now follows from
[
Har90b
, Proposition 3.8]
.
∎

6.
The
p
-adic theory

In the section we describe the
p
-adic analogue of the previous section. Recall we have fixed a prime
p
which splits completely in
F/\mathbb{Q}
(see Assumption
5.1.1
).

6.1.
Igusa varieties

To be able to define the appropriate ordinary strata in the Shimura–Deligne varieties we consider, we need to introduce certain Igusa varieties. Let
\mathcal{X}_{G}
and
\mathcal{X}_{H}
denote the adic spaces over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
associated with
X_{\mathbf{G},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
and
X_{\mathbf{H},\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, where we have base-changed along the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}},(p)}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}
induced from the fixed embedding
F\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
induced by
\tau_{0}
. Let
\mathfrak{X}_{G}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{G}
denote the formal completions of
X_{\mathbf{G},\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
and
X_{\mathbf{H},\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
respectively along the special fibre, where we have base-changed along the morphism
\mathcal{O}_{F^{\operatorname{cl}},(p)}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}
induced by
\tau_{0}
. Since
X_{\mathbf{G}}
and
X_{\mathbf{H}}
are proper, the adic generic fibres of
\mathfrak{X}_{G}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H}
are precisely
\mathcal{X}_{G}
and
\mathcal{X}_{H}
.

6.1.1.
Caraiani–Scholze Igusa varieties

We now introduce the Igusa varieties considered in
[
CS17
, §4.3]
. For
\tau\in\Psi
, consider the following
p
-divisible group over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
:

\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\mu_{p^{\infty}}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus 2n-1}&\tau=\tau_{0}\\
(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus 2n}&\tau\neq\tau_{0}\end{array}\right.

For any
\tau\in\Psi
, we have a decomposition
\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}=\mathbb{X}_{1,\tau}\oplus\mathbb{X}_{2,\tau}
, where
\mathbb{X}_{1,\tau_{0}}=\mu_{p^{\infty}}\oplus(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus n-1}
and
\mathbb{X}_{i,\tau}=(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus n}
for either
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
and
i=1,2
, or
(i,\tau)=(2,\tau_{0})
. We also let
\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}=\varprojlim_{\times p}\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}
and
\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{i,\tau}=\varprojlim_{\times p}\mathbb{X}_{i,\tau}
denote the universal covers, where the inverse limit is over multiplication by
p
. Let
\operatorname{Nilp}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
denote the category of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebras on which
p
is nilpotent.

Definition 6.1.1
.

For
\tau\in\Psi
, let
J_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
and
J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
denote the fpqc sheaves on
\operatorname{Nilp}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\operatorname{op}}
given by

J_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}(R)=\operatorname{Aut}(\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau,R}),\quad\quad J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}(R)=\operatorname{Aut}(\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau,R}).

We set
J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}=\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}J_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
and
J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
. Let
J_{H,\operatorname{ord},\tau}\subset J_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
(resp.
J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord},\tau}\subset J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
) denote the sub-sheaves preserving the decomposition
\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}=\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{1,\tau}\oplus\widetilde{\mathbb{X}}_{2,\tau}
(resp.
\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}=\mathbb{X}_{1,\tau}\oplus\mathbb{X}_{2,\tau}
). We set
J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}=\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}J_{H,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
and
J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord}}=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord},\tau}
.

Remark 6.1.2
.

Concretely, we have the following descriptions

J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}=\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}\times\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\underline{\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}}&\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}^{\oplus 2n-1}\\
&\underline{\operatorname{GL}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\end{array}\right)\times\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}\underline{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}

and

J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}=\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}\times\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}&T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}^{\oplus 2n-1}\\
&\underline{\operatorname{GL}_{2n-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}\end{array}\right)\times\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}\underline{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}

where
T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}
denotes the Tate module. The first factor will correspond to the similitude factor (see Definition
6.1.3
and Remark
6.1.4
).

Let
\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H}^{\operatorname{ord}}
denote the ordinary loci in
\mathfrak{X}_{G}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H}
respectively. We now introduce the Igusa varieties:

Definition 6.1.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
denote the functor on
\operatorname{Nilp}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}^{\operatorname{op}}
given by

\mathfrak{IG}_{G}(R)=\left\{(A,\lambda,i,\eta^{p},s,f_{\tau}):\begin{array}[]{c}(A,\lambda,i,\eta^{p})\in\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}(R),s\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}(R),\\
f_{\tau}\colon\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau,R}\xrightarrow{\sim}A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\infty}]\end{array}\right\}.

Similarly, we let
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}\to\mathfrak{X}_{H}^{\operatorname{ord}}
denote the functor on
\operatorname{Nilp}^{\operatorname{op}}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}
such that
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}(R)
consists of tuples
(A_{1},A_{2},\lambda,i,\eta^{p},s,f_{\tau})
with
(A_{1},A_{2},\lambda,i,\eta^{p})\in\mathfrak{X}_{H}^{\operatorname{ord}}(R)
,
s\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}(R)
, and
f_{\tau}\colon\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau,R}\xrightarrow{\sim}A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\infty}]
are isomorphisms preserving the decompositions
\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau}=\mathbb{X}_{1,\tau}\oplus\mathbb{X}_{2,\tau}
and
A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\infty}]=A_{1}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\infty}]\oplus A_{2}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\infty}]
.

Remark 6.1.4
.

We note that the moduli problem
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}
is equivalent to the one parameterising trivialisations of
A[p^{\infty}]
respecting the endomorphism and symplectic structure up to similitude. This is why we have included the data
s\in\underline{\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}(R)
in the above moduli problem. The same is true for
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}
.

We have the following properties of these functors.

Proposition 6.1.5
.

We have:

(1)

\mathfrak{IG}_{G}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}
are representable by flat affine
p
-adic formal schemes over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

(2)

\mathfrak{IG}_{G}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}\to\mathfrak{X}_{H}^{\operatorname{ord}}
are fpqc torsors under the groups
J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
and
J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
respectively.

(3)

The action of
J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
(resp.
J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
) on
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}
(resp.
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}
) extends to an action of
J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
(resp.
J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
).

Proof.

Parts (1) and (3) follow from
[
CS17
, p.718]
and
[
CS17
, Corollary 4.3.5]
respectively. Part (2) follows from the same proof as in
[
How20
, Lemma 5.1.1]
(the cover
\mathfrak{IG}_{\bullet}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{ord}}
is pro-finite-flat, hence fpqc).
∎

6.1.2.
Quotients of Igusa varieties

We recall some notation from
[
Gra21
]
. Let
P^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})=w_{n}K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})w_{n}^{-1}\cap P_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and let
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
denote its image under the map
P_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to M_{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Let
N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\subset J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
denote the sub fpqc sheaf given by

N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})=\underline{\{1\}}\times\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\underline{\{1\}}&(p^{\beta}T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}})^{\oplus n}\oplus T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}^{\oplus n-1}\\
&\underline{\{1\}}\end{array}\right)\times\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}\underline{\{1\}}.

and set
J^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})=N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\rtimes\underline{M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}\subset J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
. Here
\{1\}
denotes the trivial group. Also, set
P^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})=K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\cap P_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
its image under the map
P_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to M_{H}(p^{\beta})
. Let
N^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})=N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\cap J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
and
J^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})=N^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\rtimes\underline{M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})}\subset J^{+}_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
.

Definition 6.1.6
.

We introduce the following quotients:

(1)

Let
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
) denote the flat
p
-adic formal scheme obtained as the quotient of
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}
by
N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
J^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
). The map
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is a proétale
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
-torsor.

(2)

Let
\mathfrak{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
) denote the flat
p
-adic formal scheme obtained as the quotient of
\mathfrak{IG}_{H}
by
N^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
(resp.
J^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
). The map
\mathfrak{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
is a proétale
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-torsor.

We have the following important properties of these quotients.

Proposition 6.1.7
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
.

(1)

The natural maps
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{X}_{H}^{\operatorname{ord}}
are finite flat.

(2)

\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
are smooth
p
-adic formal schemes over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

(3)

If we let
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
denote the adic generic fibres of
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
, then
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
are integrally closed in
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
respectively, in the sense of
[
PS16
, §1.1]
.

Proof.

Part (1) is clear because
\mathfrak{IG}_{\bullet}\to\mathfrak{X}_{\bullet}^{\operatorname{ord}}
are pro-finite-flat torsors. Part (3) follows from part (2). Indeed, suppose that
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
is smooth. Then
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is reduced, hence we are in the setting of
[
PS16
, §1.1]
. Suppose that
\mathfrak{U}=\operatorname{Spf}R\subset\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is an open affine subspace with
R
a smooth
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra. Note that
R
is
p
-torsion free. We want to show that
R
is integrally closed in
R[1/p]
. It suffices to check this after localising at open primes ideals of
R
, i.e. if
\mathfrak{p}\subset R
is a prime ideal containing
p
, then we need to show that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is integrally closed in
R_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/p]
. But since
R/p
is a smooth
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra,
R_{\mathfrak{p}}/p
is regular, and since
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is
p
-torsion free,
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is regular and hence normal. Here we have used the fact that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a flat
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebra, hence
\operatorname{dim}R_{\mathfrak{p}}=1+\operatorname{dim}(R_{\mathfrak{p}}/p)
. This implies that
R_{\mathfrak{p}}
is integrally closed in
R_{\mathfrak{p}}[1/p]
. Therefore it remains to prove part (2).

Let
\mathfrak{Y}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
denote the moduli space parameterising subgroup schemes
C\subset A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]
which are etale locally isomorphic to
\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus n}
. For any such subgroup
C
, let
C^{\prime}=C\oplus C^{\perp}\subset A[p^{\beta}]
where
C^{\perp}
is the orthogonal complement of
C
under the symplectic pairing
A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]\times A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}^{c}}^{\beta}]\to\mu_{p^{\beta}}
induced from the polarisation and Weil pairing. Then
C^{\prime}
is an
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-stable totally isotropic finite flat subgroup, hence
A/C^{\prime}
is naturally a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme with an induced prime-to-
p
level structure.

We claim that
\mathfrak{Y}
is a smooth
p
-adic formal scheme over
\operatorname{Spf}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. To show this, is enough to prove that the finite flat map
q\colon\mathfrak{Y}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
given by
q(A,C)=A/C^{\prime}
is smooth (since
\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}
is smooth). Let
T=\operatorname{Spec}A
be an affine scheme with
A
a
\mathbb{Z}/p^{r}\mathbb{Z}
-algebra (for any
r\geq 1
) and let
J\subset A
be a square-zero ideal. Let
T_{0}=\operatorname{Spec}A/J
. We need to show that for any point
(A,C)\in\mathfrak{Y}(T_{0})
and any deformation
B\in\mathfrak{X}_{G}^{\operatorname{ord}}(T)
such that
B|_{T_{0}}=A/C^{\prime}
, there exists a point
(\widetilde{B},\widetilde{C})\in\mathfrak{Y}(T)
such that
\widetilde{B}/\widetilde{C}^{\prime}=B
.

Let
K=\overline{A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]}
denote the image of
A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]
in
A/C^{\prime}
(which is a finite flat group scheme etale locally isomorphic to
\mu_{p^{\beta}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus n-1}
). Then we have a short exact sequence

(6.1.8)

0\to K\to(A/C^{\prime})[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]\to L\to 0

where
L
is finite flat etale locally isomorphic to
\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)^{\oplus n}
. By Illusie’s deformation theory
[
Ill71
]
and since etale group schemes deform uniquely, there exists a finite flat group scheme
\mathcal{L}
etale locally isomorphic to
\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus n}
such that
\mathcal{L}|_{T_{0}}=L
, and a morphism
B[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{L}
deforming the right-hand map in (
6.1.8
). Let
\mathcal{K}
denote the kernel of the map
B[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}]\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{L}
. Then the required point is given by
\widetilde{B}=B/\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{C}
is the image of
B[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]
under
B\to\widetilde{B}
. This proves that
\mathfrak{Y}
is smooth.

For
i=2,\dots,n+1
, let
e_{i}\in\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau_{0}}[p^{\beta}]=\mu_{p^{\beta}}\oplus\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus 2n-1}
denote the basis vector of the
\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}
-factor in the
i
-th place. Let
C_{\operatorname{std}}\subset\mathbb{X}_{\operatorname{ord},\tau_{0}}[p^{\beta}]
denote the finite flat subgroup scheme generated by
\{e_{i}:i=2,\dots,n+1\}
. We have a natural map
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}\to\mathfrak{Y}
given by sending
(A,\lambda,i,\eta^{p},s,f_{\tau})
to
(A,C)
(which the induced extra structure) where
C=f_{\tau_{0}}(C_{\operatorname{std}})
. This is a fpqc torsor for a subgroup of the form
N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\rtimes\underline{M}\subset J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
, for some proetale subgroup scheme
\underline{M}\subset J^{+}_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
. We have a factorisation

\mathfrak{IG}_{G}\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{Y}.

Since the map
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}\to\mathfrak{Y}
is a proetale
M
-torsor, we see that
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{Y}
is finite etale. Since we have already shown
\mathfrak{Y}
is smooth, this implies that
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is smooth.

The proof for
\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
is very similar, using the moduli space
\mathfrak{Y}\to\mathfrak{X}_{H}^{\operatorname{ord}}
parameterising finite flat subgroup schemes
C\subset A_{1}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]
which are etale locally isomorphic to
\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus n-1}
.
∎

Let
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
denote the
p
-adic completion of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\infty}})
with ring of integers
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
, and fix a basis
\varepsilon\in T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
. Let
u\in M_{\mathbf{G}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the element in
[
Gra21
, Definition 2.4.2]
. Then we consider the following element
\gamma=1\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\gamma_{\tau}\in J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
, where
\gamma_{\tau}=u_{\tau}
for
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
and

\gamma_{\tau_{0}}=u_{\tau_{0}}\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&x_{\tau_{0}}\\
&1\end{array}\right)

where
x_{\tau_{0}}\in(T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}))^{\oplus 2n-1}
is the
(1\times 2n-1)
-matrix whose
n
-th entry is
\varepsilon\in T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
and the rest are
1\in T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
. Then we have a morphism

\mathfrak{IG}_{H,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}\xrightarrow{\cdot\gamma}\mathfrak{IG}_{G,\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}}

given by right-translation by
\gamma
. If we let
\operatorname{Unip}(J_{H,\operatorname{ord}})\cong\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}^{\oplus n-1}
denote the unipotent part of
J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
, then this morphism is equivariant for the actions of
\operatorname{Unip}(J_{H,\operatorname{ord}})
on both sides. Furthermore, we have
N_{\diamondsuit}^{H}(p^{\beta})=\gamma N^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\gamma^{-1}\cap J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
and
J_{\diamondsuit}^{H}(p^{\beta})=\gamma J_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{G}(p^{\beta})\gamma^{-1}\cap J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
, so we obtain induced morphisms

\hat{\iota}\colon\mathfrak{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta}),\quad\quad\hat{\iota}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})

Both of these morphisms only depend on the image of
\varepsilon
in
\mu_{p^{\beta}}
, hence descend to morphisms over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}[\mu_{p^{\beta}}]
. The reason for the notation
\hat{\iota}
will be explained in Proposition
6.2.13
.

6.1.3.
Differential operators

We now explain how one obtains a unipotent action on the Igusa towers
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
following the strategy in
[
How20
]
.

Let
\operatorname{Unip}(J_{G,\operatorname{ord}})
(resp.
\operatorname{Unip}(J_{H,\operatorname{ord}})
) denote the unipotent subgroup of
J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
(resp.
J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}
). Then we have

\operatorname{Unip}(J_{G,\operatorname{ord}})/N_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{G}(p^{\beta})\cong\left(\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}/p^{\beta}T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}\right)^{\oplus n}\oplus\left(\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}/T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}\right)^{\oplus n-1}

and

\operatorname{Unip}(J_{H,\operatorname{ord}})/N_{\diamondsuit}^{H}(p^{\beta})\cong\left(\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}/p^{\beta}T_{p}\mu_{p^{\infty}}\right)^{\oplus n-1}

both of which are isomorphic to copies of the formal torus
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

Definition 6.1.9
.

Let
U_{G,\beta}=(p^{-\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus n-1}
and
U_{H,\beta}=(p^{-\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus n-1}
. We view
U_{H,\beta}\subset U_{G,\beta}
by including in the first
n-1
factors. Let
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(U_{\bullet,\beta},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the algebra of continuous functions
U_{\bullet,\beta}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

By
p
-adic Fourier theory, we have identifications of
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-algebras

\displaystyle C_{\operatorname{cont}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
