and
H
. We fix a choice of
p^{\beta}
-root of unity in
L
.

Lemma 6.4.11
.

We have the following commutative cube:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}}

with the bottom square Cartesian. Here
\hat{\iota}\colon\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is the morphism defined at the end of §
6.1.2
, and
\hat{\iota}\colon P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
is the composition

P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\xrightarrow{u}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}

where the first map is the natural one and the second map is the action of
u\in\overline{P}_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}
through the torsor structure. The front and back squares provide reduction of structures, i.e.

\hat{\iota}^{*}\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})=\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\times^{[M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),u]}M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\quad\hat{\iota}^{*}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}=P_{H,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times^{[\overline{P}_{H}^{\operatorname{an}},u]}\overline{P}_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}

using the inclusions
u^{-1}M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})u\subset M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
and
u^{-1}\overline{P}_{H}^{\operatorname{an}}u\subset\overline{P}_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}
. We have a similar cube replacing
P_{\bullet,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
with
M_{\bullet,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Proof.

This follows immediately from the definitions.
∎

The above lemma implies that we have natural maps of ind-sheaves
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
and
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{H}
both of which are
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant via the inclusion
u^{-1}M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})u\subset M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Note that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{N}_{G}^{\dagger}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}

where the top map is the action of
u
followed by the natural map
\mathscr{N}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}_{H}
in Lemma
5.3.6
. Furthemore, the map
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
is equivariant for the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}
through the inclusion
\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{H}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{G}
. Finally, we note that the map
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\to\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
is equivariant for the action of
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus n-1},L)\subset C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)
because this subspace of polynomial functions is fixed (pointwise) by the action of
u
.

7.
p
-adic iteration of differential operators

The goal of this section is to show that the actions of differential operators on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
and
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}
can be
p
-adically iterated. We continue to keep the same conventions as in the previous section. In particular, we fix an integer
\beta\geq 1
and work over a finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
containing
\mu_{p^{\beta}}
. Recall the definitions of
U_{G,\beta}
and
U_{H,\beta}
from Definition
6.1.9
. We let
C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{\bullet,\beta},L)
denote the
L
-algebra of locally analytic functions
U_{\bullet,\beta}\to L
. For a real number
\varepsilon>0
, let
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\subset C_{\operatorname{cont}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
denote the subalgebra of continuous functions

(7.0.1)

f(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n-1})=\sum_{\underline{k}}a_{\underline{k}}\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{p^{\beta_1} x_1}{k_1}\cdots\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{p^{\beta_{2n-1}} x_{2n-1}}{k_{2n-1}},\quad\quad(x_{1},\dots,x_{2n-1})\in U_{G,\beta}

which satisfy
p^{(k_{1}+\cdots+k_{2n-1})\varepsilon}|a_{\underline{k}}|\to 0
as
k_{1}+\cdots+k_{2n-1}\to+\infty
, where the sum runs over all tuples of integers
\underline{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{2n-1})
with
k_{i}\geq 0
for all
i=1,\dots,2n-1
. Here (
7.0.1
) is the multivariable Mahler expansion of a continuous function
U_{G,\beta}\to L
. Note that, via the product map, we can view

C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\otimes_{L}\cdots\otimes_{L}C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{2n-1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\subset C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)

and this subspace is dense. We define
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{H,\beta},L)
similarly.

The main theorem that we wish to prove is the following:

Theorem 7.0.2
.

Recall the definitions of
\mathcal{C}_{G}
and
\mathcal{C}_{H}
from Definition
6.2.16
. Let
\varepsilon>0
.

(1)

If
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
, then there exists a continuous
L
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)

uniquely extending the action of
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)
. This action map is equivariant for the action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, where the action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
on
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
is given by the same formula as in (
5.3.4
). This action is functorial in
U
and
\varepsilon
.

(2)

If
U\in\mathcal{C}_{H}
, then there exists a continuous
L
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{H,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(U)

uniquely extending the action of
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus n-1},L)
. This action map is equivariant for the action of
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. This action is functorial in
U
and
\varepsilon
.

(3)

If
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
, the pullback map
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)
is equivariant for the action of
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{H,\beta},L)\subset C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
(note that
\hat{\iota}^{-1}U\in\mathcal{C}_{H}
).

The proof of this theorem will occupy §
7.1
–
7.3
following the strategy in
[
GPR23
]
(supplemented with the results in §
3
). After this we will describe the construction of the
p
-adic evaluation maps which will give rise to the
p
-adic
L
-function.

7.1.
Ordinary explicit strict neighbourhoods for
G

In this section, we fix
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
. To ease notation, for any integer
k\geq 1
, we let
\mathscr{G}_{k}
denote the image of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
under the map
M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to M_{G}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
. We have a decomposition
\mathscr{G}_{k}=\mathscr{G}_{k,\operatorname{sim}}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathscr{G}_{k,\tau}
(where “
\operatorname{sim}
” stands for similitude), where

\mathscr{G}_{k,\operatorname{sim}}=\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times},\quad\mathscr{G}_{k,\tau_{0}}=\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}\times\operatorname{Iw}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2n-1}}(p^{\beta})_{\operatorname{mod}p^{k}},\quad\mathscr{G}_{k,\tau}=\operatorname{Iw}_{\operatorname{GL}_{2n}}(p^{\beta})_{\operatorname{mod}p^{k}}\;\;\;(\tau\neq\tau_{0}).

Here
\operatorname{Iw}_{\operatorname{GL}_{d}}(p^{\beta})_{\operatorname{mod}p^{k}}
denotes the image of the depth
p^{\beta}
upper-triangular Iwahori subgroup of
\operatorname{GL}_{d}
under the map
\operatorname{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\operatorname{GL}_{d}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z})
, and recall that
M_{G}=\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\left(\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2n-1}\right)\times\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
.

We have a tower of finite étale torsors

\cdots\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}\to\cdots\to\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{1}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})

where
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}\to\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is a torsor for the group
\mathscr{G}_{k}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})=\varprojlim_{k}\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
.

Notation 7.1.1
.

We let
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord}},A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord}})
denote the pullback of
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
to
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
, which is the adic generic fibre of an open
\operatorname{Spf}(A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord}})\subset\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
by assumption. We let
\operatorname{Spf}(A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},k})
and
\operatorname{Spf}(A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\infty})
denote the pullbacks of
\operatorname{Spf}(A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord}})
to
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
and
\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
respectively.

We fix a generator
h
of
\hat{\delta}^{+}_{G,n+1}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
, so in particular
A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord}}=A^{+}\langle 1/h\rangle
. We also have that
A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
is the
p
-adic completion of
\varinjlim_{k}A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},k}
.

We set
A_{\operatorname{ord},k}=A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},k}[1/p]
(
k\leq+\infty
) so in particular
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k},A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})
is the pullback of
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
to
\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
.

7.1.1.
Canonical bases

We now fix several bases of the relative de Rham homology of the universal
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme, respecting the Hodge filtration.

Notation 7.1.2
.

For
\tau\in\Psi
, fix bases
\{e_{1,\tau},\dots,e_{2n,\tau}\}
and
\{e_{2n,\bar{\tau}},\dots,e_{1,\bar{\tau}}\}
of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\tau}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\bar{\tau}}
over
\operatorname{Spf}A^{+}
respectively, preserving the Hodge filtrations and satisfying the property
\langle e_{i,\tau},e_{j,\bar{\tau}}\rangle=\delta_{ij}
.
5
5

5

Here
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta function.
In particular, this defines a section of
P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\to\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
.

We also have canonical bases over the layers of the Igusa tower. Indeed, let
k\geq 1
be an integer, then from the universal object over
\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})\subset\mathfrak{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
, we obtain the data
(s,f^{(1)}_{\tau_{0}},f^{(2)}_{\tau_{0}},f_{\tau}\;(\tau\neq\tau_{0}))
where
s\in\underline{\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})
and we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle f^{(1)}_{\tau_{0}}\colon\mu_{p^{k}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{k}]^{\circ}

\displaystyle f^{(2)}_{\tau_{0}}\colon\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus 2n-1}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{k}]/\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{k}]^{\circ}

\displaystyle f_{\tau}\colon\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus 2n}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{k}]

over
\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})
, where
(-)^{\circ}
denote the connected component of a finite flat group scheme. The isomorphisms are compatible with the flags
\mathcal{C}_{\bullet,\bullet}
of finite flat groups schemes in the universal
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}
over
\operatorname{Spf}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})
.

We introduce the following basis elements:

•

Let
e_{1,\tau_{0},k}^{\operatorname{can}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\tau_{0}}/p^{k}
denote the image of

\operatorname{Lie}(f_{\tau_{0}}^{(1)})(t\partial_{t})\in\operatorname{Lie}(\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{k}]^{\circ})=\operatorname{Lie}(\mathcal{A})_{\tau_{0}}/p^{k}

under the unit root splitting, where
t\partial_{t}\in\operatorname{Lie}\mu_{p^{k}}
denotes the canonical tangent vector.

•

For
i=2,\dots,2n
, let
e_{i,\tau_{0},k}^{\operatorname{can}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\tau_{0}}
denote the element

\operatorname{dlog}\circ f^{(2)}_{\tau_{0}}(0,\dots,0,1,0,\dots,0)\in\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau_{0}}/p^{k}\subset\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\tau_{0}}/p^{k}

where
1
is in the
(i-1)
-th place.

•

For
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
and
i=1,\dots,2n
, let
e_{i,\tau,k}^{\operatorname{can}}
denote the element

\operatorname{dlog}\circ f_{\tau}(0,\dots,0,1,0,\dots,0)\in\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau}/p^{k}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\tau}/p^{k}

where the
1
is in the
i
-th place.

•

For any
i=1,\dots,2n
and
\tau\in\Psi
, let
e_{i,\bar{\tau},k}^{\operatorname{can}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\bar{\tau}}
denote the unique element such that
\langle e_{i,\tau,k}^{\operatorname{can}},e_{j,\bar{\tau},k}^{\operatorname{can}}\rangle=s\delta_{ij}
.

Definition 7.1.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{B}^{\operatorname{can}}_{k}
denote the basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}/p^{k}
over
\operatorname{Spf}A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+}
given by

\bigcup_{\tau\in\Psi}\{e_{1,\tau,k}^{\operatorname{can}},\dots,e_{2n,\tau,k}^{\operatorname{can}}\}\cup\{e_{2n,\bar{\tau},k}^{\operatorname{can}},\dots,e_{1,\bar{\tau},k}^{\operatorname{can}}\}.

This respects the Hodge filtration and symplectic structure. If
k=+\infty
, then we can define a basis

\mathfrak{B}^{\operatorname{can}}_{\infty}=\bigcup_{\tau\in\Psi}\{e_{1,\tau,\infty}^{\operatorname{can}},\dots,e_{2n,\tau,\infty}^{\operatorname{can}}\}\cup\{e_{2n,\bar{\tau},\infty}^{\operatorname{can}},\dots,e_{1,\bar{\tau},\infty}^{\operatorname{can}}\}

of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
over
\operatorname{Spf}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
in a similar way as above, by replacing the finite flat groups schemes by their respective
p
-divisible groups.

7.1.2.
Reductions of structure over the ordinary locus

We now construct certain reductions of structure of
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
over
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
. Let
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\subset\overline{P}_{G}
denote the formal subgroup scheme of elements which are congruent to the identity modulo
p^{k}
. By abuse of notation, we will also denote its adic generic fibre by
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}
.

Definition 7.1.4
.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer. Let
\mathfrak{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\to\operatorname{Spf}A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+}
denote the functor

\mathfrak{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}(S\xrightarrow{f}\operatorname{Spf}A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ bases of }f^{*}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\text{ respecting the Hodge filtration and endomorphism }\\
\text{ and symplectic structure which are congruent to }\mathfrak{B}_{k}^{\operatorname{can}}\text{ modulo }p^{k}\end{array}\right\}.

This defines an analytic torsor under the group
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}
. Furthermore,
\mathfrak{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\to\operatorname{Spf}A_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+}
is an étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,k}=\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\cdot M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
-torsor. If
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\to\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord}},A_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
denotes its adic generic fibre, then this defines a reduction of structure of
P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR},A_{\operatorname{ord}}}=P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR}}\times_{\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})}\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord}},A_{\operatorname{ord}}^{+})
.

Clearly one has a natural map
\operatorname{Spf}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\to\mathfrak{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
given by the canonical basis
\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}^{\operatorname{can}}
, and the collection
\{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\}_{k\geq 1}
defines a cofinal collection of quasi-compact open subspaces of
P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR},A_{\operatorname{ord}}}
containing the closure of
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})
in
P^{\operatorname{an}}_{G,\operatorname{dR},A_{\operatorname{ord}}}
.

Lemma 7.1.5
.

One has

\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k},A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})}\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord,\infty}},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})\cong\operatorname{Spa}\left(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\rangle,A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\rangle\right)

where
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\rangle
(resp.
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\rangle
) denotes global sections of the group
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}
over
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
(resp.
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
), i.e. convergent power series on
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}
.

Proof.

This simply follows from the isomorphism

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k}\times\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord,\infty}},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k},A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})}\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord,\infty}},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}^{\operatorname{can}}\cdot p.

∎

7.1.3.
The Gauss–Manin connection (ordinary case)

Fix an integer
i=1,\dots,2n-1
and recall the definition of the integer
\beta_{i}
from the proof of Proposition
6.3.2
(i.e.
\beta_{i}=\beta
if
i=1,\dots,n
and
0
if
i=n+1,\dots,2n-1
). Recall from §
6.4.3
that we have an operator

\nabla_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}}.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer. Since
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
is open in
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
, we obtain an induced operator

\nabla_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}).

Furthermore, since
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})\to\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k},A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})
is a pro-étale torsor, this operator extends to an operator
\nabla_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
, where

\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}=\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}\times_{\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},k},A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+})}\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}).

The following proposition will allow us to apply the general results on continuous operators in §
3
.

Proposition 7.1.6
.

With notation as above:

(1)

The operators

\displaystyle p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})

\displaystyle p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})

are integral, i.e., they preserve
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})
and
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
respectively.

(2)

One has a continuous
A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
-algebra isomorphism

\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})\cong A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

where
X_{\operatorname{sim}}
and
X_{a,b,\tau}
are variables,
\tau
runs over
\Psi
, and
1\leq a,b\leq 2n
are integers, omitting the variables
X_{a,b,\tau_{0}}
for
(a,b)\in\{(1,2),\dots,(1,2n)\}
.

(3)

Under the isomorphism in (2), the operator
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
satisfies the following properties:

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(x)\equiv p^{\beta_{i}}\theta_{i}(x)
modulo
p^{k}
for all
x\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X_{\operatorname{sim}})\equiv 0
and
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X_{a,b,\tau})\equiv 0
modulo
p^{k}
, for all
(a,b,\tau)
with
\tau\neq\tau_{0}

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X_{1,1,\tau_{0}})\equiv-p^{\beta_{i}}X_{i+1,1,\tau_{0}}
modulo
p^{k}

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}})\equiv 0
and
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X_{a,b,\tau_{0}})\equiv 0
modulo
p^{k}
, for all
a\geq 2
and
b\neq i+1

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X_{a,i+1,\tau_{0}})\equiv p^{\beta_{i}}X_{a,1,\tau_{0}}
modulo
p^{k}
, for all
a\geq 2
with
a\neq i+1
.

Here,
\theta_{i}
denotes the operator from Proposition
6.3.2
arising from the action of
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
on
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
.

Proof.

Recall that we have a canonical basis
\mathfrak{B}^{\operatorname{can}}_{\infty}
of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
over
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
. Then the universal basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
over
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}
is given by

\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}^{\operatorname{can}}\cdot\underline{X}^{\prime}

where
\underline{X}^{\prime}=X_{\operatorname{sim}}^{\prime}\times\prod_{\tau}(X_{a,b,\tau}^{\prime})_{a,b}
is the universal element in
\overline{P}^{\operatorname{an}}_{G}
over
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}
. This gives coordinates for
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
and we find that

\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})\cong A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\frac{X_{\operatorname{sim}}^{\prime}-1}{p^{k}},\frac{X_{a,b,\tau}^{\prime}-\delta_{ab}}{p^{k}}\rangle.

Let
\underline{Y}=(\underline{X}^{\prime})^{-1}=Y_{\operatorname{sim}}\times\prod_{\tau}(Y_{a,b,\tau})_{a,b}
.

Now let
\mathcal{K}_{i}\in\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}/\mathcal{O}_{L}}
denote the unique differential such that

\nabla^{\operatorname{GM}}(e_{i+1,\tau_{0},\infty}^{\operatorname{can}})=e_{1,\tau_{0},\infty}\otimes\mathcal{K}_{i}

and note that
\theta_{i}\colon A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\to A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
is the continuous derivation dual to
\mathcal{K}_{i}
(with respect to the basis
\{\mathcal{K}_{1},\dots,\mathcal{K}_{2n-1}\}
of
\Omega^{1}_{A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}/L}
– see the proof of Proposition
6.3.2
). Let
E_{1,i+1}\in\mathfrak{g}
denote the element such that

\nabla^{\operatorname{GM}}_{\theta_{i}}(\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}^{\operatorname{can}})=\mathfrak{B}_{\infty}^{\operatorname{can}}\cdot E_{1,i+1}.

As explained in the proof of Proposition
6.3.2
,
E_{1,i+1}
is equal to the block upper nilpotent matrix defined in Corollary
5.2.17
, hence the notation.

By the explicit description of
\nabla_{i}
in Lemma
5.3.3
and the description of the
\mathcal{D}
-module structure in §
5.2
, we see that for
F\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle\frac{X_{\operatorname{sim}}^{\prime}-1}{p^{k}},\frac{X_{a,b,\tau}^{\prime}-\delta_{ab}}{p^{k}}\rangle
, one has

(7.1.7)

\nabla_{i}(F)(X^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sim}},X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau})=\left(\theta_{i}\cdot F(X^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sim}},X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau})+E_{1,i+1}\star_{l}F(X^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sim}},X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau}))\right)\cdot Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}

where
\theta_{i}\cdot F
means act by the derivation on the coefficients,
\star_{l}
is the
\mathfrak{g}
-action on
\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k})
viewing
F\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\hat{\otimes}\mathcal{O}(\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{G,k})
, and the last factor arises from the final three bullet points in Lemma
5.3.3
.

From this description, one immediately sees that
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
preserves
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
. Since the map

(7.1.8)

\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\hat{\otimes}_{A_{\operatorname{ord,k}}^{+}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}=\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})

is an isometry (the fact that
A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+}\to A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
is a pro-étale torsor implies that the map (
7.1.8
) is injective modulo
p^{\ell}
for any
\ell\geq 1
), one sees that the operator
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
also preserves
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})
. This proves part (1).

Now using the explicit formula in (
7.1.7
), we can compute the action of
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
in terms of the coordinates
\underline{X}^{\prime}
. Note that
Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\equiv 1
modulo
p^{k}
. An explicit matrix calculation of the action
E_{1,i+1}\star_{l}-
shows that

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(x)\equiv p^{\beta_{i}}\theta_{i}(x)
modulo
p^{k}
, for any
x\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}

•

p^{\beta}\nabla_{i}(X^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sim}})\equiv 0
and
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau})\equiv 0
modulo
p^{k}
for any
(a,b,\tau)
with
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
.

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X^{\prime}_{1,1,\tau_{0}})\equiv-p^{\beta_{i}}X^{\prime}_{i+1,1,\tau_{0}}
modulo
p^{k}

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X^{\prime}_{j,1,\tau_{0}})\equiv 0
modulo
p^{k}
, for all
2\leq j\leq 2n

•

p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}(X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau_{0}})\equiv p^{\beta_{i}}X^{\prime}_{a,1,\tau_{0}}(X^{\prime}_{1,1,\tau_{0}})^{-1}X^{\prime}_{i+1,b,\tau_{0}}
modulo
p^{k}
, for all
2\leq a,b\leq 2n
.

Now consider the following change of coordinates:

•

X_{\operatorname{sim}}=\frac{X^{\prime}_{\operatorname{sim}}-1}{p^{k}}
and
X_{a,b,\tau}=\frac{X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau}-\delta_{ab}}{p^{k}}
for all
(a,b,\tau)
with
\tau\neq\tau_{0}

•

X_{a,b,\tau_{0}}=\frac{X^{\prime}_{a,b,\tau_{0}}-\delta_{ab}}{p^{k}}
for all
(a,b)
(except those in the set
\{(1,2),\dots,(1,2n)\}
) with
(a,b)\neq(i+1,i+1)

•

X_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}=X_{1,1,\tau_{0}}+\frac{X_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}-1}{p^{k}}
.

This change of coordinates gives rise to a continuous
A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}
-algebra isomorphism

\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})\cong A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

and one can easily verify that the properties in (3) are satisfied.
∎

We now apply the general results in §
3
.

Proposition 7.1.9
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. There exists an integer
k(\varepsilon)\geq 1
such that, for any
k\geq k(\varepsilon)
, there exists a unique continuous
L
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{i}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\times\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})

such that the action of the natural inclusion
p^{-\beta_{i}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow L
is given by
\nabla_{i}
.

Proof.

Note that
C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{i}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
is isomorphic to
C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)
isometrically, and the function
p^{-\beta_{i}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}
given by
p^{-\beta_{i}}x\mapsto x
is identified with the natural inclusion
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}
. Hence it is enough to show that there is a unique action

C_{\varepsilon}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\times\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})

such that the natural map
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\hookrightarrow L
corresponds to the action of
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
. We first prove a similar claim for
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
by iterating Proposition
3.3.1
.

Consider the Tate algebra
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
from Proposition
7.1.6
(2), which is independent of
k
. We will partition the coordinates in the following way. Let

\Sigma_{1}=\{X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\}\cup\left\{X_{a,b,\tau_{0}}:\begin{array}[]{c}2\leq a\leq 2n\\
b\neq 1,i+1\end{array}\right\}\cup\left\{X_{a,b,\tau}:\begin{array}[]{c}1\leq a,b\leq 2n\\
\tau\neq\tau_{0}\end{array}\right\}

and set
S_{1}^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle\Sigma_{1}\rangle
and
S_{1}=S_{1}^{+}[1/p]
. Let
D_{1}=p^{\beta_{i}}\theta_{i}\colon S_{1}^{+}\to S_{1}^{+}
denote the derivation which acts only on the coefficients in
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
(i.e. the coordinates in
\Sigma_{1}
are all horizontal). By §
6.1.3
, the operator
D_{1}
extends to a continuous (and hence locally analytic) action on
S_{1}
.

Now, for
2\leq r\leq i
, set
S_{r}^{+}=S_{r-1}^{+}\langle X_{r,i+1,\tau_{0}},X_{r,1,\tau_{0}}\rangle
and
S_{r}=S_{r}^{+}[1/p]
. Consider the derivation
D_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T_{D_{r-1},p^{\beta_{i}}}
on
S_{r}^{+}
as constructed in §
3.3
(i.e. it acts on
S_{r-1}^{+}
as
D_{r-1}
, and we have
D_{r}(X_{r,i+1,\tau_{0}})=p^{\beta_{i}}X_{r,1,\tau_{0}}
and
D_{r}(X_{r,1,\tau_{0}})=0
). Similarly, we set
S_{i+1}^{+}=S_{i}^{+}\langle X_{1,1,\tau_{0}},X_{i+1,1,\tau_{0}}\rangle
,
S_{i+1}=S_{i+1}^{+}[1/p]
, and
D_{i+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T_{D_{i},-p^{\beta_{i}}}
acting on
S_{i+1}^{+}
. Finally, for
i+2\leq r\leq 2n
, we set
S_{r}^{+}=S_{r-1}^{+}\langle X_{r,i+1,\tau_{0}},X_{r,1,\tau_{0}}\rangle
,
S_{r}=S_{r}^{+}[1/p]
, and
D_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T_{D_{r-1},p^{\beta_{i}}}
acting on
S_{r}^{+}
. Note that
V=S_{2n}
.

Now iteratively applying Proposition
3.3.1
to the tuples
(S_{r-1},D_{r-1},S_{r},D_{r})
and using the fact that
D_{1}
extends to a locally analytic action on
S_{1}
, we see that
T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}D_{2n}
extends to a locally analytic action on
V
. By Lemma
3.2.1
, there exists an integer
k(\varepsilon)\geq 1
, such that for any
k\geq k(\varepsilon)
and continuous operator
T^{\prime}\colon V\to V
preserving the unit ball
V^{+}=A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
, the operator
T+p^{k}T^{\prime}
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action.

Let
k\geq k(\varepsilon)
. Then, by Proposition
7.1.6
, we have an identification
\mathcal{O}^{+}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})=V^{+}
and the operator
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
is congruent to
T
modulo
p^{k}
. Since
V^{+}
is
p
-torsion free, this implies that
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}=T+p^{k}T^{\prime}
for some continuous operator
T^{\prime}\colon V^{+}\to V^{+}
, hence
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
.

Finally, since
A_{\operatorname{ord},k}^{+}\to A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
is a pro-étale torsor, the map
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
is an isometry, and therefore the operator
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})
.
∎

7.2.
Overconvergent neighbourhoods for
G

In this section we will prove an analogous result to Proposition
7.1.9
for overconvergent neighbourhoods of the Igusa tower inside
P_{G,\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
. We will continue to use the notation introduced in the previous section. In particular, we will continue to work locally with respect to a choice of
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
.

7.2.1.
Overconvergent extensions

To able to analyse the Gauss–Manin connection on overconvergent neighbourhoods, we will need to consider certain neighbourhoods with specific properties. Recall that
h
is a fixed generator of
\hat{\delta}_{G,n+1}^{+}
over
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
. For an integer
r\geq 1
, let
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{r},A^{+}_{r})=\operatorname{Spa}(A\langle\frac{p}{h^{p^{r+1}}}\rangle,A^{+}\langle\frac{p}{h^{p^{r+1}}}\rangle)\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{r}
denote the pullback of
\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})
under the affinoid morphism

\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\to\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})

(see Remark
6.2.11
).

Definition 7.2.1
.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer. We say that a quasi-compact open affinoid subspace
\mathcal{U}\subset P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
is an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
if

•

One has
\mathcal{U}\cap P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A_{\operatorname{ord}}}^{\operatorname{an}}=\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
, where the intersection is taken inside
P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}

•

\mathcal{U}
contains the closure of
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})
inside
P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Given an overconvergent extension
\mathcal{U}
and an integer
r\geq 1
, we set
\mathcal{U}_{r}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{U}\cap P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A_{r}}^{\operatorname{an}}
, which is again an overconvergent extension.

The following proposition shows that overconvergent extensions actually exist.

Proposition 7.2.2
.

For any integer
k\geq 1
, there exists an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
. Moreover, the collection of all overconvergent extensions (as
k
varies) forms a cofinal system of quasi-compact open neighbourhoods of the closure of
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})
inside
P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
is the pushout of of
\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\times_{\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})}\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord}},A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord}})
along the natural map
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})\hookrightarrow\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,k}
, the result follows from Proposition
6.4.3
.
∎

We now establish a key property for overconvergent extensions as we vary the radius of overconvergence. Let
k\geq 1
be an integer and
\mathcal{U}
an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
. Then we have a chain of quasi-compact open affinoid neighbourhoods

\mathcal{U}_{1}\supset\mathcal{U}_{2}\supset\cdots\supset\mathcal{U}_{\infty}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}

such that
\mathcal{U}_{\infty}
is the locus inside
\mathcal{U}_{r}
where
|h|=1
. On sections, this chain of neighbourhoods is induced from the chain of continuous maps

B_{1}^{+}\to B_{2}^{+}\to\cdots\to B_{\infty}^{+}

where
B_{r}^{+}=\mathcal{O}^{+}(\mathcal{U}_{r})
. Let
B_{r}=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{r})=B_{r}^{+}[1/p]
, and let
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{r}
denote the
L
-Banach norm on
B_{r}
for which
B_{r}^{+}
is the unit ball.

Lemma 7.2.3
.

Let
r\geq 1
. For any real number
0<\delta<1
, there exists an integer
s=s(\delta)\geq r
such that: for any
v\in B_{r}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
c\in\mathbb{Q}
, one has

|\!|v|\!|_{\infty}\leq p^{c-m}\text{ and }|\!|v|\!|_{r}\leq p^{c}\quad\Rightarrow\quad|\!|v|\!|_{s}\leq p^{c-\delta m}.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{U}_{r}
is an overconvergent extension, we have
B^{+}_{\infty}=B_{r}^{+}\langle 1/h\rangle
, hence
\left(B^{+}_{\infty}/p\right)=\left(B_{r}^{+}/p\right)[1/h]
. Therefore, any element in the kernel of the map
B_{r}^{+}/p\to B_{\infty}^{+}/p
is killed by some power of
h
. Since
B_{r}^{+}/p
is Noetherian, we see that there is a common power
h^{M}
which kills any element in the kernel of the map
B_{r}^{+}/p\to B_{\infty}^{+}/p
. Since
B_{\infty}^{+}
is
p
-torsion free, this implies (by a simple induction argument) that
h^{mM}
kills the kernel of
B_{r}^{+}/p^{m}\to B^{+}_{\infty}/p^{m}
for any integer
m\geq 1
.

Now, by raising
v
to an integral power, it is enough to prove the claim for
c\in\mathbb{Z}
, and by rescaling, it is enough to prove the claim when
c=0
. Therefore, we suppose we have an element
v\in B_{r}^{+}
whose image lies in
p^{m}B_{\infty}^{+}
. By the paragraph above, we must therefore have

|\!|(p^{-1}h^{M})^{m}v|\!|_{s}\leq|\!|(p^{-1}h^{M})^{m}v|\!|_{r}\leq 1

for any
s\geq r
and
m\geq 1
. Since
|\!|h^{-1}|\!|_{s}\to 1
as
s\to+\infty
, taking
s
large enough such that
|\!|h^{-1}|\!|_{s}\leq p^{\frac{(1-\delta)}{M}}
, we obtain

|\!|v|\!|_{s}\leq|\!|(ph^{-M})^{m}|\!|_{s}\leq p^{-\delta m}

as required.
∎

7.2.2.
The Gauss–Manin connection (overconvergent case)

We now prove an overconvergent analogue of Proposition
7.1.9
.

Proposition 7.2.4
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
. For any quasi-compact open neighbourhood
U
of the closure of
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})
inside
P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
, there exists a quasi-compact open neighbourhood
V
of the closure of
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})
inside
P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
and a unique continuous
L
-linear action

(7.2.5)

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(U)\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(V)

extending the action of polynomial functions in
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
induced from the operators
\{\nabla_{i}:i=1,\dots,2n-1\}
. These actions are compatible as one varies
\varepsilon
,
U
, and
V
.

Proof.

Let
i\in\{1,\dots,2n-1\}
and let
k\geq 1
be an integer such that
k\geq k(\varepsilon/2)
, where
k(\varepsilon/2)
is the integer in Proposition
7.1.9
(depending on the integer
i
). Then, increasing
k
if necessary, there exists an overconvergent extension
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
with
\mathcal{U}\subset U
because overconvergent extensions are cofinal. Without loss of generality, we may assume
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{1}
. Then, we consider the chain of
L
-Banach spaces

\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{1})\to\cdots\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{\infty})

where
\mathcal{U}_{\infty}=\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
. By Proposition
7.1.9
, the operator
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
extends to an
\varepsilon/2
-analytic action on
\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{\infty})
. Combining this with Lemma
7.2.3
, we see that the hypotheses of Proposition
3.4.1
are satisfied, hence there exists an integer
r\geq 1
such that
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
extends to an
\varepsilon
-analytic action on
\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{r})
. In other words, if we set
V_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{r}
, then there exists a continuous
L
-linear action

C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{i}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\times\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(U)\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(V_{i})

extending the action of polynomial functions in
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
. This action is unique because it can be computed on Mahler expansions.

Now take
V=\cap_{i=1}^{2n-1}V_{i}
. Using the fact that the operators
\nabla_{i}
commute with each other and the fact that

C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\otimes_{L}\cdots\otimes_{L}C_{\varepsilon}(p^{-\beta_{2n-1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p},L)\subset C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)

is dense, we see that there is a unique action as in (
7.2.5
) extending the action of polynomial functions. This action is functorial in
\varepsilon
,
U
and
V
by unicity.
∎

We now prove the first part of Theorem
7.0.2
. Let
U=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
. Then, from the functoriality properties in Proposition
7.2.4
, we obtain a continuous
L
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)

extending the action of polynomial functions. This action is functorial in
\varepsilon
. We claim that this action is unique. Indeed, by Proposition
6.4.3
, there exist integers
r_{1}<r_{2}<\cdots
and étale
\overline{\mathcal{P}}^{\square}_{G,k}
-torsors
\mathcal{U}_{r_{k},k}\to\operatorname{Spa}(A_{r_{k}},A_{r_{k}}^{+})
with
\mathcal{U}_{r_{k},k}
an overconvergent extension of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
, such that

\mathcal{U}_{r_{1},1}\supset\mathcal{U}_{r_{2},2}\supset\cdots\supset\mathcal{U}_{r_{k},k}\supset\cdots\supset\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}).

Furthermore, the collection
\{\mathcal{U}_{r_{k},k}\}_{k\geq 1}
is a cofinal system of quasi-compact open neighbourhoods of
P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}
containing the closure of
\operatorname{Spa}(A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty},A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+})
, and all of these torsors are necessarily reductions of structure of each other. This implies that

(7.2.6)

\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)\cong\left(\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{r_{k},k})\right)_{k\geq 1}.

Suppose that
\star_{1}
and
\star_{2}
are two continuous actions of
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)
extending the action of polynomial functions induced from the operators
\{\nabla_{i}:i=1,\dots,2n-1\}
. Let
f\in C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
which we can write as a limit
f=\lim_{m}f_{m}
of polynomial functions, and let
x\in\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{r_{k},k})
for some
k\geq 1
. By assumption and our definition of an action on ind-sheaves (see §
1.5
), there exists an integer
k^{\prime}\geq 1
and continuous maps

(7.2.7)

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\to\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{r_{k^{\prime}},k^{\prime}}),\quad g\mapsto g\star_{j}x\quad(j=1,2).

Since
\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{r_{k^{\prime}},k^{\prime}})
is Hausdorff and both actions extend the same action of polynomial functions, we find that

f\star_{1}x=\lim_{m}f_{m}\star_{1}x=\lim_{m}f_{m}\star_{2}x=f\star_{2}x

with the limit taking place in
\mathcal{O}_{P_{G,\operatorname{dR},A}^{\operatorname{an}}}(\mathcal{U}_{r_{k^{\prime}},k^{\prime}})
. This implies that the actions
\star_{1}
and
\star_{2}
coincide (as morphisms of ind-sheaves), thereby proving uniqueness.

To conclude the proof of Theorem
7.0.2
(1), we must show that the action is functorial in
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and equivariant for the action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Let
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
with
U_{1}\supset U_{2}
, and consider the natural (continuous) restriction map
\pi\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U_{1})\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U_{2})
. Let
x\in\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U_{1})
and consider the following two continuous maps
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U_{2})
given by

(7.2.8)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto\pi(f\star_{U_{1}}x)

(7.2.9)

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\mapsto f\star_{U_{2}}\pi(x)

where
\star_{U_{j}}
denotes the action of
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U_{j})
. By the same argument above, we can write
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U_{2})\cong(V_{k})_{k\geq 1}
as a filtered inductive system of Banach spaces and both (
7.2.8
) and (
7.2.9
) factor through some
V_{k}
(by the definition of an action on ind-sheaves). Write
f=\lim_{m}f_{m}
as a limit of polynomial functions. Since we know that the action of the operators
\{\nabla_{i}\}
(and hence the action of polynomial functions) is already functorial, we have

\pi(f\star_{U_{1}}x)=\lim_{m}\pi(f_{m}\star_{U_{1}}x)=\lim_{m}(f_{m}\star_{U_{2}}\pi(x))=f\star_{U_{2}}\pi(x)

with the limit taking place in
V_{k}
. This implies that (
7.2.8
)
=
(
7.2.9
). The proof for the equivariance under the action of
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
is very similar, and can again be deduced from the density of polynomial functions.

7.3.
The construction for
H

In this section we finish the proof of Theorem
7.0.2
. Since the construction of the action in Theorem
7.0.2
(2) follows exactly the same strategy as in §
7.1
–
7.2
, we do not repeat the construction in the same amount of detail. Instead, we simply highlight the differences between the constructions for
G
and
H
and leave the details to the interested reader.

To define the analogue of the torsors
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
, we consider quotients of the Igusa tower
\mathfrak{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
by the subgroup of
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
consisting of elements which are congruent to the identity modulo
p^{k}
. Over these quotients of the Igusa tower, we again have a canonical basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}/p^{k}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}/p^{k}
arising from the universal trivialisations, and we can consider the torsor parameterising bases of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}_{2}}
respecting the symplectic structure and Hodge filtration which reduce to the canonical basis modulo
p^{k}
. Working locally, one can show via the same method as in Proposition
7.2.2
that these torsors overconverge (and hence we can define overconvergent extensions in this setting). To define the system of overconvergent neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
inside
\mathcal{X}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
locally, we consider the exact same congruences but now involving a generator of
\hat{\delta}^{+}_{H,n+1}
(see Definition
6.2.15
).

We now proceed in exactly the same way for the construction the action for
H
. Indeed, one can choose coordinates of
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
base-changed to
\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
so that the action of
p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,n-1
) is congruent modulo
p^{k}
to a simple operator on a Tate algebra (which is an iterated version of the construction in §
3.3
). One then applies the general results in §
3
in the same way as in §
7.1
–
7.2
, and this results in the construction of the action in Theorem
7.0.2
(2). The uniqueness and functoriality results follow the same proof as at the end of §
7.2.2
.

To prove Theorem
7.0.2
(3), we again use the same strategy as at the end of §
7.2.2
. More precisely, we already know from §
6.4.4
that the pullback map is equivariant on polynomial functions, and we use the fact that
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)
is a filtered inductive system of Banach spaces. Hence equivariance on polynomial functions is enough to deduce equivariance for the
\varepsilon
-analytic actions.

7.4.
p
-adic evaluation maps

We now construct the
p
-adic analogues of the evaluation maps in §
5.3.2
following the general construction in §
2.3
.

7.4.1.
Compatibility with support conditions

Fix
U=\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+})\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and let
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U\cap(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))
. Before constructing the evaluation maps, one must first show that the structures on
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)
extend over
V
. Recall the definitions of
\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{\bullet}
and
\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{\bullet}
from Lemma
6.4.2
and Proposition
6.4.3
respectively, and also their versions with a fixed weight from Definition
6.4.4
.

Note that
\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)\subset\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
(resp.
\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V)\subset\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V)
) is the subspace killed by the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
(resp.
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
) under
\star_{\overline{P}}
. Furthermore,
\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
and
\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
are Banach
L
-algebras, and
\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V)
and
\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V)
are Fréchet
L
-algebras. Indeed, let
\delta
be a fixed generator of
\delta_{G,>n+1}^{+}
over
U
, and let
\{\gamma_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
be a monotonically decreasing sequence of rational numbers
\gamma_{i}>0
such that
\gamma_{i}\to 0
as
i\to+\infty
. Let
V_{\gamma_{i}}\subset V\subset U
denote the open affinoid subspace where
|\delta|\leq|p|^{\gamma_{i}}
. Then
\{V_{\gamma_{i}}\}
is a cover of
V
with compact restriction maps with dense image. We have

\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V)=\varprojlim_{i}\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}}),\quad\quad\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V)=\varprojlim_{i}\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})

where each term in the inverse limit is a Banach
L
-algebra and the transition maps are compact and have dense image.

Lemma 7.4.1
.

Let
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U\cap(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. Then the action in Theorem
7.0.2
(1) extends uniquely to a continuous
L
-algebra action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(V)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(V)

which is functorial in
U
,
\varepsilon
, and is
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that the rational numbers above satisfy
\gamma_{i}=1/i
. Then we have

\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})=\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)\langle\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\rangle

for all
k\geq 1
. Let
\varepsilon>0
. Let
\nabla\in\{p^{\beta_{1}}\nabla_{1},\dots,p^{\beta_{2n-1}}\nabla_{2n-1}\}
be one of the operators appearing at the start of §
6.4.3
, normalised by the power of
p
defined in Proposition
6.3.2
. Then there exists
k^{\prime}\geq k
and
r^{\prime}\geq r
such that

•

We have a continuous
L
-linear action

C_{\varepsilon/2}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(U)

extending the action of polynomial functions. In particular, there exists a constant
C\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that for any
f\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathcal{O}_{L})
of degree
\leq a
and
y\in\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
, we have
p^{-a\varepsilon/2}|\!|f\star y|\!|_{k^{\prime}}\leq C|\!|y|\!|_{k}
, where
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{k}
(resp.
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{k^{\prime}}
) denotes the Banach norm on
\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
(resp.
\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(U)
).

•

|\!|\nabla(\delta)\delta^{-1}|\!|_{k^{\prime}}\leq p^{\varepsilon/4}
for any choice of
\nabla
. Indeed,
\nabla
is integral over the ordinary locus (see Proposition
7.1.6
), so we can increase
k^{\prime}
if necessary so that this is satisfied.

Let
x=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}a_{j}\left(\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\right)^{j}\in\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})
, with
a_{j}\in\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
converging to zero. Then, by a similar argument as in the proof of Proposition
3.3.1
, we have (for
a\geq 1
)

\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{\nabla}{a}(a_{j}\left(\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\right)^{j})=\sum_{b=0}^{\operatorname{min}(a,j)}\sum\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ multinomial }\\
\text{ coefficients }\end{array}\right)\cdot\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\text{ element of }\\
C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathcal{O}_{L})\text{ of }\\
\text{ degree }\leq a\end{array}\right)(a_{j})\cdot\nabla\left(\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\right)^{b}\cdot\left(\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\right)^{j-b}

where the unlabelled sum is indexed by subsets of
\{0,\dots,a-1\}
of size
a-b
(c.f. formula (
3.3.2
)). Let
|\!|\cdot|\!|_{k^{\prime},i}
denote the Banach norm on
\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})
. Then, following the proof of Proposition
3.3.1
, we see that

(7.4.2)

\displaystyle\left|\!\left|\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{\nabla}{a}(a_{j}\left(\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\right)^{j})\right|\!\right|_{k^{\prime},i}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{max}\{p^{2\operatorname{log}_{p}(a)}|\!|f\star a_{j}|\!|_{k^{\prime}}|\!|\nabla\left(\frac{\delta^{i}}{p}\right)|\!|_{k^{\prime}}^{b}\}

(7.4.3)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{max}\{p^{2\operatorname{log}_{p}(a)}|\!|f\star a_{j}|\!|_{k^{\prime}}|\!|\nabla(\delta)\delta^{-1}|\!|_{k^{\prime}}^{b}\}

(7.4.4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p^{2\operatorname{log}_{p}(a)}p^{a\varepsilon/2}C|\!|a_{j}|\!|_{k}p^{a\varepsilon/4}

where the maxima in (
7.4.2
) and (
7.4.3
) are over
0\leq b\leq a
and polynomials
f\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathcal{O}_{L})
of degree
\leq a
. For the inequality in (
7.4.3
) we have used the fact that
\nabla(\delta^{i}/p)=i\nabla(\delta)\delta^{-1}(\delta^{i}/p)
, and for the inequality in (
7.4.4
), we have used the fact that
|\!|f\star a_{j}|\!|_{k^{\prime}}\leq p^{a\varepsilon/2}C|\!|a_{j}|\!|_{k}
and
|\!|\nabla(\delta)\delta^{-1}|\!|_{k^{\prime}}^{b}\leq p^{b\varepsilon/4}\leq p^{a\varepsilon/4}
. This implies that

p^{-a\varepsilon}\left|\!\left|\genfrac{(}{)}{0.0pt}{0}{\nabla}{a}(x)\right|\!\right|_{k^{\prime},i}\leq p^{-a\varepsilon/4+2\operatorname{log}_{p}(a)}C\operatorname{max}_{j}|\!|a_{j}|\!|_{k}

and hence the left-hand side converges to zero as
a\to+\infty
. This implies that the action
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(U)
extends uniquely to an action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})\to\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})

for any
\gamma_{i}
, and hence induces an action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\varprojlim_{i}\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})\to\varprojlim_{i}\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})

as required. The rest of the lemma follows.
∎

Remark 7.4.5
.

By a similar argument as in Lemma
7.4.1
above, one can check that Theorem
7.0.2
(2) extends to
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V)
, and we also have an analogue of Theorem
7.0.2
(3).

We will also need the following important result.

Lemma 7.4.6
.

Fix
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and set
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U\cap(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))
. Let

\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}\in\{\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(U),\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(V)\}.

Let
f\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
be any locally constant function. Then for any
x\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}
, one has
f\star x\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}
, i.e. the action of the locally constant function
f
takes overconvergent forms to overconvergent forms. We also have an analogous statement for overconvergent forms for
H
.

Proof.

We first prove the claim for
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}=\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)
. Fix an integer
i=1,\dots,2n-1
and let
\xi\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
f_{i,\xi}^{(s)}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
denote the indicator function of the following subset:

p^{-\beta_{1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times\cdots\times p^{-\beta_{i-1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times p^{-\beta_{i}}\left(\xi+p^{s}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)\times p^{-\beta_{i+1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\times\cdots\times p^{-\beta_{2n-1}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subset U_{G,\beta}.

where
s\geq 0
is an integer. It is enough to show that these indicator functions preserve overconvergent forms. We will prove this by induction on the radius of analyticity
s\geq 0
. Note that the claim for
s=0
is immediate, because
f^{(0)}_{i,\xi}\equiv 1
for any
i
,
\xi
.

Let
s\geq 1
be an integer. Let
\varepsilon>0
be any real number such that
f_{i,\xi}^{(s^{\prime})}\in C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
for any choice of
i
,
\xi
and
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
. With notation as above, let
k
be a sufficiently large integer such that we have an action

C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)\times\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k)}_{G}(U)

for some integer
r^{\prime}\geq r
. We may also assume that we have an action of
C_{\varepsilon}(U_{G,\beta},L)
on
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})
where
\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k}
is defined in §
7.1
. Since the natural restriction map

\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k)}_{G}(U)\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})

is injective and equivariant for the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
, it suffices to show for any
x\in\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})
killed by
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
, the element
f_{i,\xi}^{(s)}\star x
is also killed by
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
. Furthermore, since the natural map

\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k})\to\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})

is also injective and equivariant for the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
, it suffices to prove the same statement for elements of
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})
.

Recall from Proposition
7.1.6
that we have an identification

\mathcal{O}^{+}(\mathcal{U}_{\operatorname{HT},k,\infty})=A^{+}_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

and an operator
T\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}p^{\beta_{i}}\nabla_{i}
on this space. For the rest of this proof, we freely use notation from the proof of Proposition
7.1.6
.

Let
\mathcal{I}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
denote the ideal generated by the coordinates
X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}
(as
a,b,\tau
vary). Then note that
Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\in 1+\mathcal{I}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
. Using the description of the operator in (
7.1.7
) and explicitly calculating
\star_{l}
, we see that:

•

T(X_{\operatorname{sim}})=0
and
T(X_{a,b,\tau})=0
for all
(a,b,\tau)
with
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
.

•

T(X_{1,1,\tau_{0}})=-p^{\beta_{i}}X_{i+1,1,\tau_{0}}Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\in-p^{\beta_{i}}X_{i+1,1,\tau_{0}}+\mathcal{I}^{2}
.

•

T(X_{a,1,\tau_{0}})=0
for all
2\leq a\leq 2n

•

For
2\leq a,b\leq 2n
with
b\neq i+1
, we have

T(X_{a,b,\tau_{0}})=p^{\beta_{i}+k}X_{a,1,\tau_{0}}(X_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{i+1,b,\tau_{0}}Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\in\mathcal{I}^{2}.

•

For
2\leq a\leq 2n
with
a\neq i+1
, we have

T(X_{a,i+1,\tau_{0}})=p^{\beta_{i}}X_{a,1,\tau_{0}}(X^{\prime}_{1,1,\tau_{0}})^{-1}X_{i+1,i+1}^{\prime}Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\in p^{\beta_{i}}X_{a,1,\tau_{0}}+\mathcal{I}^{2}.

•

T(X_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}})=p^{\beta_{i}}X_{i+1,1,\tau_{0}}((X_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{\prime})^{-1}X_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}^{\prime}-1)Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\in\mathcal{I}^{2}
.

This implies that
T^{2}(\mathcal{I})\subset\mathcal{I}^{2}
. By continually applying the Leibniz rule, we see that for any integer
h\geq 1
, one has

T^{h+1}(\mathcal{I}^{h})\subset\mathcal{I}^{h+1}.

Let
\widetilde{\star}
denote the action of
C_{\operatorname{cont}}(U_{G,\beta},\mathcal{O}_{L})
on
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
given by simply acting on the coefficients (via the action in §
6.1.3
). Let
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
. Suppose that the action of
f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}
on
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
under
\star
is equal to the action of
f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}
under
\widetilde{\star}
. Let
\theta\colon A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle\to A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
denote the derivation which acts as
p^{\beta_{i}}\theta_{i}
on
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
and
\theta(X_{\operatorname{sim}})=\theta(X_{a,b,\tau})=0
. Then:

•

For any
x\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

p^{-(s^{\prime}-1)p^{l-1}(p-1)}(T-\xi)^{p^{l-1}(p-1)}(f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star x)

converges to
f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star x-f^{(s^{\prime})}_{i,\xi}\star x
as
l\to+\infty
.

•

For any
x\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

p^{-(s^{\prime}-1)p^{l-1}(p-1)}(\theta-\xi)^{p^{l-1}(p-1)}(f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star x)

converges to
f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star x-f^{(s^{\prime})}_{i,\xi}\widetilde{\star}x
as
l\to+\infty
. In particular

(\theta-\xi)^{M}(f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star x)\in p^{(s^{\prime}-1)M}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

for any
M\geq 1
.

•

The operators
T
and
\theta
commute, which implies that the idempotent operators
F\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f^{(s^{\prime})}_{i,\xi}\star-
and
\widetilde{F}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f^{(s^{\prime})}_{i,\xi}\widetilde{\star}-
commute. This implies that, for any odd integer
w\geq 1
, one has

(7.4.7)

(F-\widetilde{F})^{w}=F-\widetilde{F}.

Let
a\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}
and
y\in\mathcal{I}^{h}
(for some
h\geq 0
, with the convention that
\mathcal{I}^{0}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
). Then we have

p^{-(s^{\prime}-1)M}(T-\xi)^{M}(f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star ay)\equiv\sum_{j=0}^{h}\binom{M}{j}\left[p^{-(s^{\prime}-1)M}(\theta-\xi)^{M-j}(f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star a)\right]T^{j}(y)

modulo
\mathcal{I}^{h+1}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
, for any
M\geq h
. Here we have used that fact that
Y_{1,1,\tau_{0}}^{-1}Y_{i+1,i+1,\tau_{0}}\in 1+\mathcal{I}
,
T^{h+1}(\mathcal{I}^{h})\subset\mathcal{I}^{h+1}
, and
T(\mathcal{I}^{h^{\prime}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle)\subset\mathcal{I}^{h^{\prime}}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
for any integer
h^{\prime}\geq 0
.

For any
1\leq j\leq h
, we have that

\binom{p^{l-1}(p-1)}{j}\left[p^{-(s^{\prime}-1)p^{l-1}(p-1)}(\theta-\xi)^{p^{l-1}(p-1)-j}(f^{(s^{\prime}-1)}_{i,\xi}\star a)\right]T^{j}(y)

converges to
0
as
l\to+\infty
. Since
\mathcal{I}^{h+1}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
is a closed ideal, by taking
M=p^{l-1}(p-1)
and passing to the limit as
l\to+\infty
, this implies that

F(ay)-\widetilde{F}(ay)\in\mathcal{I}^{h+1}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

and hence

(F-\widetilde{F})(\mathcal{I}^{h}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle)\subset\mathcal{I}^{h+1}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle

for any
h\geq 0
. By using the idempotent property in (
7.4.7
), we therefore see that

(F-\widetilde{F})(x)\in\bigcap_{h\geq 0}\mathcal{I}^{h}A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle=\{0\}

for any
x\in A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
. Hence
F=\widetilde{F}
. Therefore, by an induction argument on
0\leq s^{\prime}\leq s
, we see that
f^{(s)}_{i,\xi}\star-
is equal to
f^{(s)}_{i,\xi}\widetilde{\star}-
for any
i
,
\xi
.

But the subspace of
A_{\operatorname{ord},\infty}^{+}\langle X_{\operatorname{sim}},X_{a,b,\tau}\rangle
killed by the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
is identified with the subspace of power series which are constant in the variables
\{X_{a,1,\tau_{0}}:2\leq a\leq 2n\}
, hence we see that
f^{(s)}_{i,\xi}\star-=f^{(s)}_{i,\xi}\widetilde{\star}-
preserves this subspace as required. This completes the proof of the claim when
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}=\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)
.

The claim for
\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}=\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(V)
now follows from the fact that
\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
is dense in
\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})
, and the fact that the actions of
f
and
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
are continuous. Indeed, if
x\in\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})
and
k^{\prime}\geq k
is such that the action of
f
induces a continuous operator

\mathscr{N}^{(r,k)}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})\xrightarrow{f\star-}\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime})}_{G}(V_{\gamma_{i}})

then we can write
x=\lim_{l\to+\infty}x_{l}
with
x_{l}\in\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G}(U)
and we see that

X\star_{\overline{P}}(f\star x)=\lim_{l\to+\infty}X\star_{\overline{P}}(f\star x_{l})=0

for any
X\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
.

The proof of the analogous claim for
H
follows exactly the same argument.
∎

Example 7.4.8
.

Lemma
7.4.6
implies that the actions of the locally constant functions
1_{U^{\circ}_{G,\beta}}
and
1_{U^{\circ}_{G,\beta},\chi}
(introduced in §
2
) preserve overconvergent forms.

7.4.2.
The main construction

Following §
2.3
, we now construct certain morphisms of sheaves of overconvergent forms needed to defined the
p
-adic evaluation maps. We will freely use the notation in §
2.3
.

Definition 7.4.9
.

Let
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U\cap(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))
. Let
s\geq 1
be an integer.

(1)

For
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
, let

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V)

denote the
L
-linear morphisms as constructed in Definition
2.3.5
(with
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U)
and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)
in the former case, and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(V)
and
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}=\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V)
in the latter case). These morphisms are functorial in
U
.

(2)

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a Tate affinoid algebra over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
and
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
. We let

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V)

denote the
R
-linear morphisms, which are functorial in
U
, as constructed in Definition
2.3.9
.

(3)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite-order character such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. We let

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}\colon\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V)

denote the
L
-linear morphisms constructed in Definition
2.3.10
, which are functorial in
U
.

Remark 7.4.10
.

Let
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
denote the indicator function of
U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}\subset U_{G,\beta}
(see Definition
2.2.10
). Then we have the following compatibility properties:

•

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite-order character such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. Then we have commutative diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}\star}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}

Furthermore, we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}

where
\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}
is defined in Definition
5.3.7
.

•

If
s^{\prime}\geq s
and
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}\subset\mathcal{X}_{R,s^{\prime}}
, then we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s^{\prime}\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,s^{\prime}\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}

•

If
(R,R^{+})\to(R^{\prime},(R^{\prime})^{+})
is a morphism of Tate affinoid algebras over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
, and
(\kappa^{\prime},j^{\prime})\in\mathcal{X}_{R^{\prime},s}
denotes the image of
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
under the natural map
\mathcal{X}_{R,s}\to\mathcal{X}_{R^{\prime},s}
, then
\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
and
\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa^{\prime},j^{\prime},\beta}
are compatible under the natural maps
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,(\kappa^{\prime})^{*}}(U)
and
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)\to\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa^{\prime}}^{[j^{\prime}]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)
.

We have similar compatibility relations for the modules
\mathscr{N}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)
,
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)
,
\mathscr{N}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)
etc.

We now prove an analogous result to Proposition
5.3.8
, namely that the morphisms in Definition
7.4.9
take overconvergent forms on
G
to overconvergent forms on
H
.

Proposition 7.4.11
.

Let
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and
V\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U\cap(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))
.

(1)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Then the morphisms
\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
induce
L
-linear morphisms

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V).

(2)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite-order character such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. Then the morphisms
\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}
induce
L
-linear morphisms

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V).

(3)

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a Tate affinoid algebra over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
and set
\Omega=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})
. Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
, and suppose that there exists a Zariski dense subset
\Sigma\subset\Omega(\mathbb{C}_{p})
such that for any point
x\in\Sigma
(corresponding to a morphism
x\colon R\to\mathbb{C}_{p}
), the induced character
(\kappa_{x},j_{x})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}x\circ(\kappa,j)
lies in
\mathcal{E}
. Then the morphisms
\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
induce
R
-linear morphisms

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V).

Proof.

Parts (1) and (2) follow the same strategy as in Proposition
5.3.8
. More precisely, let

(\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G},\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G},\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}},\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}},\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H})

be one of the following tuples:

\displaystyle(\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(U),\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(U),\;

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U),\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U),\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U))

\displaystyle(\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G}(V),\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G}(V),\;

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V),\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V),\;\mathscr{N}^{\dagger}_{H}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V))

and let
(\vartheta,\xi)\in\{(\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta},1_{U_{G,\beta}}),(\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta},1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi})\}
. Let
F\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\subset\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
, so in particular,
F
is killed by the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
under
\star_{\overline{P}}
. Recall from Definition
2.3.5
or Definition
2.3.10
, that the morphism
\vartheta
is the
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-invariants of the composition of two maps:

\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\xrightarrow{\pi_{1}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\xrightarrow{\pi_{2}}\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{H}\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}

where the first map
\pi_{1}
is the composition of the first three bullet points in Definition
2.3.5
(or the analogous maps in Definition
2.3.10
), and
\pi_{2}
is the natural pullback map (as discussed at the end of §
6.4.4
). Here the action of
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
on
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
and
V_{\kappa}^{*}
is through the embedding
u^{-1}M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})u\subset M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, and we have twisted the maps in Definition
2.3.5
by
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}
.

With notation as in the proof of Proposition
5.3.8
, we wish to show
E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta(F)=0
for all
i=2,\dots,n
. Since
\pi_{2}
is equivariant for the action of
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
through the embedding
\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}\subset\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
, it suffices to prove
\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\pi_{1}(F)=0
.

Fix a basis
\{v_{l}\}
of
V_{\kappa}
and note that
F
can be viewed as a
M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant map
\widetilde{F}\colon V_{\kappa}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
. Then, with notation as in Proposition
5.3.8
, we have

\pi_{1}(F)=\sum_{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\{n+2,\dots,2n\}\\
\#T=j_{\tau_{0}}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{l,T}\left[p_{T}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(\xi\star_{\mathfrak{u}}\widetilde{F}(u^{-1}\cdot v_{l}))\right]

where
p_{T}
denotes the restriction of
u^{-1}\cdot x^{T}
to a polynomial on
U_{G,\beta}
. Note that
\frac{\partial p_{T}}{\partial x_{k}}
is the restriction of
u^{-1}\frac{\partial x^{T}}{\partial x_{k}}
to
U_{G,\beta}
, for all
k=n+1,\dots,2n
(since the change of coordinates given by the action of
u^{-1}
only shifts the coordinates by
x_{i}
for
i=2,\dots,n
). Here
\star_{\mathfrak{u}}
denotes the action in Theorem
7.0.2
or Lemma
7.4.1
. By acting on both sides of (
5.3.9
) by
u^{-1}
and using the density of
\mathscr{N}_{G}(U)
in
\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
, we have for any
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}
