\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}(p_{T}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}F^{\prime})=p_{T}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}F^{\prime})+\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial p_{T}}{\partial x_{k}}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,k}\star_{\overline{P}}F^{\prime}).

Then, using the fact that
\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{G}
kills
\xi\star_{\mathfrak{u}}\widetilde{F}(u^{-1}\cdot v_{l})
(because
F\in\mathcal{M}^{\dagger}_{G}
and
\xi
preserves overconvergent forms – see Example
7.4.8
), we have

\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\pi_{1}(F)=\sum_{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}T\subset\{n+2,\dots,2n\}\\
\#T=j_{\tau_{0}}\end{subarray}}\lambda_{l,T}\left(\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\frac{\partial p_{T}}{\partial x_{k}}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}\left[\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,k}\star_{\overline{P}}(\xi\star_{\mathfrak{u}}\widetilde{F}(u^{-1}\cdot v_{l}))\right]\right).

Now we consider the following map

g\colon S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}

given by
g(y\otimes z)=(u^{-1}\cdot\Phi_{\beta}(y))|_{U_{G,\beta}}\star_{\mathfrak{u}}(\xi\star_{\mathfrak{u}}\widetilde{F}(u^{-1}\cdot z))
. This is
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant in the following sense: for any
h\in M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, we have

g(h\cdot y\otimes h\cdot z)=(u^{-1}hu)\cdot g(y\otimes z).

Here we are using the fact that
(u^{-1}hu)\cdot\xi=\sigma_{0}^{[\chi]}(h)^{-1}\xi=\xi
. Note that there are elements
A_{i}\in S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}
(
i=2,\dots,n
) such that
g(A_{i})=\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\pi_{1}(F)
(because
\xi
is killed under the action of
\mathfrak{m}_{G}
).

On the other hand, let
W
be as in Proposition
5.3.8
, then we define a morphism

\displaystyle h\colon W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{N}^{\dagger}_{G}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{*}e_{i}

\displaystyle\mapsto\operatorname{Ad}(u^{-1})E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\pi_{1}(F)

and extending linearly. This is
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant in the following sense: for any
m\in M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
and
w\in W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}
, we have

h(m\cdot w)=(u^{-1}mu)\cdot h(w).

As in Proposition
5.3.8
, we therefore obtain a
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-equivariant morphism

\bar{q}\colon W\otimes\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}\to\left(S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}\right)/\operatorname{ker}(g).

We now claim that the natural map
S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}\twoheadrightarrow\left(S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}\right)/\operatorname{ker}(g)
is actually
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant. For this, it is enough to show that
\operatorname{ker}(g)
is stable under
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. Choose a basis
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{a}\}
of
S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}
such that
\{s_{1},\dots,s_{b}\}
is a basis of
\operatorname{ker}(g)
for some
1\leq b\leq a
. Let
\mu_{i}\colon S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}
denote the linear functional projecting to the coefficient of
s_{i}
. Fix
x\in\operatorname{ker}(g)
and consider the map

\displaystyle Q_{i}\colon M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle\mapsto\mu_{i}(m\cdot x).

Since
S_{-(j-1)}\otimes V_{\kappa}
is an algebraic representation, the maps
Q_{i}
are algebraic (i.e. they extend to algebraic morphisms
M_{H}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
). We already know that for
b+1\leq i\leq a
, the functions
Q_{i}
vanish on
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. But this subgroup is Zariski dense in
M_{H}
, hence we must have
Q_{i}=0
for all
i=b+1,\dots,a
. This implies
\operatorname{ker}(g)
is stable under
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. More generally, this argument shows that any
M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
-stable subspace of a finite-dimensional algebraic representation of
M_{H}
, is actually stable under
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

A similar argument shows that
\bar{q}
is in fact
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant. But we know from the proof of Proposition
5.3.8
that any such map must be zero; hence the map
h
is zero. This completes the proof of parts (1) and (2).

We now prove part (3). Firstly note that for any
x\in\Sigma
, any
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
, and any
z\in\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa_{x}^{*}}(U)
or
\mathscr{M}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa_{x}^{*}}(V)

E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa_{x},j_{x},\beta}(z)=E_{i,1}\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa_{x},j_{x},\beta}(1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}\star z)=0

by the compatibility relations in Remark
7.4.10
and the fact that the action of
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}}
preserves overconvergent forms (Example
7.4.8
). Furthermore, let
z\in\mathscr{M}^{(r,k),s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)
(resp.
z\in\mathscr{M}^{(r,k),s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V_{\gamma_{i}})
). Then unwinding the definitions in Definition
7.4.9
, we see that there exist integers
r^{\prime},k^{\prime}
and a rational number
\gamma>0
such that

•

\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(z)\in\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime}),\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)
(resp.
\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(z)\in\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime}),\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V_{\gamma})
).

•

For any
X\in\overline{\mathfrak{u}}_{H}
, the specialisation of
X\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(z)
at any point
x\in\Sigma
is zero.

By
[
BP21
, Proposition 6.3.3]
,
\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime}),\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}U)
(resp.
\mathscr{N}^{(r^{\prime},k^{\prime}),\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(\hat{\iota}^{-1}V_{\gamma})
) is a projective Banach
C\hat{\otimes}R
-module, where
C
denotes the sections of an appropriate quasi-compact open affinoid subspace of
\mathcal{X}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. This implies that that
X\star_{\overline{P}}\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(z)=0
. Indeed, this follows from the general fact: if
M
is a projective Banach
C\hat{\otimes}R
-module, then any element
m\in M
whose specialisations satisfy
m_{x}=0
for any
x\in\Sigma
must satisfy
m=0
. This can be proven by reducing to the case where
M
is orthonormalisable, and then to the setting where
M=C\hat{\otimes}R
, where the claim follows from Zariski density.
∎

We now consider the induced morphisms on cohomology. We consider three cases:

(1)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. In this case, we set
\mathscr{F}^{(r,k)}=\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
and
\mathscr{G}^{(r,k)}=\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
. We also let
\vartheta=\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
.

(2)

Let
(R,R^{+})
be a Tate affinoid algebra over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
and
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
satisfying the conditions in Proposition
7.4.11
(2). In this case, we set
\mathscr{F}^{(r,k)}=\mathscr{M}^{(r,k),s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
and
\mathscr{G}^{(r,k)}=\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}^{(r,k),\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
(for
k\geq s+1
). We also let
\vartheta=\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
.

(3)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and let
\chi=(\chi_{\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
be a finite-order character such that
\chi_{\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
for all
\tau\in\Psi
. In this case, we set
\mathscr{F}^{(r,k)}=\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
and
\mathscr{G}^{(r,k)}=\hat{\iota}_{*}\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
. We also let
\vartheta=\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}
.

Let
\mathfrak{U}=(U_{i})_{i\in I}
be an open cover of
\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
as in Lemma
6.4.5
(for both
\mathscr{F}^{(r,k)}
and
\mathscr{G}^{(r,k)}
) and let
\mathfrak{V}=(V_{i})_{i\in I}
denote the induced cover of
\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})
(i.e. we set
V_{i}=U_{i}\cap(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))
). Then since the morphisms
\vartheta
are functorial in
U\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and take overconvergent forms to overconvergent forms, we obtain a commutative diagram of Cech complexes

(7.4.12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{r,k}\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{F}^{(r,k)};\mathfrak{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{r,k}\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{F}^{(r,k)};\mathfrak{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{r,k}\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{G}^{(r,k)};\mathfrak{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{r,k}\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{G}^{(r,k)};\mathfrak{V}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vartheta}

By Remark
6.4.8
, this induces morphisms:

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\to R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}(\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right)

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,j,\beta}\colon R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n},s\operatorname{-an}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\to R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\to R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]})^{(-,\dagger)}

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi,\beta}\colon R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\to R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}(\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\to R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right)

in cases (1), (2), and (3) respectively. In all three compositions, the first map is induced from the commutative diagram (
7.4.12
) and the second map is just restriction along the open and closed embedding in Lemma
5.1.8
(note we have used the excision property in (
6.4.7
) for the first and third map, and by abuse of notation we also use the notation
\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
for its intersection with
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
).

7.4.3.
Overconvergent evaluation maps for classical and locally algebraic weights

For
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
or
\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
, let
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}
denote the Serre dual of
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}
, i.e

\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}-w_{M_{H}}^{\operatorname{max}}\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}-2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}}

where
\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}}
denotes the half-sum of positive roots of
H
not lying in
M_{H}
.

Definition 7.4.13
.

(1)

For
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
, let

R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{U}R\Gamma(U,\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}})=\varinjlim_{r,k}R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}^{(r,k)}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)

where the colimit is over all open neighbourhoods of
\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
inside
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
(with transition maps given by restriction).

(2)

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{X}_{R,s}
. Then we define

R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varinjlim_{r,k}R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}^{(r,k),\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right).

Note that
R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}=R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}
when
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
.

Remark 7.4.14
.

As explained in
[
Gra21
, §5.4]
, we have Serre duality pairings

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{n-1-i}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]})^{(-,\dagger)}\times\operatorname{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}

\displaystyle\to L

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{n-1-i}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]})^{(-,\dagger)}\times\operatorname{H}^{i}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}

\displaystyle\to R

for
i=0,\dots,n-1
, which are compatible with changing
R
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
and let
\chi\in\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
(see Definition
5.3.13
). Let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension containing
F^{\operatorname{cl}}(\chi)
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\beta}})
. All constructions are made over this finite extension
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, which we will once again omit from the notation. Let
\chi_{p}=(\chi_{p,\tau})\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to L^{\times}
denote the restriction of
\chi
to
\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathcal{O}^{\times}_{F_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tau}}}\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Note that, for any
\tau\in\Psi
,
\chi_{p,\tau}
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
because the conductor of
\chi
divides
\mathfrak{N}_{\beta}=\mathfrak{N}p^{\beta}
.

Recall from §
5.3.2
that we have a cohomology class

[\chi]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)

associated with the anticyclotomic character
\chi
. Let
\operatorname{res}[\chi]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}
denote the image of
[\chi]
under the natural restriction map
\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}
.

Definition 7.4.15
.

With notation as above, we consider the following
L
-linear maps:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}

\displaystyle\to L

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(\eta),\operatorname{res}[\chi]\rangle

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}

\displaystyle\to L

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}(\eta),\operatorname{res}[\chi]\rangle

where, in both cases,
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
denotes the Serre duality pairing in Remark
7.4.14
.

7.4.4.
Overconvergent evaluation maps for
p
-adic weights

Let
\mathcal{W}
denote the adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
parameterising continuous characters of
\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. This has an increasing cover
\{\mathcal{W}_{h}\}_{h\geq 1}
by quasi-compact open affinoid subpaces where the universal character of
\mathcal{W}_{h}
is
h
-analytic. We can (and do) assume that
\mathcal{W}_{1}
contains all classical weights, i.e. the characters of the form

\displaystyle\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle(x_{\tau})_{\tau\in\Psi}

\displaystyle\mapsto\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}x_{\tau}^{m_{\tau}}

where
m_{\tau}
are integers.

Let
\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})
denote the adic space over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
parameterising continuous characters of
\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F)
, where
F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F
denotes the abelian extension corresponding to

C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}F^{\times}\backslash\left(\mathbb{A}_{F,f}^{\times}/\mathbb{A}_{F^{+},f}^{\times}\cdot(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(p)}_{F^{+}}+\mathfrak{N}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{(p)})^{\times}\right)

via Artin reciprocity, where
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{(p)}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\text{ finite }\\
v\nmid p\end{subarray}}\mathcal{O}_{F,v}
and similarly for
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}^{(p)}
. Note that, in general,
F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}
can be smaller than the ring class field
F[\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}]
. We define
F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F
in the same way, but replacing
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}^{(p)}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{(p)}
with
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F^{+}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}
respectively. We have an injective map

(7.4.16)

\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\cong\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathcal{O}_{F_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tau}}}^{\times}\hookrightarrow C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}

where the second map is induced from the natural inclusion of ideles, and this subgroup is identified with
\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F_{\mathfrak{N}})
. One has a natural map

\jmath\colon\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})\to\mathcal{W}

given by sending a continuous character
C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}\to R^{\times}
to its restriction to
\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
via (
7.4.16
).

Lemma 7.4.17
.

The map
\jmath
is an étale torsor under the character group scheme
X^{*}(\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F))
. In particular, the map
\jmath
is finite étale.

Proof.

Recall our convention is that multiplication of characters is written additively. The action of the group scheme is given by

\displaystyle X^{*}(\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F))(S)\times\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})(S)

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})(S)

\displaystyle(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})\mapsto\chi_{1}+\chi_{2}

for an adic space
S\to\mathcal{W}
. This is clearly free and transitive, so we just need to show the map
\jmath
has sections locally in the étale topology. Let
S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^{+})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}
be an open affinoid, which corresponds to a continuous character
\chi\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to R^{\times}
. Consider the short exact sequence (in category
\operatorname{Ab}
of abelian groups):

0\to\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}\to\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F)\to 0.

This gives rise to the exact sequence:

(7.4.18)

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Ab}}\left(C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}},R^{\times}\right)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Ab}}\left(\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},R^{\times}\right)\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{Ab}}\left(\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F),R^{\times}\right)

which is functorial in
R
. Since
\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F)
is finite abelian group, there exist positive integers
m_{1},\dots,m_{k}
such that

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{Ab}}\left(\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}}/F),R^{\times}\right)\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}R^{\times}/\left(R^{\times}\right)^{m_{i}}

functorially in
R
. The image of the continuous character
\chi
under the second map in (
7.4.18
) therefore gives rise to a class

\operatorname{ob}(\chi)=(x_{i})\in\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}R^{\times}/\left(R^{\times}\right)^{m_{i}}.

Let
T=R[X_{1},\dots,X_{k}]/(X_{1}^{m_{1}}-x_{1},\dots,X_{k}^{m_{k}}-x_{k})
, and let
T^{+}\subset T
denote the integral closure of
R^{+}
under the map
R\to T
. Then
\operatorname{Spa}(T,T^{+})\to S
is finite étale, and the image of
\operatorname{ob}(\chi)
under the map

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}R^{\times}/\left(R^{\times}\right)^{m_{i}}\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}T^{\times}/\left(T^{\times}\right)^{m_{i}}

is zero. This implies that
\chi
can be lifted to a homomorphism
C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}\to T^{\times}
. It is automatically continuous because its restriction
\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to T^{\times}
is continuous (and
\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
is an open subgroup of
C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}
). This implies that
\jmath
has a section over
\operatorname{Spa}(T,T^{+})
as required.
∎

We can view the characters introduced in Definition
5.3.13
as points on the adic space
\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})
. More precisely, for any
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
, there is an injective map

\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})(\mathbb{C}_{p}),\quad\chi\mapsto\hat{\chi}

where
\hat{\chi}\colon C_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
is the continuous character defined by the formula:

\hat{\chi}(z)=\iota_{p}(\chi(z))\cdot z_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}}^{\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}}}z_{\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}_{0}}}^{j_{\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}}\cdot\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}z_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}z_{\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}}}^{j_{\tau_{0}}},\quad\quad z\in\mathbb{A}_{F,f}^{\times}.

Here, for an embedding
\sigma\colon F\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
,
z_{\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}}
denotes the component of
z
at the prime
\mathfrak{p}_{\sigma}
above
p
determined by the embedding
F\xrightarrow{\sigma}\mathbb{C}\xrightarrow{\iota_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.

We let
\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})_{h}=\jmath^{-1}(\mathcal{W}_{h})
which, by the above lemma, is a quasi-compact open affinoid subspace. Let
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
be a finite extension containing
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\beta}})
and
\iota_{p}(F_{\mathfrak{N}})
, and let
\Omega=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{\Omega},\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}^{+})
be the adic spectrum of Tate affinoid adic space over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
. For
h\geq 1
, we set

\Omega_{h}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Omega\times_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})_{h}=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}\hat{\otimes}_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})_{h}})=\operatorname{Spa}(\mathscr{O}_{\Omega_{h}}).

Let
s\geq\operatorname{max}(h,\beta)
and let
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}^{\times}
be a
s
-analytic character satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
. Let
\chi\colon\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F)\to\mathscr{O}_{\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})_{h}}^{\times}
denote the universal character. We let
\widetilde{\kappa}
and
\widetilde{\chi}
denote the characters
\kappa
and
\chi
respectively, viewed as homomorphisms valued in
\mathscr{O}_{\Omega_{h}}^{\times}
. Note that
\widetilde{\kappa}
and
\jmath(\widetilde{\chi})
are both
s
-analytic. We let

\displaystyle j\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{O}_{\Omega_{h}}^{\times}

\displaystyle(x_{\tau})

\displaystyle\mapsto\widetilde{\kappa}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}(x_{\tau_{0}})\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\jmath(\widetilde{\chi})(x_{\tau})

which is
s
-analytic.

We introduce some notation.

Notation 7.4.19
.

Let
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}\subset\Omega_{h}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
denote a subset satisfying the following properties:

(1)

Let
\kappa_{x}\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
and
j_{x}\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
denote the specialisations of
\widetilde{\kappa}
and
j
at
x\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
respectively. We assume that there exists a finite-order character
\chi_{x,p}\colon\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}_{p}^{\times}
which is trivial on
\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}(1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p})
such that
(\kappa_{x},j_{x}-\chi_{x,p})\in\mathcal{E}
.

(2)

We assume that the projection of
x\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
to a point in
\mathcal{W}(\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty})(\mathbb{C}_{p})
lies in
\Sigma_{\kappa_{x},j_{x}-\chi_{x,p}}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
. We let
\chi_{x}
denote the corresponding character (so that
\hat{\chi}_{x}
is equal to the specialisation of
\chi
at
x
).

We also let
\Sigma\subset\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
denote a subset such that
\chi_{x}
has conductor dividing
\mathfrak{N}
for any
x\in\Sigma
(which implies that
\chi_{x,p}
is trivial). Note that one automatically has
\Sigma\subset\Omega_{1}(\mathbb{C}_{p})
.

Lemma 7.4.20
.

There exists a cohomology class
[\chi]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\widetilde{\kappa}}^{[j],\vee}\right)^{(+,\dagger)}
such that: for any
x\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
, the image of
[\chi]
under the specialisation map

\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\widetilde{\kappa}}^{[j],\vee}\right)^{(+,\dagger)}\to\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa_{x}}^{[j_{x}],\vee}\right)^{(+,\dagger)}

is equal to
\operatorname{res}[\chi_{x}]
. If
\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
is Zariski dense in
\Omega_{h}
, then
[\chi]
is uniquely determined by this specialisation property.

Proof.

This follows from the construction in
[
Gra21
, §7]
. More precisely, let
(\mathbf{R},h_{\mathbf{R}})
denote the PEL Shimura datum as in §7.1 in
op.cit.
, and let
\mathcal{R}_{s}\subset R^{\operatorname{an}}
denote the affinoid subgroup

\mathcal{R}_{s}=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\left(1+\mathcal{B}_{s}\right)\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\left(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\left(1+\mathcal{B}_{s}\right)\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\left(1+\mathcal{B}_{s}\right)\right).

Let
\Delta
denote the adic Shimura variety over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
associated with
(\mathbf{R},h_{\mathbf{R}})
of level
C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{det}(U)\subset\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
, where
U\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
denotes the level of
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
and
\operatorname{det}\colon\mathbf{H}\to\mathbf{R}
is determinant map in §7.1 of
op.cit.
. The compact open subgroup
C
decomposes as
C=C^{p}C_{p}
with
C^{p}\subset\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
C_{p}\subset\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. By Shimura reciprocity and the fact that
L
contains
\mathbb{Q}_{p}(\mu_{p^{\beta}})
, we see that there is a finite unramified extension
L^{\prime}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
such that

\Delta_{\Phi}=\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/C

where
\Phi=L\cdot L^{\prime}\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. If
F_{p}\in\Gal(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{ab}}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denotes the geometric Frobenius corresponding
p\in\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}
under the Artin reciprocity map of local class field theory, then (after choosing a lift to
\Gal(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})
) there is an integer
m\geq 1
such that
F^{m}_{p}
generates the cyclic Galois group
\Gal(\Phi/L)
. By Shimura reciprocity, the action of
F^{m}_{p}
on
[x]\in\Delta(\Phi)
(with
x\in\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
) is given by

F_{p}^{m}\cdot[x]=[y\cdot x]

where
y\in\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
denotes the point which is trivial outside the
p
-component, and at the
p
-component is given by
y_{p}=(y_{0};y_{1,\tau},y_{2,\tau})_{\tau\in\Psi}
with
y_{0}=p^{m}
,
y_{1,\tau_{0}}=p^{m}
,
y_{2,\tau_{0}}=1
, and
y_{i,\tau}=1
for all
i=1,2
and
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
.

The torsor
R_{\operatorname{dR}}^{\operatorname{an}}
has a reduction of structure
{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{HT},s}}
which can similarly be described as

{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{HT},s,\Phi}}=\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/C^{p}\times\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\right)\times\mathcal{R}_{s,\Phi}\right]/C_{p}

where
C_{p}
acts diagonally with
C_{p}
acting on
\mathcal{R}_{s,\Phi}
through the map
C_{p}\to\mathcal{R}_{s,\Phi}
,
z\mapsto z^{-1}
. The action of
F_{p}^{m}
on
[x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}]\in{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{HT},s}}(\Phi)
(with
x\in\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,
x^{\prime}\in\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
,
x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{R}_{s}(\Phi)
) is given by

F_{p}^{m}\cdot[x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}]=[x,y_{p}\cdot x^{\prime},F_{p}^{m}(x^{\prime\prime})]

where
F_{p}^{m}(x^{\prime\prime})
denotes the natural Galois action.

Let
\lambda\colon\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\mathscr{O}_{\Omega_{h}}^{\times}
denote the unique character such that
\lambda(z_{1},z_{2})=\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}(z_{2}/z_{1})
, where
\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}
denotes the unique
p
-adic Hecke character on
\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1)
satisfying
\widetilde{\chi}=\widetilde{\chi}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{N}
. Let
f\colon{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{HT},s,\Omega_{h,\Phi}}}\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\operatorname{an}}_{\Omega_{h,\Phi}}
denote the (well-defined) function given by

f([x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}])=\lambda(x)\lambda(x^{\prime})\lambda(x^{\prime\prime})

which makes sense because
\lambda
restricted to
R(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is
s
-analytic (because
\jmath(\chi)
is). Then
f
corresponds to a class
[\lambda]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(\Delta_{\Omega_{h,\Phi}},\mathcal{F}_{\lambda^{-1}})
, where
\mathcal{F}_{\lambda^{-1}}
denotes the line bundle associated with the character
\lambda^{-1}\colon\mathcal{R}_{s}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\operatorname{an}}
. Note
\lambda(y_{p})=1
because
L
contains
\iota_{p}(F_{\mathfrak{N}})
and
\widetilde{\chi}
is trivial on
F^{\times}\mathbb{A}_{F^{+},f}^{\times}(\widehat{\mathcal{O}}^{(p)}_{F^{+}}+\mathfrak{N}\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{F}^{(p)})^{\times}
. By the description of the Galois actions above, we therefore see that
[\lambda]
descends to a class
[\lambda]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(\Delta_{\Omega_{h}},\mathcal{F}_{\lambda^{-1}})
. As explained in
[
Gra21
, §7.3]
, there is a natural map

\operatorname{det}\colon{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{H,\operatorname{HT},s}}\to{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{R}_{\operatorname{HT},s}}

and we define
[\chi]\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{det}^{*}[\lambda]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\widetilde{\kappa}}^{[j],\vee}\right)^{(+,\dagger)}
(note that
\sigma_{\widetilde{\kappa}}^{[j],\vee}=\lambda^{-1}\circ\operatorname{det}
).

One can easily verify that this class interpolates
\operatorname{res}[\chi_{x}]
for
x\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
. The uniqueness claim follows from the fact that (after possibly enlarging
L
) we can write the dagger space
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})^{\dagger}
as a disjoint union of connected components over which the sheaf
\sigma_{\widetilde{\kappa}}^{[j],\vee}
trivialises.
∎

We now define the overconvergent evaluation map in this setting.

Definition 7.4.21
.

With notation as above, suppose that
\Sigma
is Zariski dense in
\Omega_{h}
(or equivalently, Zariski dense in
\Omega_{1}
). We define the following
\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}
-linear map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,h,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n},s\operatorname{-an}}\left(\kappa^{*};\beta\right)^{(-,\dagger)}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{O}_{\Omega_{h}}

\displaystyle\eta

\displaystyle\mapsto\langle\vartheta^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\widetilde{\kappa},j,\beta}(\widetilde{\eta}),[\chi]\rangle

where
\widetilde{\eta}
denotes the pullback of
\eta
to a class in
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n},s\operatorname{-an}}\left(\widetilde{\kappa}^{*};\beta\right)^{(-,\dagger)}
and
[\chi]
denotes the class in Lemma
7.4.20
. Note that the assumptions in Proposition
7.4.11
(3) are satisfied.

7.4.5.
Compatibilities

One can easily verify that
\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,h,\beta}
is compatible with changing
s
and
h
. In particular, by passing to the limit over
s
and
h
, one obtains a
\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}
-linear map

\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,\operatorname{la}}_{\kappa,\beta}\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n},\operatorname{la}}\left(\kappa^{*};\beta\right)^{(-,\dagger)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\varprojlim_{s}\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n},s\operatorname{-an}}\left(\kappa^{*};\beta\right)^{(-,\dagger)}\to\mathscr{D}^{\operatorname{la}}\left(\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F),\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}\right)

where
\mathscr{D}^{\operatorname{la}}\left(\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F),\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}\right)=\varprojlim_{h}\mathscr{O}_{\Omega_{h}}
denotes the
\mathscr{O}_{\Omega}
-module of locally analytic distributions on
\Gal(F_{\mathfrak{N}p^{\infty}}/F)
. Furthermore, one has the interpolation property:

\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,s\operatorname{-an}}_{\kappa,h,\beta}(\eta)(x)=\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa_{x},j_{x}-\chi_{x,p},\chi_{x},\beta}(\eta_{x})

for any
x\in\Sigma^{\prime}_{\beta}
, where
\eta_{x}
denotes the image of
\eta
under the specialisation map
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n},s\operatorname{-an}}\left(\kappa^{*};\beta\right)^{(-,\dagger)}\to\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}\left(\kappa_{x}^{*};\beta\right)^{(-,\dagger)}
.

8.
Hecke operators and higher Coleman theory

The construction in the previous section will be used for the
p
-adic
L
-function. However to find the suitable test data and prove the interpolation property, we need to understand the action of Hecke operators on the space of overconvergent forms.

8.1.
Notations for the flag variety

Consider the following flag varieties
\mathtt{FL}^{G}=P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\backslash G^{\operatorname{an}}=\mathcal{P}_{G}\backslash\mathcal{G}
and
\mathtt{FL}^{H}=P_{H}^{\operatorname{an}}\backslash H^{\operatorname{an}}=\mathcal{P}_{H}\backslash\mathcal{H}
which are adic spaces over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Recall from Definition
5.1.6
that we have fixed neat compact open subgroups
K^{p}\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
U^{p}\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. To simplify notation in this section, we will let
\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}
(resp.
\mathcal{S}_{H,K_{p}}
) denote the adic Shimura variety for
\mathbf{G}
(resp.
\mathbf{H}
) over
\operatorname{Spa}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p})
of level
K^{p}K_{p}\subset\mathbf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
(resp.
U^{p}K_{p}\subset\mathbf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
) for any compact open subgroup
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(resp.
K_{p}\subset H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
). In particular,
\mathcal{S}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})=\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}
for
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, and similarly for
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
.

As usual, let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer and
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
a finite extension containing
\mu_{p^{\beta}}
. We will often consider the base-change of the above flag varieties and Shimura varieties to
\operatorname{Spa}(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
but omit this from the notation. Recall that for any compact open
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
K_{p}\subset H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
) we have truncated Hodge–Tate period maps (which we simply view as maps of topological spaces):

\displaystyle\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}\colon\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{FL}^{G}/K_{p}

\displaystyle\text{(resp. }\pi_{\operatorname{HT},H,K_{p}}\colon\mathcal{S}_{H,K_{p}}

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{FL}^{H}/K_{p}\text{ )}

which we will often denote simply by
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}
when the context is clear.

Recall from
[
Gra21
, Definition 3.0.1]
that
\mathtt{FL}^{G}\cong\mathbb{P}^{2n-1}
and in coordinates the right-action of
g\in G^{\operatorname{an}}
(denoted
\star
) is given by

[x_{0}:x_{1}:\cdots:x_{2n-1}]\star g=[x_{0}:x_{1}:\cdots:x_{2n-1}]\cdot{{}^{t}g_{\tau_{0}}^{-1}}

where the right hand side is the usual action on row vectors and
g_{\tau_{0}}\in\operatorname{GL}_{2n}^{\operatorname{an}}
denotes the
\tau_{0}
-component of
g
. We have a similar description for
\mathtt{FL}^{H}
. Finally, we let
]C^{?}_{w}[_{m,k}\subset\mathtt{FL}^{?}
(and their partially compactified versions) denote the tubes of Bruhat cells as in
[
Gra21
, Definition 3.2.3]
.

Definition 8.1.1
.

For an integer
m\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{Q}_{m}\subset\mathcal{G}
denote the subgroup which coincides with
\mathcal{P}_{G}
outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is equal to the subgroup of block matrices

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&B\\
C&D\end{array}\right)

with
A\in\mathcal{GL}_{n+1}
,
B\in\mathcal{M}_{(n+1)\times(n-1)}
,
C\in\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1)\times(n+1)}
and
D\in\mathcal{GL}_{n-1}
. Here
\mathcal{M}_{r\times s}
denotes the adic group scheme with
\mathcal{M}_{r\times s}(\operatorname{Spa}(A,A^{+}))
equal to the group of
r\times s
-matrices with coefficients in
A^{+}
, and
\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ}
is the “open disc” defined in
[
Gra21
, Definition 3.2.1]
.

We have the following useful lemma.

Lemma 8.1.2
.

For any integer
k\geq 1
and compact open subgroup
K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(resp.
K_{p}\subset H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
), set
\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}^{-1}\left(]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\right)\subset\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}
(resp.
\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\pi_{\operatorname{HT},H,K_{p}}^{-1}\left(]C^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\right)\subset\mathcal{S}_{H,K_{p}}
). Then

(1)

For
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
, we have

\overline{\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})}=\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}

where
\overline{\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})}
denotes the closure of
\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
inside
\mathcal{S}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})=\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. In particular
\{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}\}_{k\geq 1}
is a cofinal system of (quasi-Stein) open neighbourhoods of
\overline{\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})}
.

(2)

Let
K_{p}=K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
and let
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
denote the pullback of
\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
along the open and closed embedding
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\subset\mathcal{X}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
(see Lemma
5.1.8
). Then

\overline{\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}=\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}

where
\overline{\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}
denotes the closure of
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
inside
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. In particular
\{\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}\}_{k\geq 1}
is a cofinal system of (quasi-Stein) open neighbourhoods of
\overline{\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}
.

(3)

Let
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Then we have

\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})=\bigcap_{m\geq 1}\pi^{-1}_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}\left(\mathcal{P}_{G}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{G}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}\right).

Proof.

Let
x\colon\operatorname{Spa}(F,\mathcal{O}_{F})\to\mathcal{S}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
be a rank one point. Then
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}(x)
can be described as follows. The point
x
corresponds to a tuple
(A,\lambda,i,\eta^{p})
(a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme over
F
with
K^{p}
-level structure) and flags

0=C_{0,\tau}\subset C_{1,\tau}\subset\cdots\subset C_{2n,\tau}=A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}],\quad\quad\tau\in\Psi

of finite-flat group schemes satisfying the conditions in Definition
5.1.7
(1). Assume without loss of generality that
F=\widehat{F^{\operatorname{sep}}}
is equal to the completion of its separable closure. We know that
(A,\lambda,i,\eta^{p})
extends to a
\boldsymbol{\Psi}
-unitary abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}
over
\mathcal{O}_{F}
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{i,\tau}
denote the Zariski closure of
C_{i,\tau}
inside
\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta}]
(which are finite flat). Let
T_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)=T_{p}(A[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\infty}])=\varprojlim_{\beta^{\prime}\geq 1}\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta^{\prime}}](F)=\varprojlim_{\beta^{\prime}\geq 1}\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta^{\prime}}](\mathcal{O}_{F})
denote the corresponding Tate modules, and set
V_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)=T_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Since
F
is separably closed, we can choose bases
\{e_{1,\tau},\dots,e_{2n,\tau}\}
of
T_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)
which are compatible with the flags
C_{\bullet,\tau}
modulo
p^{\beta}
(i.e.
e_{1,\tau}
modulo
p^{\beta}
generates
C_{1,\tau}(F)=\mathcal{C}_{1,\tau}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
etc.). Any other such choice of basis differs from this one by elements of the depth
p^{\beta}
upper-triangular Iwahori subgroups of
\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}(T_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A))
.

On the other hand, for any
\beta^{\prime}\geq 1
, we have “dlog” morphisms

\operatorname{dlog}_{\beta^{\prime},\tau}\colon\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}^{\beta^{\prime}}](\mathcal{O}_{F})\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau}/p^{\beta^{\prime}}

which are compatible as
\beta^{\prime}
varies. Hence, we obtain morphisms
\operatorname{dlog}_{\tau}\colon T_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)\to\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau}
and

\operatorname{HT}_{\tau}\colon V_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}F=T_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}F\xrightarrow{\operatorname{dlog}_{\tau}\otimes 1}\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}F=\omega_{A^{D},\tau}.

The morphisms
\operatorname{HT}_{\tau}
are surjective and
F
-linear, and isomorphisms when
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
by the signature condition. Let
\{f_{1,\tau},\dots,f_{2n,\tau}\}
be a
F
-basis of
V_{\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}}(A)\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}F
respecting the filtration given by
\operatorname{HT}_{\tau}
(i.e. we require that
\operatorname{HT}_{\tau_{0}}(f_{2,\tau_{0}}),\dots,\operatorname{HT}_{\tau_{0}}(f_{2n,\tau_{0}})
generate
\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}}
). Any other choice of basis differs by an element of
P_{G}(F)
(ignoring the similitude factor). Then
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}(x)\in\mathtt{FL}^{G}(F)/K_{p}=P_{G}(F)\backslash G(F)/K_{p}
is represented by the element
g=(1;g_{\tau})\in G(F)=F^{\times}\times\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\operatorname{GL}_{2n}(F)
satisfying

(e_{1,\tau},\dots,e_{2n,\tau})=(f_{1,\tau},\dots,f_{2n,\tau})\cdot g_{\tau}

for all
\tau\in\Psi
.

We now prove parts (1) and (3). Firstly, if
x\in\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
then we can choose the bases
\{f_{i,\tau}\}
above such that
f_{i,\tau_{0}}=e_{i-1,\tau_{0}}
for
i=2,\dots,n+1
,
f_{1,\tau_{0}}=e_{n+1,\tau_{0}}
and
f_{i,\tau_{0}}=e_{i,\tau_{0}}
for
i=n+2,\dots,2n
(because
\mathcal{C}_{n,\tau_{0}}
is étale and
\mathcal{C}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}/\mathcal{C}_{n,\tau_{0}}
is multiplicative). Hence, we see that
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}(x)
is represented by
w_{n}
. On the other hand, suppose
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}(x)
is represented by
w_{n}
; so we can find bases above such that
g=w_{n}
. This means that
f_{i,\tau}=e_{i,\tau}
for all
(i,\tau)
with
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
, and
f_{i,\tau_{0}}=e_{i-1,\tau_{0}}
for
i=2,\dots,n+1
,
f_{1,\tau_{0}}=e_{n+1,\tau_{0}}
and
f_{i,\tau_{0}}=e_{i,\tau_{0}}
for
i=n+2,\dots,2n
. Then we see that

\{\operatorname{dlog}_{\tau_{0}}(e_{1,\tau_{0}}),\dots,\operatorname{dlog}_{\tau_{0}}(e_{n,\tau_{0}})\}

give elements of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{n,\tau_{0}}^{D}}
and their image in
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau_{0}}/p^{\beta}
span a free
\mathcal{O}_{F}/p^{\beta}
-subspace of rank
n
. This can only happen if
\operatorname{deg}(\mathcal{C}_{n,\tau_{0}}^{D})=n\beta
, which coincides with its rank. This implies
\mathcal{C}_{n,\tau_{0}}
is étale. Similarly, the fact that
\operatorname{HT}_{\tau_{0}}(e_{n+1,\tau_{0}})=\operatorname{HT}_{\tau_{0}}(f_{1,\tau_{0}})=0
implies that
\mathcal{C}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}/\mathcal{C}_{n,\tau_{0}}
is multiplicative. Putting this altogether, we see that

\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})^{\operatorname{rk}1}=\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{G}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{G}\cdot w_{n}\cdot K_{p}\right)^{\operatorname{rk}1}=\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}\right)^{\operatorname{rk}1}.

The claim in part (1) easily follows from this.

We now prove part (3). Suppose that
x\in\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})^{\operatorname{rk}1}
. Then, since the
p
-rank of
\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}]^{D}
must be
\geq n-1
, we can find a
p
-divisible group
\mathcal{D}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\subset\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}]
with
\mathcal{D}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}[p^{\beta^{\prime}}]=\mathcal{C}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}
and
\mathcal{E}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\infty}]/\mathcal{D}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}
étale. This implies that we have a quotient
\omega_{A^{D},\tau_{0}}\twoheadrightarrow\omega_{\mathcal{E}^{D}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}F
of rank
n-1
, and hence we may take
f_{i,\tau_{0}}=e_{i,\tau_{0}}
for
i=n+2,\dots,2n
. Furthermore, we may take
f_{i,\tau_{0}}
(
i=1,\dots,n+1
) to be in the
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-span of
\{e_{1,\tau_{0}},\dots,e_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\}
. This implies that
g\in\bigcap_{m\geq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{m}(F)
. On the other hand, suppose that matrix
g
representing
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}(x)
is in
\bigcap_{m\geq 1}\mathcal{Q}_{m}(F)
. Then we see that
f_{i,\tau}
(
i=1,\dots,n+1
) is in the
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-span of
\{e_{1,\tau_{0}},\dots,e_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\}
. This implies that
\{\operatorname{dlog}_{\tau_{0}}(e_{1,\tau_{0}}),\dots,\operatorname{dlog}_{\tau_{0}}(e_{n+1,\tau_{0}})\}
generate a free submodule of
\omega_{\mathcal{A}^{D},\tau_{0}}/p^{\beta}
of rank
n
. This forces
\omega_{\mathcal{C}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}^{D}}
to have rank
n
and hence

\operatorname{deg}\left(\left(\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]/\mathcal{C}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\right)^{D}\right)=(2n-1)\beta-\operatorname{deg}(\mathcal{C}^{D}_{n+1,\tau_{0}})=(n-1)\beta.

This implies that
\mathcal{A}[\mathfrak{p}_{\tau_{0}}^{\beta}]/\mathcal{C}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}
is étale and part (3) follows.

The proof of part (2) is very similar to that of part (1) and we leave the details to the reader.
∎

8.2.
Topological Hecke correspondences

We continue with the notation introduced in the previous section.

Definition 8.2.1
.

Let
T^{G,-}\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
(resp.
T^{H,-}\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
) denote the submonoid of elements
t\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
which satisfy
v_{p}(\alpha(t))\leq 0
for any positive root
\alpha
of
G
(resp.
H
). For any
t\in T^{G,-}
(resp.
t\in T^{H,-}
) we let

\displaystyle\operatorname{min}_{G}(t)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{min}\{-v_{p}(\alpha(t)):\alpha\in\Phi_{G}^{+}\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{max}_{G}(t)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{max}\{-v_{p}(\alpha(t)):\alpha\in\Phi_{G}^{+}\}

\displaystyle\text{ ( resp. }\operatorname{min}_{H}(t)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{min}\{-v_{p}(\alpha(t)):\alpha\in\Phi_{H}^{+}\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{max}_{H}(t)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{max}\{-v_{p}(\alpha(t)):\alpha\in\Phi_{H}^{+}\}\text{ ) }

where
\Phi_{G}^{+}
and
\Phi_{H}^{+}
denote the positive roots of
G
and
H
respectively.

For brevity and to ease notation, we place ourselves in one of the following two cases:

(8.2.2)

(\mathscr{G},g,K_{p},\mathcal{Z})\in\left\{(G,w_{n},K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})),(H,\operatorname{id},K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta}))\right\}

where, by abuse of notation, we write
\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
for its intersection with
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
. For an integer
m\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{Z}_{m}
denote the closure of
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}^{-1}(\mathcal{P}_{G}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{G}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p})
(resp.
\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},m}
) in
\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}}
in the case
\mathscr{G}=G
(resp.
\mathscr{G}=H
).

Let
t\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}
. Then we can consider the following correspondence

(8.2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where
K_{p}^{\prime}=tK_{p}t^{-1}\cap K_{p}
,
p_{1}
is the forgetful map, and
p_{2}
is induced from right multiplication by
t
. Note that
p_{1}
and
p_{2}
are finite étale. The first property we need for (
8.2.3
) is compatibility with support conditions.

Lemma 8.2.4
.

For any integer
m\geq 1
, we have
p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})\subset\mathcal{Z}_{m}
. Furthermore, we have
p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathcal{Z}
.

Proof.

We first consider the case
\mathscr{G}=G
. Write
t=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}t_{1}&\\
&t_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with
t_{1}
(resp.
t_{2}
) a
(n+1)\times(n+1)
(resp.
(n-1)\times(n-1)
) diagonal matrix. Let
m\geq 1
be an integer. Then there exists an integer
r\geq 0
such that

t_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{B}_{m+r}^{\circ}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1)\times(n+1)}t_{1}\subset\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1)\times(n+1)}.

Furthermore, since
t\in T^{G,-}
, we see that
t_{2}\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1)\times(n+1)}t_{1}^{-1}\subset\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1)\times(n+1)}
. For any
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}A&\\
C&D\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\mathcal{Q}_{m+r}
(with notation as in Definition
8.1.1
), we have

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\\
C&D\end{array}\right)=tt^{-1}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\\
C&D\end{array}\right)tt^{-1}=t\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}t_{1}^{-1}At_{1}&\\
t_{2}^{-1}Ct_{1}&t_{2}^{-1}Dt_{2}\end{array}\right)t^{-1}=p\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A^{\prime}&\\
C^{\prime}&1\end{array}\right)t^{-1}

for some
p\in P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}
,
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{GL}_{n+1}
and
C^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ}\mathcal{M}_{(n-1)\times(n+1)}
. Here we have used the fact that
\operatorname{GL}_{n+1}^{\operatorname{an}}
modulo any parabolic subgroup is proper, so we may assume
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{GL}_{n+1}
. By the above inclusion and the Iwahori factorisation of
\mathcal{Q}_{m}
, we therefore see that

P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m+r}\subset P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}t^{-1}

and so
P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m+r}K_{p}\subset P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}t^{-1}K_{p}
.

On the other hand, we also have
\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset\mathcal{Q}_{m}t^{-1}K_{p}
because
t\in T^{G,-}
. Indeed, by the Iwahori factorisation of
K_{p}
, we may replace
K_{p}
with lower-triangular matrices. This, and the inclusions above, imply that

P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}t^{-1}K_{p}\subset P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}t^{-1}K_{p}\subset P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}.

By pulling back along
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},G,K_{p}}
and taking closures, we therefore see that
\mathcal{Z}_{m+r}\subset p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})\subset\mathcal{Z}_{m}
, which gives the first claim. The second claim follows from taking the intersection over
m\geq 1
.

The case
\mathscr{G}=H
is even simpler. Indeed, one can easily show that

]C^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}[_{k+r,k+r}K_{p}\subset]C^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}[_{k,k}(K_{p}t^{-1}K_{p})\subset]C^{H}_{\operatorname{id}}[_{k,k}K_{p}

for any
r\geq\operatorname{max}_{H}(t)
.
∎

We now consider an overconvergent version of the above correspondence. We first note the following lemma:

Lemma 8.2.5
.

Let
t\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}
and
p_{1},p_{2}
the maps in the correspondence (
8.2.3
). Let
d=\operatorname{max}_{\mathscr{G}}(t)
and
k\geq 1
an integer. Then

(1)

p_{2}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d})\subset\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}

(2)

There exists an integer
m\geq k
such that
p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})\subset\mathcal{U}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}^{\mathscr{G}}

(3)

\mathcal{U}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}^{\mathscr{G}}\subset p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})
is an open and closed subspace.

In particular, the induced map
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{U}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}^{\mathscr{G}}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
is finite étale.

Proof.

For part (1), we have

]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}t=]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}tt^{-1}K_{p}^{\prime}t\subset]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}tK_{p},\quad\quad k\geq 1,

so it suffices to show that
]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k+d,k+d}t\subset]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}
. But this is clear.

For part (2), we first prove the claim for
\mathscr{G}=G
. For any integer
m\geq 1
, set
A_{m}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{P}_{G}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{G}\mathcal{Q}_{m}\subset\mathtt{FL}^{G}
. Then it suffices to show that for
m\gg k
, one has

]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap A_{m}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}.

Note that
A_{m}K_{p}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}K_{p}\bigcup\cup_{w<w_{n}}]X^{G}_{w}[
(with notation as in
[
Gra21
, Definition 3.1.1]
) and we have
]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]X^{G}_{w}[t^{-1}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]X^{G}_{w}[=\varnothing
for any
w<w_{n}
(because
t\in T^{G,-}
). In addition to this, suppose that we have

[x_{0}:\cdots:x_{2n-1}]\in]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}K_{p}t^{-1}.

Let
\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},\dots,t_{2n})
denote the
\tau_{0}
-component of
t
. Then there exist
a_{1},\dots,a_{2n},a_{1}^{\prime},\dots,a_{2n}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with the following properties:

•

a_{n+1},a_{n+1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
and
a_{n+2},\dots,a_{2n},a_{n+2}^{\prime},\dots,a_{2n}^{\prime}\in p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}

•

For
i=1,\dots,n
, we have
x_{i-1}\in a_{i}+\mathcal{B}_{k}

•

x_{n}\in(a_{n+1}+\mathcal{B}_{k}^{\circ})\cap(a_{n+1}^{\prime}+\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ})

•

For
i=n+2,\dots,2n
, we have
x_{i-1}\in(a_{i}+\mathcal{B}_{k}^{\circ})\cap(t_{i}t_{n+1}^{-1}a_{i}^{\prime}+\mathcal{B}_{m}^{\circ})
.

This implies that

(x_{0},\cdots,x_{2n-1})\in(a_{1},\dots,a_{n},a^{\prime}_{n+1},t_{n+2}t_{n+1}^{-1}a_{n+2}^{\prime},\dots,t_{2n}t_{n+1}^{-1}a_{2n}^{\prime})+(\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{0},\dots,\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{0},\mathcal{B}^{\circ}_{m},\dots,\mathcal{B}^{\circ}_{m})

with
n
lots of
\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
, and hence
]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}K_{p}^{\prime}
. So to prove (2) in the case
\mathscr{G}=G
, it suffices to show that

]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}.

But, as long as we take
m\geq k+\operatorname{max}_{G}(t)
, then this follows from a similar explicit calculation above (take
a_{i}^{\prime}=0
for
i\neq n+1
). This completes the proof of (2) in the case
\mathscr{G}=G
. The proof of part (2) for
\mathscr{G}=H
is simpler. Indeed, one can easily show that
p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},m})\subset\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}
for any
m\gg k
(depending on
\operatorname{max}_{H}(t)
).

We now prove part (3). For
\mathscr{G}=G
, we simply note that

]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}=\bigcup_{\underline{a}}\left(\underline{a}+(\mathcal{B}_{k},\dots,\mathcal{B}_{k},0,\mathcal{B}_{k}^{\circ},\dots,\mathcal{B}_{k}^{\circ})\right)

where the
0
is in the
(n+1)
-th place and the union is over tuples
(a_{1},\dots,a_{n},1,a_{n+2},\dots,a_{2n})
, with
a_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{k}\mathbb{Z}
(resp.
a_{i}\in p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\operatorname{max}(\beta,k)}\mathbb{Z}
) for
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
(resp.
i\in\{n+2,\dots,2n\}
). Clearly there are only finitely many such tuples, and this is a disjoint union of open and closed subspaces. Furthermore, one easily sees that
]C_{w_{n}}^{G}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}
is a union over the cosets for which

a_{i}\in p^{\beta^{\prime}_{i}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\operatorname{max}(\beta^{\prime}_{i},k)}\mathbb{Z}\subset p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\operatorname{max}(\beta,k)}\mathbb{Z},\quad\quad\beta^{\prime}_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\beta+v_{p}(t_{i}t_{n+1}^{-1})

for all
i\in\{n+2,\dots,2n\}
. This proves the claim – the proof of (3) in the case
\mathscr{G}=H
follows an identical argument. Finally, since
p_{1}\colon p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
is finite étale, the induced morphism
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
is also finite étale (by part (3)).
∎

We now consider the overconvergent version of the Hecke correpondence. Let
t\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}
and
d=\operatorname{max}_{\mathscr{G}}(t)
. Then for any
k\geq 1
, Lemma
8.2.5
implies that we have a correspondence:

(8.2.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+d}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

This is compatible with the correspondence in (
8.2.3
) in the obvious way, i.e. we have a commutative (but often not Cartesian) diagram relating the correspondences (
8.2.3
) and (
8.2.6
).

Finally, we will consider a version of this correspondence over the ordinary locus. Recall from §
6.1.2
that we have group schemes
J^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
and
J^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
, both of which we denote by
J_{p}
in cases
\mathscr{G}=G
and
\mathscr{G}=H
respectively. For any
t\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}
we can naturally view
t^{\prime}=gtg^{-1}\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\subset J_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{ord}}
, and we consider the group scheme
J_{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}t^{\prime}J_{p}(t^{\prime})^{-1}\cap J_{p}
. Then we have a natural correspondence between quotients of Igusa varieties

(8.2.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}

where
q_{1}
is the natural forgetful map and
q_{2}
is induced from the right-action of
t^{\prime}
. Here
\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}
is the adic generic fibre of
\mathfrak{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}
. Both of the morphisms
q_{1}
and
q_{2}
are finite étale. Furthermore, there exists a finite extension
L^{\prime}/L
(depending on
t
) and a commutative diagram:

(8.2.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}\right)_{L^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}^{\prime}\right)_{L^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}\right)_{L^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+d})_{L^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d})_{L^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})_{L^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where the vertical maps are open immersions induced from right-translation by
g
.

Remark 8.2.9
.

The extension
L^{\prime}
is obtained by adjoining
\mu_{p^{\beta+d}}
to
L
(where
d=\operatorname{max}_{\mathscr{G}}(t)
). The reason for this is similar to the discussion at the end of §
6.1.2
and is due to the fact that we need to trivialise
\mu_{p^{\beta+d}}
to compare level structures for quotients of the Igusa variety and adic Shimura varieties.

Lemma 8.2.10
.

If
k\geq\beta-1
, then the left-hand square in (
8.2.8
) is Cartesian.

Proof.

It suffices to check the claim on rank one points. Note that
\left(\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}\right)_{L^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{rk}1}
(resp.
\left(\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}^{\prime}\right)_{L^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{rk}1}
) is identified with
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}\right)^{\operatorname{rk}1}
(resp.
\pi_{\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime}}^{-1}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}^{\prime}\right)^{\operatorname{rk}1}
). Therefore, it is enough to show that

\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}\;\cap\;]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k+d,k+d}K_{p}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}^{\prime}

with the intersection taking place in
\mathtt{FL}^{\mathscr{G}}
. In the case
\mathscr{G}=G
, an element

[x_{0}:\cdots:x_{2n-1}]\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}\;\cap\;]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k+d,k+d}

can be described (uniquely) as a point on the flag variety satisfying:

•

For
i=0,\dots n-1
, we have
x_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cap\mathcal{B}_{k+d}=p^{k+d}\mathbb{Z}_{p}

•

x_{n}=1

•

For
i=n+1,\dots,2n-1
, we have
x_{i}\in p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cap\mathcal{B}^{\circ}_{k+d}=p^{\operatorname{max}(\beta,k+d+1)}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\subset p^{\beta+d}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.

We then see that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}\;\cap\;]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k+d,k+d}\;\subset\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}\backslash\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}gK_{p}^{\prime}
which is sufficient for establishing the claim. The case
\mathscr{G}=H
is similar and left to the reader.
∎

8.3.
Cohomological correspondences

We now discuss the maps of sheaves associated with each correspondence in the previous section. We then explain how one obtains the action of Hecke operators on cohomology (with and without partial compact support). We continue with the general notation in the previous section (i.e. we deal with the cases
\mathscr{G}=G
and
\mathscr{G}=H
simultaneously).

We begin with the cohomological correspondence for classical weights. Recall that we have an
M_{\mathscr{G}}^{\operatorname{an}}
-torsor
M^{\operatorname{an}}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}}
(where we now include the level subgroup in the notation). Let
t\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}
and consider the correspondence in (
8.2.3
).

Definition 8.3.1
.

(1)

Let
\phi_{t}\colon p_{1}^{-1}M^{\operatorname{an}}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}\to p_{2}^{-1}M^{\operatorname{an}}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}
denote the composition of the map induced by the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant structure on the torsors
M^{\operatorname{an}}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},-}
and the action of
gtg^{-1}\in M_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
through the torsor structure (in either order).

(2)

Let
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
be a
M_{\mathscr{G}}
-dominant weight, and let
V_{\kappa}^{*}
denote the linear dual of the algebraic representation of
M_{\mathscr{G}}
of highest weight
\kappa
. We let
\phi_{t,\kappa^{*}}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\to p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}
denote the morphism of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}^{\prime}}}
-modules obtained as the
M_{\mathscr{G}}^{\operatorname{an}}
invariants of:

p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G}}\otimes V^{*}_{\kappa}\xrightarrow{\phi_{t}^{*}\otimes(\kappa^{*}(gt^{-1}g^{-1})gtg^{-1}\cdot-)}p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G}}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}.

This induces the following operators on cohomology.

Definition 8.3.2
.

Let
U_{t}\colon R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)\to R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)
denote the Hecke correspondence defined as the composition:

\displaystyle R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{2}^{*}}R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}^{\prime}},p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{t,\kappa^{*}}}R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}^{\prime}},p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(-g^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t)\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}}R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)

where
\rho
is the half-sum of the positive roots in
\mathscr{G}
and
\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}
is the trace map from
[
BP21
, Lemma 2.1.2]
. Since
p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathcal{Z}
(Lemma
8.2.4
) we also obtain a Hecke correspondence
U_{t}
on
R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)
in exactly the same way, and these two operators are compatible with each other under the natural corestriction map

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)\to R\Gamma\left(\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right).

Remark 8.3.3
.

The Hecke operator
U_{t}
is optimally normalised for those weights
\kappa
with
C(\kappa^{*})^{-}=\{g\}
(see
[
BP21
, §5.9]
).

We now discuss the overconvergent version of this. Let
k\geq 1
be an integer and recall the definition of
\mathscr{G}^{1}_{k,k}
from
[
BP21
, §3.3.10]
(i.e. the subgroup of elements in
\mathscr{G}
which land in the lower-triangular Borel unipotent modulo
p^{k}
, and in the upper-triangular Borel unipotent modulo
p^{k+\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon>0
). Let
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}=t^{-1}K_{p}^{\prime}t
. Then, for
U_{p}=K_{p},K_{p}^{\prime},K_{p}^{\prime\prime}
we have torsors

\displaystyle\mathtt{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},U_{p},k}=g^{-1}g\mathscr{G}^{1}_{k,k}U_{p}g^{-1}/\left(\operatorname{Unip}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}})\cap g\mathscr{G}^{1}_{k,k}U_{p}g^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\to]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}U_{p}

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\mapsto x^{-1}

under the group
U_{p,g,k}^{\circ}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G}}
obtained as the image of
g\mathscr{G}^{1}_{k,k}U_{p}g^{-1}\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}
under projection to the Levi. Let
U_{p,g,k}\subset\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G}}
denote the affinoid subgroup obtained as the image of
g\mathscr{G}^{1}_{k}U_{p}g^{-1}\cap\mathcal{P}_{\mathscr{G}}
under projection to the Levi, where
\mathscr{G}^{1}_{k}\subset\mathscr{G}
denotes the open affinoid subgroup of elements which reduce to the identity modulo
p^{k}
. Note that
K_{p,g,k}=\mathcal{M}^{\square}_{G,k}
(resp.
K_{p,g,k}=\mathcal{M}^{\diamondsuit}_{H,k}
) in the case
\mathscr{G}=G
(resp.
\mathscr{G}=H
).

By pulling back under the Hodge–Tate period morphism, descending to finite level, twisting along the Hodge cocharacter
\mu
(or its restriction to
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
in the case
\mathscr{G}=H
), and pushing out along the inclusion
U_{p,g,k}^{\circ}\subset U_{p,g,k}
we obtain étale
U_{p,g,k}
-torsors
{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},U_{p},k}}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{U_{p},k}
. Suppose we are in the setting of (
8.2.3
), and let
t\colon\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},k}
denote the map induced from right-translation by
t
. Then we have a diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{t^{*}\left({{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime\prime},k}}\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left.p_{1}^{-1}\left({{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},K_{p},k+d}}\right)\right|_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\left.p_{2}^{-1}\left({{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},K_{p},k}}\right)\right|_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{t}}

where the vertical maps are reductions of structure and
\phi_{t}
is induced from the morphism

\displaystyle\mathtt{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}

\displaystyle\to\mathtt{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime\prime},k}

\displaystyle[x]

\displaystyle\mapsto[t^{-1}x(gtg^{-1})].

As the notation suggests, the map
\phi_{t}
commutes with the one in Definition
8.3.1
(1). We now construct the overconvergent cohomological correspondences.

Definition 8.3.4
.

Let
\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},U_{p},k}}}
denote the pushforward of the structure sheaf of
{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{HT},U_{p},k}}
to
\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{U_{p},k}
. Let
(R,R^{+})
be a complete Tate affinoid algebra over
(L,\mathcal{O}_{L})
. Let
T^{M_{G},-}\subset T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
be the submonoid of elements
t\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
satisfying
v_{p}(\alpha(t))\leq 0
for all positive roots
\alpha\in\Phi_{M_{G}}^{+}
.

(1)

Suppose we are in case
\mathscr{G}=G
. Let
\kappa\colon T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to(R^{+})^{\times}
be an
s
-analytic character and let
k\geq s+1
. Recall that
D_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}
denotes the continuous
R
-dual of
V_{G,\kappa}^{\circ,s\operatorname{-an}}
, which comes equipped with an action of the submonoid of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
generated by
K_{p,w_{n},k}
and
T^{M_{G},-}
(see
[
BP21
, §6.2.20]
). We set
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{G,\operatorname{HT},K_{p},k}}}\hat{\otimes}D_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\right)^{K_{p,w_{n},k}}
. Then we obtain a cohomological correspondence

\phi_{t,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\colon p_{2}^{*}(\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}})\to p_{1}^{*}(\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}})

defined on
\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}
, which is induced from the morphism

\left(t^{*}\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{G,\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime\prime},k}}}\hat{\otimes}D_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\right)^{K^{\prime\prime}_{p,w_{n},k}}\to\left(\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{G,\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}}}\hat{\otimes}D_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\right)^{K^{\prime}_{p,w_{n},k+d}}

given as the tensor product
\phi_{t}^{*}\otimes((w_{n}tw_{n}^{-1})\cdot-)
, noting that
w_{n}tw_{n}^{-1}\in T^{M_{G},-}
.

(2)

Suppose that we are in case
\mathscr{G}=H
and let
\sigma\colon M^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})\to(R^{+})^{\times}
be a
s
-analytic character. Let
k\geq s
. We set
\mathscr{M}^{\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{H,\operatorname{HT},K_{p},k}}}\hat{\otimes}\sigma\right)^{K_{p,\operatorname{id},k}}
. Then we obtain a cohomological correspondence

\phi_{t,\sigma}^{\operatorname{an}}\colon p_{2}^{*}(\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma}^{\operatorname{an}})\to p_{1}^{*}(\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma}^{\operatorname{an}})

defined over
\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}
, which is induced from the morphism

\left(t^{*}\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{H,\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime\prime},k}}}\hat{\otimes}\sigma\right)^{K^{\prime\prime}_{p,\operatorname{id},k}}\to\left(\mathscr{O}_{{{}^{\mu}\mathcal{M}_{H,\operatorname{HT},K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}}}\hat{\otimes}\sigma\right)^{K^{\prime}_{p,\operatorname{id},k+d}}

given as the tensor product
\phi_{t}^{*}\otimes\operatorname{id}
. This is well-defined because
\sigma(x)=\sigma(txt^{-1})
for any
x\in K_{p,\operatorname{id},k}^{\prime\prime}
.

Remark 8.3.5
.

If
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
is
M_{G}
-dominant, then the morphism
\phi_{t,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}
is compatible with the restriction of
\phi_{t,\kappa^{*}}
to
\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}
via the natural map
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\to\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
induced from the map of representations
D_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{s\operatorname{-an}}\to V_{\kappa}^{*}
. Similarly, if
\sigma
is an algebraic character of
M_{H}
, then the morphism
\phi_{t,\sigma}^{\operatorname{an}}
coincides with the restriction of
\phi_{t,\sigma}
to
\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}
.

By Lemmas
8.2.4
and
8.2.5
the collections
\{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}\}_{k\geq 1}
and
\{\mathcal{Z}_{m}\}_{m\geq 1}
form a system of support conditions for the correspondence (
8.2.3
), in the sense of Definition
4.0.1
. Furthermore, note that:

•

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}})=R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}

•

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{M}^{s\operatorname{-an}}_{G,\kappa^{*}})=R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n},s\operatorname{-an}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}

•

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma})=R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma}\right)

•

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{M}^{\operatorname{an}}_{H,\sigma})=R\Gamma^{H}_{\operatorname{id},\operatorname{an}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma)^{(-,\dagger)}

because the support conditions defined by inequalities involving
\hat{\delta}_{G,n+1}^{+}
,
\delta_{G,>n+1}^{+}
and
\hat{\delta}_{H,n+1}^{+}
intertwine with the support conditions
(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet})
. By applying the general construction in §
4
(and normalising the trace map by
(-g^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t)
), we therefore obtain Hecke operators on each of the above cohomologies, all of which we denote by
U_{t}
.

Remark 8.3.6
.

By Remark
8.3.5
, the natural restriction map

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}

is
U_{t}
-equivariant. Similarly, the Hecke operator
U_{t}
on
R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
can be seen as the colimit of the following operators:

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}\cap\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{p_{2}^{*}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}\cap p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d},p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{t,\kappa^{*}}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}\cap p_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d},p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(-g^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t)\widetilde{\operatorname{Tr}}_{p_{1}}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+d}\cap\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+d},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

where
\widetilde{\operatorname{Tr}}_{p_{1}}
denotes the trace map associated with the finite flat morphism
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}\to\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+d}
and we have used the fact that
p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathcal{Z}
(see
[
BP21
, Lemma 2.1.2]
). This alternative description holds because
\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+d}
is open and closed in
p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+d})
.

Remark 8.3.7
.

As explained in
[
BP21
, §4.2]
, the operators
\{U_{t}:t\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}\}
commute with each other after passing to the cohomology groups of the complexes above.

8.4.
Frobenius

We now discuss the Frobenius operator acting on the cohomology of the Shimura–Deligne varieties. We will continue to use the same notation as in §
8.2
, i.e.
(\mathscr{G},g,K_{p},\mathcal{Z})
will denote one of the tuples in (
8.2.2
). However in this section we will focus on a single correspondence which is not necessarily associated with an element of
T^{\mathscr{G},-}
.

Let
\xi\in T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the element which is trivial outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is equal to

\xi=\operatorname{diag}(p^{-1},1,\dots,1).

Note that
\xi\in T^{M_{\mathscr{G}},-}
. We let
t=g^{-1}\xi g
and set
K_{p}^{\prime}=tK_{p}t\cap K_{p}
and
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}=t^{-1}K_{p}^{\prime}t
. We consider the following correspondence

(8.4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}

where
p_{1}
is the forgetful map, and
p_{2}
is induced from right-translation by
t
. We have the following properties:

Lemma 8.4.2
.

Let
k,m\geq 1
be integers. Then

(1)

p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})\subset\mathcal{Z}_{m}
and
p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z})=\mathcal{Z}
.

(2)

p_{2}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+1})\subset\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
and the map
p_{2}\colon\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+1}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
is finite étale of degree
[K_{p}:K_{p}^{\prime\prime}]
.

(3)

The map
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
is an isomorphism.

(4)

For
m\gg k
, we have
p_{1}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})\cap p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{m})\subset\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}
.

In particular, the collections
\{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\mathcal{Z}_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
form a system of support conditions for the correspondence (
8.4.1
) as in Definition
4.0.1
.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to Lemmas
8.2.4
and
8.2.5
even though
t\not\in T^{\mathscr{G},-}
. Indeed, for part (1) in the case
G=\mathscr{G}
, it is enough to show that

P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m+1}K_{p}\subset P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}t^{-1}K_{p}\subset P_{G}^{\operatorname{an}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}K_{p}

and we just follow the same argument in Lemma
8.2.4
(noting that we only need
v_{p}(\alpha(t))\leq 0
for the positive roots
\alpha
not contained in the
(n+1,n-1)
-parabolic in the
\tau_{0}
-component). The case
\mathscr{G}=H
is similar.

For part (2) it is enough to show
p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})=\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+1}
, but this follows from the easy calculation that

]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}K_{p}t^{-1}K_{p}^{\prime}=]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k+1,k+1}K_{p}^{\prime}.

For part (3), one can show that
]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}K_{p}
is the disjoint union of
[K_{p}:K_{p}^{\prime}]
open and closed subspaces, and one of these subspaces is equal to
]C^{\mathscr{G}}_{g}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}
, hence the map
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}\to\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}
