must be an isomorphism.

Finally, for part (4) the case
\mathscr{G}=H
follows from
p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})=\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+1}
, so we give the proof in the case
\mathscr{G}=G
. In this case, one can follow the same proof as in Lemma
8.2.5
with some minor modifications. Firstly, one can reduce to showing

]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}

because
A_{m}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset A_{m}K_{p}
. By an explicit calculation, one can then show that

]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{0}}K_{p}t^{-1}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{1}}K_{p}^{\prime}

so it suffices to show that
]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}\cap]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{m,\bar{1}}\subset]C^{G}_{w_{n}}[_{k,k}K_{p}^{\prime}
. But this latter inclusion follows from an explicit calculation using the fact that
\mathcal{B}_{k}\subset\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
and
\mathbb{Z}_{p}\cap\overline{\mathcal{B}}_{1}=p\mathbb{Z}_{p}
.
∎

We now introduce the cohomological correspondences. We are only interested in such morphisms for automorphic sheaves of classical weight. Let
\kappa\in X^{*}(T)
be a
M_{\mathscr{G}}
-dominant weight, and let
V_{\kappa}
denote the algebraic representation of
M_{\mathscr{G}}
with highest weight
\kappa
. Let
\psi\colon p_{1}^{-1}M_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to p_{2}^{-1}M_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}
denote the morphism obtained as the composition of the equivariant structure on the torsors
M_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},-}^{\operatorname{an}}
and the action of
\xi\in M_{\mathscr{G}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
through the torsor structure. Let
\pi_{i}\colon p_{i}^{-1}M_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathscr{G},K_{p}^{\prime}}
denote the structural map. Then we let

\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\colon p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}=\left((\pi_{2})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{p_{2}^{-1}M_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\right)^{M_{\mathscr{G}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\to\left((\pi_{1})_{*}\mathcal{O}_{p_{1}^{-1}M_{\mathscr{G},\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}}\otimes V_{\kappa}^{*}\right)^{M_{\mathscr{G}}^{\operatorname{an}}}=p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}

denote the morphism induced from
\psi^{*}\otimes(\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-1})\xi\cdot-)
. Here
\kappa^{*}=-w_{M_{\mathscr{G}}}^{\operatorname{max}}\kappa
. Recall that

R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}})=R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}\text{ and }R\Gamma(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},\bullet},\mathcal{Z}_{\bullet};\mathscr{M}_{H,\kappa^{*}})=R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\kappa^{*}}\right).

Then by the general procedure in §
4
, we obtain Hecke operators (both of which we denote by
\varphi_{\mathscr{G}}
) on
R\Gamma^{G}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
and
R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\kappa^{*}}\right)
associated with the correspondence (
8.4.1
) and cohomological correspondence
\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}
. These Hecke operators only depend on the restriction of
\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}
to
\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k}
for any integer
k\geq 1
, therefore it will be useful to consider the following morphism

\varphi\colon\mathcal{U}_{K_{p},k+1}^{\mathscr{G}}\xrightarrow{p_{1}^{-1}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p}^{\prime},k+1}\xrightarrow{p_{2}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}.

We can view the restriction of
\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa_{*}}
as a morphism
\varphi^{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}}\to\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+1}}
, or by adjunction, as a morphism

(8.4.3)

\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}}\to\varphi_{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}|_{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+1}}.

By Lemma
8.4.2
we have
\varphi^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k})=\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+1}
, and the operator is simply induced from the colimit of the morphism (
8.4.3
) on cohomology with support in
\mathcal{Z}
, i.e.
\varphi_{\mathscr{G}}
is the colimit of the maps

R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)\xrightarrow{\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}}R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k},\varphi_{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)=R\Gamma_{\mathcal{Z}\cap\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+1}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+1},\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\right)

where the last equality uses the fact that
\varphi
is finite.

8.4.1.
A version over the ordinary locus

We now consider the Frobenius operator acting on Igusa towers. Suppose that
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is a sufficiently large extension containing
\mu_{p^{\beta+1}}
. Let
J_{p}
be as at the end of §
8.2
and set
J_{p}^{\prime}=\xi J_{p}\xi^{-1}\cap J_{p}
and
J_{p}^{\prime\prime}=\xi^{-1}J_{p}^{\prime}\xi
. Then we have a correspondence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}

where
q_{1}
is the natural forgetful map and
q_{2}
is induced from right-translation by
\xi
. One can easily check that
q_{1}
is an isomorphism and
q_{2}
is finite étale of degree
[J_{p}:J_{p}^{\prime\prime}]=[K_{p}:K_{p}^{\prime\prime}]
. We let

\varphi\colon\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}\xrightarrow{q_{1}^{-1}}\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}^{\prime}\xrightarrow{q_{2}}\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}

which is finite étale of degree
[K_{p}:K_{p}^{\prime\prime}]
. We have a Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k+1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{\mathscr{G}}_{K_{p},k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

and the pullback
\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}|_{\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}\to\varphi_{*}\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}|_{\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}}
of
\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}
in (
8.4.3
) can be described as follows. Let
U(J_{p})\subset J_{p}
denote the unipotent part, and
M(J_{p})
the Levi part. Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/U(J_{p})\to\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}
denote the corresponding proétale
M(J_{p})
-torsor. Then for an open
V\subset\mathcal{IG}_{\mathscr{G}}/J_{p}
, we have

\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}(V)=\{f\colon\pi^{-1}(V)\to V_{\kappa}^{*}:f(x\cdot m)=m^{-1}\cdot f(x)\text{ all }m\in M(J_{p})\}.

The morphism
\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}(V)\to\mathscr{M}_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}(\varphi^{-1}(V))
is then simply described as

(\psi_{\mathscr{G},\kappa^{*}}f)(x)=\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-1})\xi\cdot f(x\cdot\xi)\quad\quad\quad x\in\pi^{-1}(\varphi^{-1}(V)).

8.5.
Properties of evaluation maps

In this section, we prove an “interpolation formula” for the evaluation maps
\operatorname{Ev}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}^{\dagger,\circ}
when the conductor of
\chi
is divisible by
p
. We expect one can also establish a similar formula for unramified characters, however this seems harder than the ramified case. The proofs in this section will involve studying the relations between Hecke operators, Frobenius, and the action of
C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
, and will continually make use of the following strategy. Firstly, one establishes the relation over the ordinary locus. Then one constructs an appropriate
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
-acyclic (or
\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
-acyclic) cover such that the restriction map from sections over an element of this cover to sections over the ordinary locus is injective. This injectivity property allows one to establish the desired overconvergent version of the relation on the level of Cech complexes – the final result is then obtained by passing to cohomology. Although the idea is not too difficult, this section is unfortunately rather technical.

We now state the main result. Fix
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
satisfying Assumption
5.3.12
(i.e.
\kappa_{0}=0
,
\kappa_{1,\tau_{0}}+\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}=n-1
, and
w=\kappa_{2,\tau_{0}}+\kappa_{2n,\tau_{0}}=-1
). Let
\chi\in\Sigma_{\kappa,j}(\mathfrak{N}_{\beta})
be an anticyclotomic character. Let
\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}\colon\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denote the finite-order character obtained as the restriction of
\chi
to
\mathcal{O}_{F_{\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tau_{0}}}}^{\times}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. We suppose that the conductor of
\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}
is
p^{\beta^{\prime}}
for some
1\leq\beta^{\prime}\leq\beta
. Finally, we fix
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{h})_{h\geq 0}
a compatible system of
p
-th roots of unity in
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
, i.e.
\varepsilon_{h}
is a primitive
p^{h}
-th root of unity, and
\varepsilon_{h+1}^{p}=\varepsilon_{h}
for all
h\geq 0
. The Gauss sum associated with
\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}
is

\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{1}{p^{h-\beta^{\prime}}}\sum_{a\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{h}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(a)\varepsilon_{\beta^{\prime}}^{a}

for any
h\geq\beta^{\prime}
(which is non-zero because
\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}
has conductor
p^{\beta^{\prime}}
). For
i=0,1
, let
t_{i}\in T^{G,-}
be the element which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is equal to
\operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1,p,\dots,p)
where there are
n-i
lots of
p
.

Theorem 8.5.1
.

Let
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
and suppose that
U_{t_{i}}\cdot\eta=\alpha_{i}\eta
for
i=0,1
and some
\alpha_{i}\in L^{\times}
. Then

\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\eta)=\left(\frac{\alpha_{0}}{\alpha_{1}}\right)^{\beta^{\prime}}p^{\beta^{\prime}\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}}(1-p^{-1})\chi(\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}_{0}}})^{-\beta^{\prime}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(-1)\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}})\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\eta)

where
\varpi_{\mathfrak{p}_{\bar{\tau}_{0}}}\in\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
denotes the image of
p
under the natural embedding
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\cong F_{\bar{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tau_{0}}}^{\times}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{A}_{F}^{\times}
.

We will prove this theorem in several steps. First, we recall and introduce some notation. Recall the definition of the weighted indicator function
1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
, namely

1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(a_{n+1})&\text{ if }(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\in U_{G,\beta}^{\circ}\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\end{array}\right..

We let
f_{\chi_{p}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
denote the function given by

f_{\chi_{p}}(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(p^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{n+1})&\text{ if }a_{n+1}\in p^{-\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
0&\text{ otherwise }\end{array}\right.

and let
1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{H,\beta},L)
denote the indicator function of the subset
U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}=\left(p^{-\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\right)^{\oplus n-1}\subset U_{H,\beta}
. We also let
1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
denote the function given by
(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\mapsto 1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}(a_{2},\dots,a_{n})
. Finally, we set

1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}^{\spadesuit}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}f_{\chi_{p}}\cdot 1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L).

8.5.1.
Step 1

In this step, we describe the eigenvalues of the cohomology class
[\chi]
under the action of certain
U_{p}
-Hecke operators. We first describe the transpose of the Hecke operators defined in §
8.3
with respect to Serre duality. Let
t\in T^{H,-}
and consider the correspondence in (
8.2.3
) (in the case
\mathscr{G}=H
). Consider the morphism

(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(t)\phi_{t,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}^{-1}\colon p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\to p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}

which is well-defined because the morphism in Definition
8.3.1
(2) is an isomorphism. Because
p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k})\subset\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}
, we obtain a Hecke correspondence

U_{t^{-1}}\colon\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}\to\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}

as the colimit over
k
of following composition:

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k},\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(p_{1}p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}),\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)\xrightarrow{p_{1}^{*}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}),p_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(t)\phi_{t,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}^{-1}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(p_{2}^{-1}(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k}),p_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{2}}}\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(\mathcal{U}^{H}_{K_{p},k},\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}\right).

Recall that we have a Serre duality pairing

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]})^{(-,\dagger)}\times\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee})^{(+,\dagger)}\to L.

We have the following relation between
U_{t}
and
U_{t^{-1}}
.

Lemma 8.5.2
.

For any
t\in T^{H,-}
, one has
\langle U_{t}\cdot-,-\rangle=\langle-,U_{t^{-1}}\cdot-\rangle
.

Proof.

This follows from the duality of pullback and trace maps (note that the normalisation of
\operatorname{Tr}_{p_{1}}
in the definition of
U_{t}
is trivial), and the fact that
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}(t)\phi_{t,\sigma^{[j]}_{\kappa}}
corresponds to
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}(t^{-1})\phi_{t,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}}^{-1}
under Verdier duality (see
[
BP21
, Proposition 4.2.9]
).
∎

Let
\langle-\rangle\colon\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
denote the natural projection arising from the identification
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}=p^{\mathbb{Z}}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. We extend this naturally to a map
\langle-\rangle\colon T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We have the following property for the cohomology classes associated with anticyclotomic characters.

Lemma 8.5.3
.

Let
t\in T^{H,-}
be any element which is trivial outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is given by
\operatorname{diag}(y,1,\dots,1)
. We have

U_{t^{-1}}\cdot\operatorname{res}[\chi]=(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(\langle t\rangle)\hat{\chi}(z_{t^{-1}})\cdot\operatorname{res}[\chi]

where
z_{t^{-1}}
denotes the idele which is
y
in the factor corresponding to the prime
\overline{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tau_{0}}
(and the identity outside this prime).

Proof.

By analysing the construction of
[\chi]
in
[
Gra21
, §7]
, one can see that the action of
(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(t)^{-1}U_{t^{-1}}
is described as follows. We will freely use notation from the proof of Lemma
7.4.20
. Without loss of generality, we may also assume that
L=\mathbb{C}_{p}
.

Let
C=\operatorname{det}(U^{p}K_{p})
and
C^{\prime}=\operatorname{det}(U^{p}K_{p}^{\prime})
, which are compact open subgroups of
\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. One can easily see that
C=C^{\prime}
because
K_{p}^{\prime}
contains all elements in
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which lie in
T^{\diamondsuit}
modulo
p^{\beta}
. Then we have a commutative diagram of torsors:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{2}^{-1}M_{H,\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{\operatorname{dR},C_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{p_{1}^{-1}M_{H,\operatorname{dR},K_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R_{\operatorname{dR},C_{p}}^{\operatorname{an}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{t}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

where the horizontal maps are induced from the determinant morphism, and the morphism
f
is described as

\displaystyle\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/C^{p}\times\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\right)\times R^{\operatorname{an}}\right]/C_{p}

\displaystyle\to\left[\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/C^{p}\times\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\right)\times R^{\operatorname{an}}\right]/C_{p}

\displaystyle[x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}]

\displaystyle\mapsto[x,\operatorname{det}(t^{-1})x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}].

Let
\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}
be the unique
p
-adic Hecke character on
\operatorname{Res}_{F^{+}/\mathbb{Q}}\operatorname{U}(1)
such that
\widehat{\chi}=\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}\circ\mathcal{N}
, and let
\lambda\colon\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathbf{R}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to L^{\times}
denote the continuous character given by
\lambda(z_{1},z_{2})=\widehat{\chi}^{\prime}(z_{2}/z_{1})
. Then, since
\operatorname{res}[\chi]
is constructed as the pullback of the function
\xi\colon R^{\operatorname{an}}_{\operatorname{dR},C_{p}}\to\mathbb{A}^{1,\operatorname{an}}
,
\xi([x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}])\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lambda(x)\lambda(x^{\prime})\mu(x^{\prime\prime})
(where
\mu
is the unique algebraic character satisfying
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}=\mu^{-1}\circ\operatorname{det}
), it is enough to understand
\xi\circ f
. But clearly one has

\xi\circ f=\lambda(\operatorname{det}(t^{-1}))\xi=\hat{\chi}(z_{t^{-1}})\xi.

The result now follows from the fact that the degree of the morphism
p_{1}
is given by
p^{v_{p}((2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(t^{-1}))}
.
∎

8.5.2.
Step 2

We now establish an intertwining property between Frobenius and the evaluation maps. We introduce the following matrices. For any
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, let
\xi_{c}
denote the diagonal matrix which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and at the
\tau_{0}
-component is given by
(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}},1,\dots,1)
. We let
t_{c}=w_{n}^{-1}\xi_{c}w_{n}
. For notational brevity, let
L_{p}=K^{H}_{\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
and
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. For any integer
k\geq 1
, we consider the following map

B_{c}\colon\mathcal{U}^{H}_{L_{p},k+\beta^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathcal{U}^{H}_{L_{p},k}\xrightarrow{\cdot\xi_{c}}\mathcal{U}^{H}_{L_{p},k}\xrightarrow{\hat{\iota}}\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}\xrightarrow{\cdot t_{c}^{-1}}\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}.

We also consider

A\colon\mathcal{U}^{H}_{L_{p},k+\beta^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\hat{\iota}}\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+\beta^{\prime}}\xrightarrow{\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}.

Note that
B_{c}
only depends on
c
modulo
p^{\beta}
. We will need the following lemma.

Lemma 8.5.4
.

Let
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
.

(1)

One has
A^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))=B_{c}^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta}))=\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
(where by abuse of notation,
\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
also denotes its intersection with
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
).

(2)

Suppose that
U\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
is a quasi-compact open subset arising as the adic generic fibre of an open subspace in
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
. Then
A^{-1}(U)=B_{c}^{-1}(U)
.

Proof.

The first part follows from Lemma
8.2.4
and Lemma
8.4.2
. For the second part we may assume, without loss of generality, that
L
contains
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
. Then, over
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
, the morphisms
A
and
B_{c}
are induced from the morphisms

\mathcal{IG}_{H}\to\mathcal{IG}_{G}

given by right-translation by
\gamma\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}
and
\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\xi_{c}\gamma\xi_{c}^{-1}
respectively, where
\xi
is the element of the torus in the definition of Frobenius (see §
8.4
) and
\gamma
is the element introduced at the end of §
6.1.2
. For any
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, let

\omega_{c}=(\omega_{c,i})_{1\leq i\leq 2n-1}\in\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}^{\oplus 2n-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})

denote the element satisfying
\omega_{c,i}=1
if
i\neq n
, and
\omega_{c,n}=\varepsilon^{c}
. Let
u_{c}\in J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
denote the element which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and at the
\tau_{0}
-component is given by the block matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\omega_{c}\\
&1\end{array}\right).

Then an explicit calculation shows that
\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\xi_{c}\gamma\xi_{c}^{-1}=\gamma\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}u_{c}
. The claim now follows from the fact that right-translation by
u_{c}
maps any open subscheme of
\mathfrak{IG}_{G}
into itself.
∎

We now introduce some morphisms which encode the intertwining property over the ordinary loci.

Definition 8.5.5
.

Let
U\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
be a quasi-compact open subset arising as the adic generic fibre of an open subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
. Set
U^{\prime}=A^{-1}(U)=B_{c}^{-1}(U)
(for any
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
).

(1)

Let
\rho_{A,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime})
denote the
L
-linear morphism given by the composition

\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)\xrightarrow{\psi_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}U)\xrightarrow{\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}).

(2)

Let
\rho_{B_{c},U}^{\operatorname{ord}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime})
denote the
L
-linear morphism obtained as the following composition:

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{t_{c}^{-1},\kappa^{*}}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((t_{c}^{-1})^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota})^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{\xi_{c},\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}U)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\psi_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}).

We set

\rho_{B,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}\rho_{B_{c},U}^{\operatorname{ord}}

for a fixed choice of representatives of
\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
.

The following lemma shows that these morphisms are in fact equal.

Lemma 8.5.6
.

We have
\rho_{A,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}=\rho_{B,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove the statement after base-changing to an extension
L
containing
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
. Let
\pi_{G}\colon\mathcal{IG}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
and
\pi_{H}\colon\mathcal{IG}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})\to\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
denote the structural maps. Let
F\in\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U)
, which we can view as a morphism
F\colon\pi_{G}^{-1}(U)\to V_{\kappa}^{*}
satisfying
F(-\cdot m)=m^{-1}\cdot F(-)
for all
m\in M^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
. Recall we have a differential operator
\delta_{\kappa,j}\in V_{\kappa}\otimes C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)
which is an eigenvector for the action of
M_{H}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
with eigencharacter
\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],-1}
. Fix a basis
\{v_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
V_{\kappa}
. Then we can write
\delta_{\kappa,j}=\sum_{i\in I}v_{i}\otimes\delta_{i}
, for some
\delta_{i}\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\oplus 2n-1},L)
. Recall

\delta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}=1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\cdot\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}=\sum_{i\in I}u^{-1}\cdot v_{i}\otimes 1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\cdot(u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}.

Consider the action of
\xi^{-1}
on
C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
given by
(\xi^{-1}\cdot\phi)(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})=\phi(pa_{2},\dots,pa_{2n})
. Then one can easily verify that

\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\cdot\left((\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}\cdot\phi)\star h\right)=\phi\star(\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\cdot h)

for any
h\in\mathcal{O}(\pi_{G}^{-1}(U))
and
\phi\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
.

Since
\xi
commutes with
u
, we have
(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}},\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\cdot\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}=\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}(\xi^{\beta^{\prime}})\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
, and hence

(8.5.7)

\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}=\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}(\xi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I}\left(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}u^{-1}\cdot v_{i}\otimes(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\right).

We now compute
\rho^{\operatorname{ord}}_{A,U}(F)
. Firstly, consider the function
H\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\psi_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{\beta^{\prime}}(F)
. By the explicit description of the Frobenius morphism over the ordinary locus (see §
8.4.1
), we have

H(-)=\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\cdot F(-\cdot\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}).

For any
x\in\pi_{H}^{-1}(U^{\prime})
, we therefore have:

\displaystyle\rho^{\operatorname{ord}}_{A,U}(F)(x)

\displaystyle=\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}(H)(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in I}\left(\left[1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\cdot(u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\right]\star\langle H(-),u^{-1}v_{i}\rangle\right)(\hat{\iota}(x))

\displaystyle=\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{i\in I}\left(\left[1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\cdot(u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\right]\star\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\cdot\langle F(-),\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}u^{-1}v_{i}\rangle\right)(\hat{\iota}(x))

\displaystyle=\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{i\in I}\left(\left[1^{\spadesuit}_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\cdot(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\right]\star\langle F(-),\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}u^{-1}v_{i}\rangle\right)(\hat{\iota}(x)\xi^{\beta^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}\sum_{i\in I}\left(\left[1^{\spadesuit}_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}\cdot(u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\right]\star\langle F(-),u^{-1}v_{i}\rangle\right)(\hat{\iota}(x)\xi^{\beta^{\prime}})

where for the fourth equality we have used the fact that
\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}\cdot 1_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}=1^{\spadesuit}_{U_{G,\beta}^{\circ},\chi_{p}}
, and for the fifth equality we have used
\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}(\xi^{\beta^{\prime}})=p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}
and (
8.5.7
).

Recall the definitions of
\omega_{c}
and
u_{c}
from Lemma
8.5.4
. For any tuple
\underline{d}=(d_{2},\dots,d_{n})\in(p^{\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z})^{\oplus n-1}
, let

\omega_{\underline{d}}=(\omega_{\underline{d},i})_{2\leq i\leq 2n}\in\widetilde{\mu_{p^{\infty}}}^{\oplus 2n-1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})

be the element which satisfies
\omega_{\underline{d},i}=\varepsilon^{d_{i}}
if
2\leq i\leq n
, and
\omega_{\underline{d},i}=1
if
i\geq n+1
. Let
v_{\underline{d}}\in J_{H,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})\subset J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}^{+}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}})
denote the element which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
is given by the block matrix

\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\omega_{\underline{d}}\\
&1\end{array}\right).

Then, with notation as in §
6.1.3
, we have the following relations:

•

The function
f_{\chi_{p}}
satisfies

(8.5.8)

f_{\chi_{p}}=\frac{1}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}\chi_{\omega_{c}}.

Indeed, evaluating the right-hand side of (
8.5.8
) at an element
(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})\in U_{G,\beta}
, we have

\frac{1}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}\varepsilon_{\beta}^{cp^{\beta}a_{n+1}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}0&\text{ if }a_{n+1}\not\in p^{-\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\\
\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(p^{\beta^{\prime}}a_{n+1})&\text{ if }a_{n+1}\in p^{-\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\end{array}\right.

•

The function
1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}
satisfies

1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}=\frac{1}{p^{(n-1)(\beta-\beta^{\prime})}}\sum_{\underline{d}\in\left(p^{\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus n-1}}\chi_{\omega_{\underline{d}}}.

Using these expressions, we see that
\rho^{\operatorname{ord}}_{A,U}(F)(x)
is equal to:

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}

\displaystyle\cdot

\displaystyle\hskip-85.35826pt\left(\frac{1}{p^{(n-1)(\beta-\beta^{\prime})}}\sum_{\underline{d}\in\left(p^{\beta^{\prime}}\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\oplus n-1}}\sum_{i\in I}\left((u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\star\langle F(-),u^{-1}v_{i}\rangle\right)(\hat{\iota}(x)\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}u_{c}v_{\underline{d}})\right).

Now because
\hat{\iota}
is induced from right-translation by
\gamma
and commutes with elements in
\operatorname{Unip}(J_{H,\operatorname{ord}})
, we see that
\hat{\iota}(x)\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}u_{c}v_{\underline{d}}=\hat{\iota}(x\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}v_{\underline{d}}\xi_{c})\xi_{c}^{-1}
. Set
F^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\phi_{t_{c}^{-1},\kappa^{*}}(F)
– explicitly, one has
F^{\prime}(-)=\kappa^{*}(\xi_{c})\xi_{c}^{-1}\cdot F(-\cdot\xi_{c}^{-1})
. Since
(\xi_{c}^{-1},\xi_{c}^{-1})\cdot\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}=\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}(\xi_{c})\delta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}
, we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}\left((u^{-1}\delta_{i})|_{U_{G,\beta}}\star\langle F(-),u^{-1}v_{i}\rangle\right)(\hat{\iota}(x\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}v_{\underline{d}}\xi_{c})\xi_{c}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\kappa^{*}(\xi_{c})^{-1}\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}(\xi_{c})\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F^{\prime})(x\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}v_{\underline{d}}\xi_{c})

\displaystyle=(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}\cdot\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F^{\prime})(x\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}v_{\underline{d}}\xi_{c}).

Therefore, we see that

\rho^{\operatorname{ord}}_{A,U}(F)=\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}\left[\psi_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ(1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-)\circ\phi_{\xi_{c},\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right](\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(F^{\prime})).

This coincides with
\rho^{\operatorname{ord}}_{B,U}
as required.
∎

We now consider an overconvergent version of this result. We will continually use the following fact: if
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
is a finite morphism of adic spaces which is of finite presentation, and
\mathscr{F}
is a sheaf on
\mathcal{Y}
which is locally free of finite rank, then
f^{-1}
sends
\mathscr{F}
-acyclic covers of
\mathcal{Y}
to
f^{*}\mathscr{F}
-acyclic covers of
\mathcal{X}
. This follows from a simple application of the projection formula. We will apply this to the setting where
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are both open subspaces of a third space
\mathcal{C}
,
\mathscr{F}
is a sheaf on
\mathcal{C}
locally free of finite rank, and
f
corresponds to a Hecke operator (for example Frobenius) satisfying the property
f^{*}\mathscr{F}|_{\mathcal{Y}}\cong\mathscr{F}|_{\mathcal{X}}
. One then sees that
f^{-1}
sends
\mathscr{F}
-acyclic covers of
\mathcal{Y}
to
\mathscr{F}
-acyclic covers of
\mathcal{X}
.

Since, in this section, we are working with Shimura varieties rather than the moduli spaces of unitary abelian varieties, we set
\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
and
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k}=\mathcal{X}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
for any integer
k\geq 1
. We will also frequently use the following notation: if
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
is an open cover of an adic space
\mathcal{X}
, then for any subset
\mathbf{I}\subset I
, we set
U_{\mathbf{I}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\cap_{i\in\mathbf{I}}U_{i}
.

We begin with the following proposition.

Proposition 8.5.9
.

Let
k\geq 1
be an integer such that
\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}\subset\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},\beta}
. Let
\mathfrak{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a finite
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
-acyclic cover of
\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
as in Lemma
6.4.5
(i.e.
U_{i}\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and
\mathfrak{V}=\{V_{i}\}_{i\in I}=\{U_{i}-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})\}_{i\in I}
is an acyclic cover). Then there exists a sufficiently large integer
k^{\prime}\geq k
and a finite collection
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}=\{U_{j}^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
of quasi-compact open subspaces of
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
such that:

(1)

There exists a surjective map
q\colon J\twoheadrightarrow I
such that for all
j\in J
:

U_{j}^{\prime}\subset A^{-1}(U_{q(j)})\cap\bigcap_{c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}B_{c}^{-1}(U_{q(j)}).

(2)

\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\subset\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}^{\prime}
and the covers
\{U_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\}_{j\in J}
and
\{V_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\}_{j\in J}
are
\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
-acyclic. Here
V_{j}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U_{j}^{\prime}-\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
.

(3)

For any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
, the restriction map

\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta}))

is injective.

(4)

For any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
and
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
:

•

The map
\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}
of ind-systems induces well-defined maps

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\left((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\right)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})

\displaystyle\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\left((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(V_{q(\mathbf{J})})\right)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(V^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})

which are functorial in
\mathbf{J}
(via the various restriction maps).

•

The map
1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-
of ind-systems induces well-defined maps:

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(V_{q(\mathbf{J})})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(V_{q(\mathbf{J})})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

which are functorial in
\mathbf{J}
, for some
r\gg 0
(not depending on
c
nor
\mathbf{J}
) satisfying

U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}\subset(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}\left[(t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right].

Proof.

Since
t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c}
only depends on
c
modulo
p^{\beta}
, we can certainly find an integer
r\gg 0
such that for any subset
\mathbf{I}\subset I
and
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, the maps
\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}
and
1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-
induce morphisms:

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\left((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\hat{\iota})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\left((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(V_{\mathbf{I}})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\hat{\iota})^{-1}(V_{\mathbf{I}})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(V_{\mathbf{I}})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(V_{\mathbf{I}})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

Note that
(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and
(t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\in\mathcal{C}_{H}
because the morphisms under which we are pulling back the opens extend to integral models over the ordinary loci.

Set
U_{i}^{\operatorname{ord}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}U_{i}\cap\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
. This is a quasi-compact open subspace which is the adic generic fibre of an open in
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
, hence we have
W_{i}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A^{-1}(U_{i}^{\operatorname{ord}})=B_{c}^{-1}(U_{i}^{\operatorname{ord}})
for any
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
(see Lemma
8.5.4
). Choose a refinement
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime\prime}=\{U_{j}^{\prime\prime}\}_{j\in J}
of the cover

\left\{A^{-1}(U_{i})\cap\bigcap_{c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}}B_{c}^{-1}(U_{i})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\cap(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r})\right\}_{i\in I}=:\{Y_{i}\}_{i\in I}

such that
\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}|_{U_{j}^{\prime\prime}}=\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U_{j}^{\prime\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{U_{j}^{\prime\prime}}
(for all
j\in J
). We may assume that
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime\prime}
is finite and consists of quasi-compact open subspaces. Let
q\colon J\twoheadrightarrow I
be a surjective map such that

U_{j}^{\prime\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})=U_{j}^{\prime\prime}\cap W_{q(j)}\subset U_{j}^{\prime\prime}\subset Y_{q(j)}.

Let
U_{j}^{\prime}
denote the union of all connected components of
U_{j}^{\prime\prime}
which intersect non-trivially with
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
. Then, for any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
, the map
\pi_{0}(U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta}))\twoheadrightarrow\pi_{0}(U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime})
is surjective. Since
U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}
is an open subspace of a smooth qcqs adic space, this implies that the natural restriction map
\mathcal{O}(U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{O}(U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta}))
is injective. This gives part (3) because
\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
is free over
U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
\cup_{i\in I}Y_{i}=\cup_{j\in J}U_{j}^{\prime\prime}
contains the closure of
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
, hence
\cup_{j\in J}U_{j}^{\prime}
must also contain the closure of
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
. This implies that there exists an integer
k^{\prime}\geq k
such that
\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\subset\cup_{j\in J}U_{j}^{\prime}
. The rest of the proposition easily follows.
∎

We now define the overconvergent morphisms.

Definition 8.5.10
.

Let
\mathfrak{U}
be a finite cover as in Proposition
8.5.9
, and let
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}
be the corresponding collection of quasi-compact open subspaces of
\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta})
.

(1)

For any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
, let
\rho^{\dagger}_{A,\mathbf{J}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})
denote the continuous
L
-linear morphism (which is functorial in
\mathbf{J}
) given by the following composition:

\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\xrightarrow{\psi_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\xrightarrow{\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}).

We define
\rho^{\dagger}_{A,\mathbf{J}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V_{q(\mathbf{J})})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(V^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})
similarly, which is the unique continuous
L
-linear morphism extending
\rho^{\dagger}_{A,\mathbf{J}}
.

(2)

Let
c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. For any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
, let
\rho^{\dagger}_{B_{c},\mathbf{J}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})
denote the continuous
L
-linear morphism (which is functorial in
\mathbf{J}
) given by the following composition:

\displaystyle\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{t_{c}^{-1},\kappa^{*}}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((t_{c}^{-1})^{-1}U_{q(\mathbf{J})})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota})^{-1}U_{q(\mathbf{J})})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{\xi_{c},\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}U_{q(\mathbf{J})})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\left((t_{c}^{-1}\circ\hat{\iota}\circ\xi_{c})^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{r}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\psi_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}^{\beta^{\prime}}}\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}).

We define
\rho^{\dagger}_{B_{c},\mathbf{J}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V_{q(\mathbf{J})})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(V^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})
similarly, which is the unique continuous
L
-linear morphism extending
\rho^{\dagger}_{B_{c},\mathbf{J}}
.

We set

\rho^{\dagger}_{B,\mathbf{J}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}\rho^{\dagger}_{B_{c},\mathbf{J}}

for a fixed set of representatives of
\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
.

Lemma 8.5.11
.

One has
\rho^{\dagger}_{A,\mathbf{J}}=\rho^{\dagger}_{B,\mathbf{J}}
.

Proof.

Since the morphisms
\rho^{\dagger}_{A,\mathbf{J}},\rho^{\dagger}_{B,\mathbf{J}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(V_{q(\mathbf{J})})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(V^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})
uniquely extend

(8.5.12)

\rho^{\dagger}_{A,\mathbf{J}},\rho^{\dagger}_{B,\mathbf{J}}\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})\to\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})

it suffices to prove the morphisms in (
8.5.12
) are equal. Set
U_{q(\mathbf{J})}^{\operatorname{ord}}=U_{q(\mathbf{J})}\cap\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
which is a quasi-compact open subspace which is the adic generic fibre of an open in
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
. Set
(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})^{\operatorname{ord}}=U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})
and note that
(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})^{\operatorname{ord}}\subset A^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})
. Then for
\bullet=A,B
we have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\operatorname{ord}}_{q(\mathbf{J})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}((U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})^{\operatorname{ord}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}\circ\rho^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\bullet,U^{\operatorname{ord}}_{q(\mathbf{J})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\dagger}_{\bullet,\mathbf{J}}}

where the vertical arrows are given by restriction. The result now follows from Lemma
8.5.6
because the right-hand vertical map is injective (see Proposition
8.5.9
).
∎

We now consider the analogous morphisms on Cech complexes. More precisely, let
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=\{U_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\}_{j\in J}
denote the
\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}
-acyclic cover as in Proposition
8.5.9
. Then, for
\bullet=A,B
, we obtain a commutative diagram of Cech complexes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}};\mathfrak{U})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}};\mathfrak{V})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}};\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}};\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}\circ\rho^{\dagger}_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}\circ\rho^{\dagger}_{\bullet}}

where
\operatorname{res}
denotes restriction from elements of
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}
(resp.
\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}
) to
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
(resp.
\mathfrak{V}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}
). Here
\rho^{\dagger}_{\bullet}
denotes the induced morphism of complexes built up from the maps
\rho^{\dagger}_{\bullet,\mathbf{J}}
for different subsets
\mathbf{J}\subset J
. By passing to cohomology of the mapping fibres of the horizontal arrows, we obtain morphisms

\operatorname{H}^{n-1}(\rho^{\dagger}_{\bullet})\colon\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{\mathcal{Z}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\operatorname{id}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}},\mathscr{M}_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}\right).

By Lemma
8.5.11
, one has
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}(\rho^{\dagger}_{A})=\operatorname{H}^{n-1}(\rho^{\dagger}_{B})
. On the other hand, using the acyclicity property of finite morphisms discussed just before Proposition
8.5.9
:

•

One has
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}(\rho^{\dagger}_{A})=\vartheta^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j+\chi_{p},\beta}\circ\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}
.

•

One has

\operatorname{H}^{n-1}(\rho^{\dagger}_{B})=\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}\left(\varphi_{H}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ(1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-)\circ U_{\xi_{c}}\circ\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\dagger}\circ\widetilde{U}_{t_{c}^{-1}}\right)

where
\widetilde{U}_{t_{c}^{-1}}=(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{c})U_{t_{c}^{-1}}
.

We therefore obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 8.5.13
.

For any
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
, one has

\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}\eta)=p^{\beta^{\prime}\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-1}(p-1)\chi(z_{\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}})\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(-1)\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}})\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\eta).

Proof.

We first note that
\widetilde{U}_{t_{c}^{-1}}
acts on the cohomology group
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
through multiplication by
\kappa^{*}(\xi_{c})
. Furthermore, for
0\leq m\leq\beta
, let
F_{1}^{m}
and
F_{2}^{m}
denote the operators on
\operatorname{H}^{0}_{\operatorname{id}}\left(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j],\vee}\right)^{(+,\dagger)}
which are adjoint under Serre duality to
\varphi_{H}^{m}
and
(\varphi_{H}^{t})^{m}
respectively, where
\varphi_{H}^{t}
denotes the colimit (after passing to cohomology) of the morphisms
\operatorname{deg}(\varphi)^{-1}\operatorname{Tr}_{\varphi}\circ\psi_{H,\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]}}^{-1}
. One can easily check that
F_{1}^{m}=U_{\xi^{-m}}
and one has the relation
F_{1}^{m}\circ F_{2}^{m}=\operatorname{id}
. By Lemma
8.5.3
, we therefore see that

F_{1}^{m}(\operatorname{res}[\chi])=(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(\langle\xi^{m}\rangle)\hat{\chi}(z_{\xi^{-m}})\operatorname{res}[\chi],\quad\quad F_{2}^{m}(\operatorname{res}[\chi])=(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(\langle\xi^{m}\rangle)^{-1}\hat{\chi}(z_{\xi^{-m}})^{-1}\operatorname{res}[\chi].

Note that
(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(\langle\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\rangle)\hat{\chi}(z_{\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}})=\chi(z_{\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}})p^{\beta^{\prime}(\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}})}
. By Remark
8.5.20
, we have

(\varphi^{t}_{H})^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\circ(1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-)=(\varphi^{t}_{H})^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}

hence
\langle 1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star x,\operatorname{res}[\chi]\rangle=\langle x,\operatorname{res}[\chi]\rangle
for any
x\in\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]})^{(-,\dagger)}
. We also have
U_{\xi_{c}^{-1}}\cdot\operatorname{res}[\chi]=(2\rho_{H,\operatorname{nc}})(\langle\xi_{c}\rangle)\hat{\chi}(z_{\xi_{c}^{-1}})\cdot\operatorname{res}[\chi]=\chi_{p,\tau_{0}}(c)(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{j_{\tau_{0}}+\kappa_{1,\tau_{0}}}\cdot\operatorname{res}[\chi]
.

Putting this all together, we find that
\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger,\circ}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}\eta)
is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}

\displaystyle\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(c)^{-1}(c+p^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-j_{\tau_{0}}}\langle\left(\varphi_{H}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ(1_{U_{H,\beta^{\prime}}}\star-)\circ U_{\xi_{c}}\circ\vartheta_{\kappa,j,\beta}^{\dagger}\circ\widetilde{U}_{t_{c}^{-1}}\right)(\eta),\operatorname{res}[\chi]\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}j_{\tau_{0}}}}{p^{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\sum_{c\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\chi(z_{\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}})p^{\beta^{\prime}(\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}})}\langle\vartheta^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\beta}(\eta),\operatorname{res}[\chi]\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{p^{\beta^{\prime}(1+\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}})}(p-1)}{p\;\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})}\chi(z_{\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}}})\operatorname{Ev}^{\dagger}_{\kappa,j,\chi,\beta}(\eta).

Now using the relation

\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}^{-1})^{-1}=\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}}(-1)p^{-\beta^{\prime}}\mathscr{G}(\chi_{p,\bar{\tau}_{0}})

the result follows.
∎

8.5.3.
Step 3

To complete the proof of Theorem
8.5.1
, we need a relation between Hecke operators, Frobenius and the action of indicator functions. As above, we will establish this over the ordinary locus and then use the injectivity of certain restriction maps to deduce the overconvergent version. For
i=0,1
, recall that
t_{i}\in T^{G,-}
denotes the element which is the identity outside the
\tau_{0}
-component, and in the
\tau_{0}
-component is equal to

\operatorname{diag}(1,\dots,1,p,\dots,p)

where there are
n-i
lots of
p
. We will consider the Hecke operators associated with these elements as in §
8.2
. However to be able to establish a relation between them, we need to view them as a cohomological correspondence for the same (topological) correspondence. We first consider the correspondences over the ordinary locus. Let
t^{\prime}_{i}=w_{n}t_{i}w_{n}^{-1}\in J_{G,\operatorname{ord}}
. With notation as in §
8.2
, we have a commutative diagram:

(8.5.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,1}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,0}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot(t_{1}^{\prime})^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot(t_{0}^{\prime})^{\beta^{\prime}}}

where
J_{p,i}^{\prime}=(t_{i}^{\prime})^{\beta^{\prime}}J_{p}(t_{i}^{\prime})^{-\beta^{\prime}}\cap J_{p}
and the unlabelled arrows are the natural maps. The inner square is not Cartesian in general, however
\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J^{\prime}_{p,1}
is an open and closed subspace of the Cartesian product
\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}\times_{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,0}^{\prime}
(with respect to the maps in the bottom half of the square). We will denote the left-hand (resp. right-hand) curved arrow by
q_{1}
(resp.
q_{2}
).

We claim that we have an overconvergent version of the above diagram. To see this, consider the following compact open subgroups. Let
K_{p}=K^{G}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})
,
K_{p,i}^{\prime}=t_{i}^{\beta^{\prime}}K_{p}t_{i}^{-\beta^{\prime}}\cap K_{p}
,
K_{p,\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{\prime}=(w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n})K_{p}(w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n})^{-1}\cap K_{p}
, and
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}=K_{p,\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{\prime}\cap(w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n})K_{p,0}^{\prime}(w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n})^{-1}
. Note that
K_{p}^{\prime\prime}\subset K_{p,1}^{\prime}
. We have a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p,\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p,0}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}}}

and
\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}
is an open and closed subspace in the Cartesian product
\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p,\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{\prime}}\times_{\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}}}\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p,0}^{\prime}}
formed from the maps in the bottom half of the middle square. One can easily show that the natural map
\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p}^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathcal{S}_{G,K_{p,1}^{\prime}}
induces an isomorphism
a\colon\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p}^{\prime\prime},k}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k}
. We obtain a commutative diagram:

(8.5.15)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}^{\prime},k+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k+\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\star)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdot t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}}}

where the map
(\star)
is the composition of
a^{-1}
with
\cdot w_{n}^{-1}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}w_{n}
. We will also denote the left-hand (resp. right-hand map) in the above diagram by
q_{1}
(resp.
q_{2}
). We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,1}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}_{K_{p},k+2\beta^{\prime}}^{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}

and the left-hand square is Cartesian for
k
sufficiently large (and the vertical maps are induced from right-translation by
w_{n}
).

Consider the inner diamond of (
8.5.15
) (and identifying
\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}},k+2\beta^{\prime}}\cong\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+2\beta^{\prime}}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k+\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k+\beta^{\prime}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

Note that all the maps in this diagram are finite étale. Let
\mathfrak{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a finite
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
-acyclic cover of
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}\subset\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}}
as in Lemma
6.4.5
, where
k_{0}\geq 1
is an auxiliary integer. Since
\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}
is finite and integral over the ordinary locus, we see that
(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{i})\in\mathcal{C}_{G}
and the cover
\{(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{i})\}_{i\in I}
is
(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\cong\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
-acyclic. For any subset
\mathbf{I}\subset I
, we consider the following two maps:

•

The composition
\alpha_{0,\mathbf{I}}
given by

\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}}))\xrightarrow{(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\operatorname{Tr}_{\mu}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{\mathbf{I}})\xrightarrow{\psi^{\beta^{\prime}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})).

•

The composition
\alpha_{1,\mathbf{I}}
given by

\displaystyle\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}}))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\lambda^{*}}\lambda^{*}\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\lambda^{-1}\mu^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}}))

\displaystyle=q_{1}^{*}(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(q_{1}^{-1}(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}}))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}})\operatorname{Tr}_{q_{1}}}(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}}))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\psi^{\beta^{\prime}}_{G,\kappa^{*}}}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{\mathbf{I}})).

Let
\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\in C^{\operatorname{la}}(U_{G,\beta},L)
denote the indicator function associated with the subset
(p^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\oplus n}\oplus\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\oplus n-1}\subset U_{G,\beta}
. Then, following the same strategy as in the proof of Proposition
8.5.9
, there exists an integer
k^{\prime}\geq k
and a finite collection
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}=\{U_{j}^{\prime}\}_{j\in J}
of quasi-compact open subspaces of
\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}}
, with the following properties:

•

One has a surjective map
q\colon J\twoheadrightarrow I
such that: for any
j\in J

U^{\prime}_{j}\subset(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{q(j)}).

Furthermore, we have an action map
\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-\colon\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})}))\to\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})
for any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
.

•

We have
\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\subset\bigcup_{j\in J}U_{j}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{U}^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}}=\{U_{j}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\}_{j\in J}
is a
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}
-acyclic cover.

•

For any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
, the restriction map
\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime})\to\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U_{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta}))
is injective.

This leads to the following lemma.

Lemma 8.5.16
.

For any subset
\mathbf{J}\subset J
, we have
\operatorname{res}\circ\alpha_{0,q(\mathbf{J})}=(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ\alpha_{1,q(\mathbf{J})}
as morphisms

\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})}))\to\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}).

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may work over
\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\operatorname{cycl}}
. We can then consider the following diagram (which is the inner diamond of (
8.5.14
)):

(8.5.17)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,1}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,0}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}=:\;\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

and define ordinary analogues of the morphisms above. Indeed, for any open
U\subset\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})=\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}
arising as the adic generic fibre of an open in
\mathfrak{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
, we define:

•

\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{0,U}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\psi_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\operatorname{Tr}_{\mu}
,

•

\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{1,U}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\psi_{G,\kappa^{*}}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}})\operatorname{Tr}_{q_{1}}\circ\;\lambda^{*}
,

both of which are morphisms
\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U))\to\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}((\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}})^{-1}(U))
. We then have commutative diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}\circ\;\alpha_{0,q(\mathbf{J})}^{\operatorname{ord}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}\circ\;\alpha_{0,q(\mathbf{J})}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U_{q(\mathbf{J})}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(U^{\prime}_{\mathbf{J}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ\;\alpha_{1,q(\mathbf{J})}^{\operatorname{ord}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ\;\alpha_{1,q(\mathbf{J})}}

where the vertical maps are restriction, and
\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{i,q(\mathbf{J})}
denotes the morphism
\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{i,W}
above for
W=U_{q(\mathbf{J})}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})
. Since the right-hand vertical maps are injective, it suffices to prove that
\operatorname{res}\circ\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{0,q(\mathbf{J})}=(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{1,q(\mathbf{J})}
. We can do this for any
U
as above, i.e. show that
\alpha_{0,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}=(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ\alpha_{1,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}
. But this follows from an explicit calculation. Indeed, let
U(J_{p})
and
M(J_{p})
denote the unipotent and Levi parts of
J_{p}
respectively, so that
J_{p}=U(J_{p})\rtimes M(J_{p})
. We use similar notation for
J_{p,i}^{\prime}
(
i=0,1
). Let
\pi\colon\mathcal{IG}_{G}/U(J_{p})\to\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p}
and
\pi_{i}^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{IG}_{G}/U(J_{p,i}^{\prime})\to\mathcal{IG}_{G}/J_{p,i}^{\prime}
denote the natural maps, and consider the following diagram lifting the morphisms in (
8.5.17
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/U(J_{p,1}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/U(J_{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{IG}_{G}/U(J_{p,0}^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{IG}_{G}/U(J_{p})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mu}}

(i.e.,
\pi^{\prime}_{0}\circ\widetilde{\lambda}=\lambda\circ\pi^{\prime}_{1}
etc.). Let
F\in\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}(\mu^{-1}(U))
, which we view as a function

F\colon(\pi_{0}^{\prime})^{-1}(\mu^{-1}U)\to V_{\kappa}^{*}

satisfying a certain transformation property under the action of
M(J^{\prime}_{p,0})
. Then for any
x\in(\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\pi)^{-1}(U)
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{0,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}(F)(x)

\displaystyle=(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{m\in M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,0}^{\prime})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in(\pi\circ\widetilde{\mu})^{-1}(U)\\
\widetilde{\mu}(y)=x\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\cdot m\end{subarray}}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}m\cdot F(y)

\displaystyle\alpha_{1,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}(F)(x)

\displaystyle=(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}})\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{m\in M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,1}^{\prime})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in(\pi\circ\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\widetilde{q}_{1})^{-1}(U)\\
\widetilde{q}_{1}(z)=x\cdot m\end{subarray}}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}m\cdot F(\widetilde{\lambda}(z)).

Note that
M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,0}^{\prime})=M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,1}^{\prime})
. For
m\in M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,0}^{\prime})
, consider the following sets:

\displaystyle X_{0,m}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{y\in(\pi\circ\widetilde{\mu})^{-1}(U):\widetilde{\mu}(y)=x\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}\cdot m\right\}

\displaystyle X_{1,m}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left\{z\in(\pi\circ\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\widetilde{q}_{1})^{-1}(U):\widetilde{q}_{1}(z)=x\cdot m\right\}.

Then since
\xi
commutes with
M(J_{p})
, we clearly have
\widetilde{\lambda}(X_{1,m})\subset X_{0,m}
. Note that
\widetilde{q}_{1}
is an isomorphism and
\widetilde{\mu}
has degree
p^{n\beta^{\prime}}
. Write
X_{1,m}=\{x_{1,m}\}
. Then we have

X_{0,m}=\{\widetilde{\lambda}(x_{1,m})\cdot u:u\in U(J_{p})/U(J_{p,0}^{\prime})\}=\{\widetilde{\lambda}(x_{1,m}\cdot v):v\in U(J_{p})^{1/p^{\beta^{\prime}}}/U(J_{p})\}.

Hence

\displaystyle\alpha_{0,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}(F)(x)

\displaystyle=(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{m\in M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,0}^{\prime})}\sum_{y\in X_{0,m}}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}m\cdot F(y)

\displaystyle=(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{m\in M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,1}^{\prime})}\sum_{v\in U(J_{p})^{1/p^{\beta^{\prime}}}/U(J_{p})}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}m\cdot F(\widetilde{\lambda}(x_{1,m}\cdot v))

\displaystyle=(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\kappa^{*}(\xi^{-\beta^{\prime}})\sum_{m\in M(J_{p})/M(J_{p,1}^{\prime})}\sum_{v\in U(J_{p})^{1/p^{\beta^{\prime}}}/U(J_{p})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\in(\pi\circ\varphi^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\widetilde{q}_{1})^{-1}(U)\\
\widetilde{q}_{1}(z)=x\cdot(mvm^{-1})m\end{subarray}}\xi^{\beta^{\prime}}m\cdot F(\widetilde{\lambda}(z))

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in U(J_{p})^{1/p^{\beta^{\prime}}}/U(J_{p})}\frac{(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})}{(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}})}\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{1,U}(F)(x\cdot v)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{n\beta^{\prime}}}\sum_{v\in U(J_{p})^{1/p^{\beta^{\prime}}}/U(J_{p})}\alpha^{\operatorname{ord}}_{1,U}(F)(x\cdot v)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star\alpha_{1,U}^{\operatorname{ord}}(F))(x)

as required. Here in the fourth equality we have replaced
mvm^{-1}
by
v
, since conjugation by
m
permutes
U(J_{p})^{1/p^{\beta^{\prime}}}/U(J_{p})
.
∎

We now consider the following morphisms
\widetilde{\alpha}_{0,\mathbf{J}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\operatorname{res}\circ\alpha_{0,q(\mathbf{J})}
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{1,\mathbf{J}}=\operatorname{res}\circ(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ\alpha_{1,q(\mathbf{J})}
which induce maps of Cech complexes

\widetilde{\alpha}_{0},\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}\colon\operatorname{Cech}(\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}};\{\mu^{-1}(U_{i})\}_{i\in I})\to\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}};\{U^{\prime}_{j}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\}_{j\in J}).

By Lemma
8.5.16
, these maps of Cech complexes coincide. After possibly increasing
k^{\prime}
, these morphisms extend uniquely to

\widetilde{\alpha}_{0},\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}\colon\operatorname{Cech}(\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}};\{\mu^{-1}(V_{i})\}_{i\in I})\to\operatorname{Cech}(\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}};\{V^{\prime}_{j}\cap\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k^{\prime}}\}_{j\in J})

where
V_{i}=U_{i}\cap\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})\right)
and
V^{\prime}_{j}=U^{\prime}_{j}\cap\left(\mathcal{X}_{G,\operatorname{Iw}}(p^{\beta})-\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})\right)
. For ease of notation, set
\mathcal{Z}=\mathcal{Z}_{G,>n+1}(p^{\beta})
and
\mathcal{X}_{k}=\mathcal{X}_{G,w_{n}}(p^{\beta})_{k}
. We therefore see that

•

\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}
induces a map on cohomology

\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}\colon\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z})}\left(\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}),\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{X}_{k^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{k^{\prime}},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

which is just
(-w_{n}^{-1}\rho+\rho)(t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}})\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\operatorname{Tr}_{\mu}
.

•

\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}
induces a map on cohomology

\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}\colon\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z})}\left(\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}),\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)\to\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{X}_{k^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{X}_{k^{\prime}},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

which is just
(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ(-w_{n}\rho+\rho)(t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}})\operatorname{Tr}_{q_{1}}\circ\;q_{1}^{*}(\psi^{\beta^{\prime}}_{G,\kappa^{*}})\circ\lambda^{*}
.

These two morphisms are equal.

Corollary 8.5.18
.

We have
\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ U_{t_{0}}^{\beta^{\prime}}=(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ U_{t_{1}}^{\beta^{\prime}}
as endomorphisms of
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
.

Proof.

Consider the right-half of the diagram in (
8.5.15
), i.e.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lambda}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}

and consider the map

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}\colon\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}}\cap\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sigma^{*}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}}\cap\sigma^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}},\sigma^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{t_{0}^{\beta^{\prime}},\kappa^{*}}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}}\cap\sigma^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}},\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{cores}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}}\cap\mu^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,0}^{\prime},k_{0}+\beta^{\prime}},\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z})}\left(\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}),\mu^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

where the corestriction is well-defined because
\sigma^{-1}\mathcal{Z}\subset\mu^{-1}\mathcal{Z}
(see Lemma
8.2.4
). Also consider the map:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}\colon\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}}\cap\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p},k_{0}},\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{q_{2}^{*}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}}\cap q_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}},q_{2}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\phi_{t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}},\kappa^{*}}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}}\cap q_{2}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}},q_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{cores}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}}\cap q_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{Z}}\left(\mathcal{U}^{G}_{K_{p,1}^{\prime},k_{0}+2\beta^{\prime}},q_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\operatorname{res}}\operatorname{H}^{\bullet}_{\lambda^{-1}\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}\cap\mathcal{Z})}\left(\lambda^{-1}\mu^{-1}(\mathcal{X}_{k}),q_{1}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{G,\kappa^{*}}\right).

One can easily verify that
\gamma_{1}=q_{1}^{*}(\psi^{\beta^{\prime}}_{G,\kappa^{*}})\circ\lambda^{*}\circ\gamma_{0}
. We then see that

(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ(-w_{n}\rho+\rho)(t_{1}^{\beta^{\prime}})\operatorname{Tr}_{q_{1}}\circ\gamma_{1}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{1}\circ\gamma_{0}=\widetilde{\alpha}_{0}\circ\gamma_{0}.

The left-hand side induces the operator
(\mathbb{I}_{\beta^{\prime}}\star-)\circ U_{t_{1}}^{\beta^{\prime}}
and the right-hand side induces the operator
\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ U_{t_{0}}^{\beta^{\prime}}
(both after passing to the colimit over
k_{0}
and
k^{\prime}
). They must therefore be equal.
∎

Remark 8.5.19
.

One can also show that
U_{t_{0}}^{\beta^{\prime}}\circ\varphi_{G}^{\beta^{\prime}}=U_{t_{1}}^{\beta^{\prime}}
as endomorphisms of
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
, however we will not need this. We leave the details to the reader.

Remark 8.5.20
.

By exactly the same method (which is even simpler in this case), one can show that, for
0\leq m\leq\beta
,
(\varphi_{H}^{t})^{m}\circ\varphi_{H}^{m}=\operatorname{id}
and
(\varphi_{H})^{m}\circ(\varphi_{H}^{t})^{m}=1_{U_{H,\beta-m}}\star-
as endomorphisms of
\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{\operatorname{id}}(\mathcal{S}_{H,\diamondsuit}(p^{\beta}),\sigma_{\kappa}^{[j]})^{(-,\dagger)}
.

8.5.4.
Step 4

We now finish the proof of Theorem
8.5.1
. Let
\eta\in\operatorname{H}^{n-1}_{w_{n}}(\kappa^{*};\beta)^{(-,\dagger)}
and suppose that
U_{t_{i}}\cdot\eta=\alpha_{i}\eta
for
i=0,1
and some
\alpha_{i}\in L^{\times}
. Then
