. By the transformation properties of
f
, we see that

f\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&U_{\sigma}+u^{\prime}\\
&1\end{array}\right)\right)=f\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
&Z^{-1}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&U_{\sigma}+u^{\prime}\\
&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
&Z\end{array}\right)\right)=f\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&CZ\\
&1\end{array}\right)\right)

which doesn’t depend on
T_{n}
.

We now prove part (2). Set
\sigma^{\prime}(i)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}a+1-\sigma(a+1-i)
, and
\sigma^{\prime\prime}(i)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}a+1-\sigma(i)
. Let
U_{\sigma}\in M_{a\times b}(R)
denote the matrix

(U_{\sigma})_{i,j}=\delta_{b+1-\sigma(i),j}T_{a+1-i}=\delta_{b-a+\sigma^{\prime\prime}(i),j}T_{a+1-i}.

Then, as above, we can calculate
(\mu_{\sigma}\star f)(u)
be the same expression in (
A.0.4
). Let
C=U_{\sigma}+u^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime}\in\operatorname{GL}_{a}(R)
the right-hand
(a\times a)
block. Then
C_{i,j}=0
if
j\in\{1,\dots,b-a\}
, and
C^{\prime}=w_{a}X_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{(a)}(t)
where
t=\operatorname{diag}(T_{1},\dots,T_{a})
. Let

•

A=w_{a}X_{\sigma^{\prime}}^{(a)}(t)^{+}w_{a}^{-1}\in\operatorname{GL}_{a}(R)
which is lower-triangular. One has

\operatorname{det}A=\prod_{\mathfrak{c}}\left(1+\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{c})\prod_{k\in|\mathfrak{c}|}T_{k}\right)

by Lemma
A.0.2
, where the product is over all cycles
\mathfrak{c}
which appear in the decomposition of
\sigma^{\prime}
.

•

X=A^{-1}
which is lower-triangular. By Lemma
A.0.2
, its projection to the diagonal torus is of the form
(x_{1},\dots,x_{a})
where

x_{j}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\left(1+\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{c})\prod_{k\in|\mathfrak{c}|}T_{k}\right)^{-1}&\text{ if }j=a+1-\operatorname{min}(\mathfrak{c})\text{ for some cycle }\mathfrak{c}\\
1\text{ otherwise }\end{array}\right..

•

Z\in\operatorname{GL}_{b}(R)
is the matrix given by
Z_{i,j}=\delta_{i,j}
if either
i
or
j
is not contained in
\{b-a+1,\dots,b\}
, and
Z_{i,j}=(X^{(a)}_{\sigma^{\prime}}(t)^{-})_{i-(b-a),j-(b-a)}
if
i,j\in\{b-a+1,\dots,b\}
. This lies in the lower triangular unipotent of
\operatorname{GL}_{b}(R)
.

•

B=Z^{-1}
, which satisfies
\operatorname{det}B=1
.

Using the transformation properties for
f
, we see that:

\displaystyle f\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&U_{\sigma}+u^{\prime}\\
&1\end{array}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=f\left(\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}A&\\
&B\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&u^{\prime}\\
&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}X&\\
&Z\end{array}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=(\operatorname{det}A)^{\nu_{1}}\prod_{\mathfrak{c}}\left(1+\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{c})\prod_{k\in|\mathfrak{c}|}T_{k}\right)^{\kappa_{a+1-\operatorname{min}(\mathfrak{c})}}f(u)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\mathfrak{c}}\left(1+\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{c})\prod_{k\in|\mathfrak{c}|}T_{k}\right)^{\nu_{1}+\kappa_{a+1-\operatorname{min}(\mathfrak{c})}}f(u).

Differentiating and setting
T_{1}=\cdots=T_{a}=0
, we see that

(\mu_{\sigma}\star f)(u)=(-1)^{a}\prod_{\mathfrak{c}}\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{c})(\nu_{1}+\kappa_{a+1-\operatorname{min}(\mathfrak{c})}).

To conclude, we note that
\prod_{\mathfrak{c}}\operatorname{sgn}(\mathfrak{c})=\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma^{\prime})=\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)
, and the cycles appearing in the decomposition of
\sigma
are
w_{a}\circ\mathfrak{c}\circ w_{a}
(and we have
\operatorname{max}(w_{a}\circ\mathfrak{c}\circ w_{a})=a+1-\operatorname{min}(\mathfrak{c})
). Here
w_{a}
is the permutation of
\{1,\dots,a\}
given by
w_{a}(i)=a+1-i
.
∎

A.1.
Explicit branching operators

We now return to the setting of §
2.2
. It turns out that (up to a non-zero rational number depending on
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
), one can express
v_{\kappa}^{[j]}
in terms of
v_{\kappa}^{[0]}
via certain explicit elements of the universal enveloping algebras of
\mathfrak{g}=\operatorname{Lie}(G)
and
\mathfrak{m}_{G}=\operatorname{Lie}M_{G}
. More precisely, let
J\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
be a multiset of size
j_{\tau_{0}}
. Fix a basis
\{e_{J}\}
of
S_{-j}
(as
J
runs over all such multisets) such that a general torus element
(x;y_{1,\tau},\dots,y_{2n,\tau})\in T
acts on
e_{J}
as

(x;y_{1,\tau},\dots,y_{2n,\tau})\cdot e_{J}=y_{1,\tau_{0}}^{j_{\tau_{0}}}\left(\prod_{i\in J}y_{i,\tau_{0}}^{-1}\right)e_{J}.

If
j_{\tau_{0}}=0
, then we identify
S_{-j}
with the trivial representation and the basis is a singleton
\{e_{\varnothing}\}
.

For any
(i,j)\in\{1,\dots,2n\}
, let
E_{i,j,\tau}\in\mathfrak{g}
denote the element which is zero outside the
\tau
-component, and in the
\tau
-component is equal to the elementary
(2n\times 2n)
-matrix with non-zero entry in the
(i,j)
-th place. For any
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
, let
\operatorname{det}_{\tau}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2n})
denote the element obtained as the determinant of the
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2n})
-valued
(n\times n)
-matrix
(E_{i,j+n,\tau})_{i,j}
(where
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
). This is well-defined and independent of any ordering because all the elementary matrices considered commute with each other. We view
\operatorname{det}_{\tau}
as elements of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{m}_{G})
by identifying
\mathfrak{gl}_{2n}
with the
\tau
-component of
\mathfrak{m}_{G}
.

Similarly, for any
k\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
, we define
(-1)^{k-(n+1)}\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{2n-1})
as the determinant of the
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{2n-1})
-valued
(n-1\times n-1)
-matrix whose
(i,j)
-th component is

\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}E_{i+1,j+n}&\text{ if }j<k-n\\
E_{i+1,j+n+1}&\text{ if }j\geq k-n\end{array}\right.

as
i,j
run through the set
\{1,\dots,n-1\}
. As above, this is well-defined and independent of ordering because the elementary matrices considered commute with each other. We view
\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}
as an element of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{m}_{G})
by identifying the
\tau_{0}
-component of
\mathfrak{m}_{G}
with
\mathfrak{gl}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{2n-1}
. Finally, for any multiset
J\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
of size
j_{\tau_{0}}
, we define

\operatorname{det}^{[j]}_{J}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\left(\prod_{k\in J}\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\right)\prod_{\tau\neq\tau_{0}}\operatorname{det}_{\tau}^{j_{\tau}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{m}_{G}).

Proposition A.1.1
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
. Then

\sum_{J}\operatorname{det}^{[j]}_{J}\cdot v_{\kappa}^{[0]}\otimes e_{J}\in V_{\kappa}\otimes S_{-j}

is a non-zero multiple of
v_{\kappa}^{[j]}
, where the sum runs over all multisets
J\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
of size
j_{\tau_{0}}
.

Proof.

For
\tau\in\Psi
, let
M_{G,\tau}
denote the
\tau
-component of
M_{G}
(so
M_{G,\tau_{0}}=\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{2n-1}
and
M_{G,\tau}=\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
for
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
). We use similar notation for
M_{H}
. Write

V_{\kappa}=(-)^{\kappa_{0}}\otimes\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Psi}V_{\kappa_{\tau}}

where
V_{\kappa_{\tau}}
is (up to isomorphism) the irreducible representation of
M_{G,\tau}
of highest weight
\kappa_{\tau}=(\kappa_{1,\tau},\dots,\kappa_{2n,\tau})
, and
(-)^{\kappa_{0}}
denotes the line on which
\operatorname{GL}_{1}
acts through the character
x\mapsto x^{\kappa_{0}}
. Similarly, we let
S_{-j,\tau_{0}}
denote the irreducible representation of
M_{H,\tau_{0}}
of highest weight
(j_{\tau_{0}},\dots,-j_{\tau_{0}})
.

In this proof only, we will use a slightly different identification of
V_{\kappa_{\tau}}
with algebraic functions than in §
2.2
. More precisely, we identify
V_{\kappa_{\tau}}
with the space of algebraic functions

f\colon M_{G,\tau}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}

such that
f(-b)=\kappa_{\tau}(b^{-1})f(-)
for any
b
in the standard
lower-triangular
Borel subgroup of
M_{G,\tau}
. Then
v_{\kappa}^{[0]}
is identified with

1\otimes\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Psi}f_{\tau}\in(-)^{\kappa_{0}}\otimes\bigotimes_{\tau\in\Psi}V_{\kappa_{\tau}}

where
f_{\tau}\colon M_{G,\tau}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
are functions as above, with
f_{\tau}(m^{-1}-)=\sigma_{\kappa}^{[0],-1}(m)f_{\tau}(-)
for any
m\in M_{H,\tau}
. If we let
u_{\tau}^{t}
denote the transpose in
M_{G,\tau}
of the
\tau
-component of
u
(see Definition
2.1.1
), then
u_{\tau}^{t}
is a representative for the open orbit of
M_{H,\tau}
on the quotient of
M_{G,\tau}
by the lower-triangular Borel subgroup. Therefore, we have
f_{\tau}(u_{\tau}^{t})\neq 0
for all
\tau\in\Psi
.

It suffices to prove the claim for each individual
\tau\in\Psi
. We first deal with the case
\tau\neq\tau_{0}
. In this setting, we have an algebraic function

f_{\tau}\colon\operatorname{GL}_{2n}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}

such that
f(m^{-1}-)=f(-)
for all
m\in M_{H,\tau}
, and
f_{\tau}(-b)=\kappa_{\tau}(b^{-1})f(-)
for all
b
in the lower-triangular Borel subgroup of
\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
. Note that
\operatorname{Ad}(m)\operatorname{det}_{\tau}=\operatorname{det}m_{1}\operatorname{det}m_{2}^{-1}\operatorname{det}_{\tau}
for
m=(m_{1},m_{2})\in\operatorname{GL}_{n}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}=M_{H,\tau}
. By iterating Proposition
A.0.3
, we see that

(\operatorname{det}_{\tau}^{j_{\tau}}\cdot f_{\tau})(u_{\tau}^{t})=\pm C\cdot f_{\tau}(u_{\tau}^{t})

where
C
is product of terms of the form

(A.1.2)

\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\prod_{i}(\kappa_{m_{i},\tau}-\nu)

where
\nu
is an integer satisfying
0\leq\nu\leq j_{\tau}-1
, and
1\leq m_{i}\leq n
are integers depending on the cycle decomposition of
\sigma
. Here, in the notation of Proposition
A.0.3
, we have used that
\operatorname{det}_{\tau}=\pm\left(\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\mu_{\sigma}\right)
. Since
j_{\tau}\leq\kappa_{n,\tau}\leq\kappa_{n-1,\tau}\leq\cdots\leq\kappa_{1,\tau}
, we see that (
A.1.2
) is a sum of (non-zero) positive integers, hence must be non-zero itself. This implies
C\neq 0
and hence
\operatorname{det}_{\tau}^{j_{\tau}}\cdot f_{\tau}
is non-zero. By multiplicity one, it must therefore be a non-zero multiple of the
\tau
-component of
v_{\kappa}^{[j]}
.

We now consider the case
\tau=\tau_{0}
. Let
W_{-j,\tau_{0}}
denote the algebraic representation of
M_{G,\tau_{0}}
of highest weight
(j_{\tau_{0}},0,\dots,0,-j_{\tau_{0}})
. We can naturally view
S_{-j,\tau_{0}}\subset W_{-j,\tau_{0}}
, and note that the subspace
S_{-j,\tau_{0}}
is killed under the action of any elementary matrix
E_{i,j,\tau_{0}}\in\mathfrak{gl}_{1}\oplus\mathfrak{gl}_{2n-1}
with
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
and
j\in\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
. Recall from the proof of Theorem
2.2.4
that
V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}^{\prime}}
appears in
V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}}\otimes S_{-j,\tau_{0}}\subset V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}}\otimes W_{-j,\tau_{0}}
with multiplicity one, where
\kappa_{\tau_{0}}^{\prime}
is the weight

(\kappa_{1,\tau_{0}}+j_{\tau_{0}},\kappa_{2,\tau_{0}},\dots,\kappa_{n,\tau_{0}},\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}},\kappa_{n+2,\tau_{0}},\dots,\kappa_{2n,\tau_{0}}).

Suppose
0\leq j_{\tau_{0}}<\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+2,\tau_{0}}
. Suppose that
\sum_{J}\prod_{k\in J}\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\cdot f_{\tau_{0}}\otimes e_{J}\in V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}}\otimes S_{-j,\tau_{0}}\subset V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}}\otimes W_{-j,\tau_{0}}
is a non-zero multiple of the
\tau_{0}
-component of
v_{\kappa}^{[j]}
. Then, by multiplicity one, it must correspond to a function
F\in V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}^{\prime}}
.

Let
H\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\cdot F\otimes e_{\{k\}}\in V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}^{\prime}}\otimes S_{-1,\tau_{0}}
. Then the image of
H
under the map

(A.1.3)

V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}^{\prime}}\otimes S_{-1,\tau_{0}}\to V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}}\otimes S_{-j,\tau_{0}}\otimes S_{-1,\tau_{0}}\to V_{\kappa_{\tau_{0}}}\otimes S_{-(j+1),\tau_{0}}

is equal to

\sum_{l=n+1}^{2n}\sum_{J}\prod_{k\in J}\operatorname{det}_{l,\tau_{0}}\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\cdot f_{\tau_{0}}\otimes e_{J\cup\{l\}}=\sum_{A}\prod_{k\in A}\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\cdot f_{\tau_{0}}\otimes e_{A}

where
A
runs of all multisets in
\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
of size
j_{\tau_{0}}+1
, and the last map in (
A.1.3
) is the natural one using the fact that
S_{-j,\tau_{0}}=\operatorname{Sym}^{j_{\tau_{0}}}S_{-1,\tau_{0}}
. Note that Lemma
2.2.1
implies the map (
A.1.3
) is injective on the
M_{H,\tau_{0}}
-eigenspaces with eigencharacter:

\displaystyle M_{H,\tau_{0}}=\operatorname{GL}_{1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n-1}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}

\displaystyle\to\mathbb{G}_{m}

(A.1.4)

\displaystyle(m_{1},m_{2},m_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto m_{1}^{\kappa_{1,\tau_{0}}+j_{\tau_{0}}+1}\operatorname{det}m_{2}^{w+j_{\tau_{0}}+1-\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}}\operatorname{det}m_{3}^{\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-(j_{\tau_{0}}+1)}.

We will show that
H
is non-zero and an eigenvector under
M_{H,\tau_{0}}
with eigencharacter (
A.1.4
), and then the general claim follows from induction on
j_{\tau_{0}}
.

The fact that
H
transforms through the character (
A.1.4
) under the action of
M_{H,\tau_{0}}
follows from a direct calculation. To show
H
is non-zero, we will again apply Proposition
A.0.3
. It suffices to show that
(\operatorname{det}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\cdot F)(u_{\tau_{0}}^{t})
is non-zero.
9
9

9

In fact, one can also use Proposition
A.0.3
to show that
(\operatorname{det}_{k,\tau_{0}}\cdot F)(u_{\tau_{0}}^{t})=0
for any
k>n+1
.
But in the notation of Proposition
A.0.3
(with
a=n-1
,
b=n
), we see that
\operatorname{det}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}=\pm\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n-1}}\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\mu_{\sigma}
, where we naturally view
\mu_{\sigma}
in
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{m}_{G})
via the inclusion
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2n-1})\subset\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{m}_{G})
. But now we are in the setting of Proposition
A.0.3
(2), and hence
(\operatorname{det}_{n+1,\tau_{0}}\cdot F)(u_{\tau_{0}}^{t})=\pm C\cdot F(u_{\tau_{0}}^{t})
, where
C
is of the form

C=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n-1}}\prod_{i}(\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}}-w+\kappa_{m_{i},\tau_{0}})=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n-1}}\prod_{i}(\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{2n+2-m_{i},\tau_{0}}-j_{\tau_{0}})

where
2\leq m_{i}\leq n
are certain integers. This is non-zero because
j_{\tau_{0}}<\kappa_{n+1,\tau_{0}}-\kappa_{n+2,\tau_{0}}
, and
F(u_{\tau_{0}}^{t})
is non-zero by the induction hypothesis. This finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

Let
\operatorname{det}_{\tau_{0}}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2n})
denote the determinant of the
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{gl}_{2n})
-valued matrix
(E_{i,j+n,\tau_{0}})_{i,j}
with
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. We view this as an element of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g})
by identifying the
\tau_{0}
-component of
\mathfrak{g}
with
\mathfrak{gl}_{2n}
. We make the following definition:

Definition A.1.5
.

Let
(\kappa,j)\in\mathcal{E}
and recall the definition of
\delta_{\kappa,j}
from Definition
2.2.9
(which makes sense over any characteristic zero field). With notation as above:

(1)

Let
C_{\kappa,j}\in\mathbb{Q}^{\times}
denote the unique non-zero rational number satisfying

\delta_{\kappa,j}=C_{\kappa,j}\cdot\left(\sum_{J}\operatorname{det}^{[j]}_{J}\cdot\delta_{\kappa,0}\otimes x_{J}\right)

where the sum runs over all multisets
J\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
of size
j_{\tau_{0}}
, and
x_{J}\in C^{\operatorname{pol}}(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{\oplus 2n-1},\mathbb{G}_{a})
is the algebraic function satisfying
x_{J}(a_{2},\dots,a_{2n})=\prod_{k\in J}a_{k}
. Note that
C_{\kappa,j}
exists by Proposition
A.1.1
.

(2)

We define:

\Delta_{\kappa}^{[j]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}C_{\kappa,j}\cdot\prod_{\tau\in\Psi}\operatorname{det}_{\tau}^{j_{\tau}}\;\in\;\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{g}).

Note that
\Delta_{\kappa}^{[j]}=\sum_{J}\left(\prod_{k\in J}E_{1,k,\tau_{0}}\right)\operatorname{det}^{[j]}_{J}
, where the sum runs over all multisets
J\subset\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
of size
j_{\tau_{0}}
.

Appendix B
Equivariant linear functionals

Let
G=\operatorname{GL}_{2n}
and
H=\operatorname{GL}_{n}\times\operatorname{GL}_{n}
, and consider the block diagonal embedding
H\subset G
. In this section, we record some useful transformation properties for
H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant linear functionals on smooth representations of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

B.1.
Trace-compatibility

Let
\beta\geq 1
be an integer and let
K_{G,\beta}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denote the depth
p^{\beta}
upper-triangular Iwahori subgroup, i.e., all elements which land in the standard upper triangular Borel subgroup modulo
p^{\beta}
. Let
\hat{\gamma}\in G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
be any element such that
H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\cdot\hat{\gamma}\cdot B(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is Zariski dense in
G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
(such an element exists because the pair
(G,H)
is spherical). Here
B\subset G
denotes the upper-triangular Borel subgroup.

Let
K_{H,\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\hat{\gamma}K_{G,\beta}\hat{\gamma}^{-1}\cap H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Let
\nu\colon H\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
denote the morphism given by
\nu(h_{1},h_{2})=\operatorname{det}h_{2}/\operatorname{det}h_{1}
. Then
K_{H,\beta}
is contained in
\nu^{-1}(1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\subset H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Indeed, it suffices to check this for a single choice of
\hat{\gamma}
– for example the block matrix

(B.1.1)

\hat{\gamma}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
w_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{\operatorname{max}}&1\end{array}\right)

with block sizes
n\times n
, where
w_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{\operatorname{max}}
is the antidiagonal matrix with
1
s along the antidiagonal – and this is a simple computation. Furthermore, one can verify that
\hat{\gamma}^{-1}K_{H,\beta}\hat{\gamma}\backslash K_{G,\beta}/K_{G,\beta+1}
is a singleton and
\hat{\gamma}^{-1}K_{H,\beta}\hat{\gamma}\cap K_{G,\beta+1}=\hat{\gamma}^{-1}K_{H,\beta+1}\hat{\gamma}
(again, it suffices to check this for one example of
\hat{\gamma}
).

Lemma B.1.2
.

Let
\beta\geq 1
and
1\leq e\leq\beta
. Suppose that
\chi\colon\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is a smooth character which is trivial on
1+p^{e}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
\pi
be a smooth representation of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and let
\mathfrak{Z}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(\pi,\chi^{-1}\circ\nu)
. Let
\phi\in\pi^{K_{G,\beta}}
and let

\operatorname{tr}(\phi)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\sum_{k\in K_{G,e}/K_{G,\beta}}k\cdot\phi\;\in\;\pi^{K_{G,e}}.

Then
\mathfrak{Z}(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\operatorname{tr}(\phi))=p^{(\beta-e)(2n-1)n}\mathfrak{Z}(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\phi)
.

Proof.

By above, we have

\hat{\gamma}\cdot\operatorname{tr}(\phi)=\sum_{l\in K_{H,e}/K_{H,\beta}}l\cdot\hat{\gamma}\cdot\phi.

The result now follows from the
H
-equivariance of
\mathfrak{Z}
, the fact that
\chi^{-1}(\nu(l))=1
for any
l\in K_{H,e}
, and
[K_{H,e}:K_{H,\beta}]=[K_{G,e}:K_{G,\beta}]=p^{(\beta-e)(2n-1)n}
.
∎

B.2.
Dual eigenvectors

We continue with the notation in the previous section, but we now fix
\hat{\gamma}
to be the element in (
B.1.1
). Let
\phi\in\pi^{K_{G,1}}
and let
t_{p},s_{p}\in G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
denote the diagonal matrices

t_{p}=\operatorname{diag}(p^{2n-1},p^{2n-2},\dots,p,1),\quad\quad s_{p}=w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}t_{p}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}

where
w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\in G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denotes the antidiagonal matrix with
1
s along the antidiagonal. For any
\beta\geq 1
, set

\psi_{\beta}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}[K_{G,\beta}s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}K_{G,1}]\cdot\phi=\sum_{k\in K_{G,\beta}/(s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}K_{G,1}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}s_{p}^{-\beta}\cap K_{G,\beta})}ks_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\phi.

Let
\delta_{B}\colon B(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
denote the standard modulus character which satisfies:

\delta_{B}(b)=|t_{1}|^{2n-1}\cdot|t_{2}|^{2n-3}\cdots|t_{2n-1}|^{3-2n}\cdot|t_{2n}|^{1-2n},\quad b\in B(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),

where
\operatorname{diag}(t_{1},\dots,t_{2n})
denotes the projection of
b
to the diagonal torus, and
|\cdot|
is the
p
-adic absolute value normalised so that
|p|=p^{-1}
.

Lemma B.2.1
.

Let
\chi\colon\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times}\to\mathbb{C}^{\times}
be a smooth character which is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}
, and let
\mathfrak{Z}\in\operatorname{Hom}_{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}(\pi,\chi^{-1}\circ\nu)
. Then

\mathfrak{Z}(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\psi_{\beta})=\delta_{B}(t_{p})^{-\beta}p^{-n(2n-1)}[K_{G,1}:K_{G,\beta}]^{-1}\chi(-1)^{n}\cdot\mathfrak{Z}({{}^{t}\hat{\gamma}^{-1}}t_{p}^{\beta}\cdot\phi).

Proof.

An explicit calculation shows that

\hat{\gamma}^{-1}K_{H,\beta}\hat{\gamma}\backslash K_{G,\beta}/(s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}K_{G,1}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}s_{p}^{-\beta}\cap K_{G,\beta})

is a singleton, hence using the transformation properties of
\mathfrak{Z}
(and the fact that
\chi
is trivial on
1+p^{\beta}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Z}(\hat{\gamma}\cdot\psi_{\beta})

\displaystyle=[K_{G,\beta}:(s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}K_{G,1}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}s_{p}^{-\beta}\cap K_{G,\beta})]\mathfrak{Z}(\hat{\gamma}s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\phi)

\displaystyle=\delta_{B}(t_{p})^{-\beta}p^{-n(2n-1)}[K_{G,1}:K_{G,\beta}]^{-1}\mathfrak{Z}(\hat{\gamma}s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}\cdot\phi).

Now we have

\displaystyle\hat{\gamma}s_{p}^{\beta}w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
w_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{\operatorname{max}}&1\end{array}\right)w_{G}^{\operatorname{max}}t_{p}^{\beta}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-1&\\
&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&-w_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{\operatorname{max}}\\
&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&\\
w_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{\operatorname{max}}&1\end{array}\right)t_{p}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\in\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-1&\\
&1\end{array}\right){{}^{t}\hat{\gamma}^{-1}}t_{p}^{\beta}K_{G,1}.

The claim now follows because
\phi
is fixed by
K_{G,1}
.
∎
